```java
    if (!visited.add(start)) {
        return;
    }

    for (NodeId next : graph.getOrDefault(start, List.of())) {
        dfs(next, graph, visited);
    }
}
```

## BFS

```java
public Set<NodeId> reachableFrom(
    NodeId start,
    Map<NodeId, List<NodeId>> graph
) {
    Set<NodeId> visited = new HashSet<>();
    Queue<NodeId> queue = new ArrayDeque<>();

    visited.add(start);
    queue.offer(start);

    while (!queue.isEmpty()) {
        NodeId current = queue.poll();

        for (NodeId next : graph.getOrDefault(current, List.of())) {
            if (visited.add(next)) {
                queue.offer(next);
            }
        }
    }

    return visited;
}
```

---

# 26. Cycle Detection Pattern

## Problem

Detect cyclic dependency.

## Pattern

```java
public final class CycleDetector {
    private enum VisitState {
        VISITING,
        VISITED
    }

    public boolean hasCycle(Map<NodeId, List<NodeId>> graph) {
        Map<NodeId, VisitState> states = new HashMap<>();

        for (NodeId node : graph.keySet()) {
            if (hasCycleFrom(node, graph, states)) {
                return true;
            }
        }

        return false;
    }

    private boolean hasCycleFrom(
        NodeId node,
        Map<NodeId, List<NodeId>> graph,
        Map<NodeId, VisitState> states
    ) {
        VisitState state = states.get(node);

        if (state == VisitState.VISITING) {
            return true;
        }

        if (state == VisitState.VISITED) {
            return false;
        }

        states.put(node, VisitState.VISITING);

        for (NodeId next : graph.getOrDefault(node, List.of())) {
            if (hasCycleFrom(next, graph, states)) {
                return true;
            }
        }

        states.put(node, VisitState.VISITED);

        return false;
    }
}
```

## Use Case

```text
module dependency
workflow transition validation
approval hierarchy
catalog dependency
build dependency
```

---

# 27. Topological Sort Pattern

## Problem

Need process nodes after dependencies.

Example:

```text
migration steps
workflow dependency
build tasks
data import order
module initialization
```

## Pattern

```java
public List<NodeId> topologicalSort(Map<NodeId, List<NodeId>> graph) {
    Map<NodeId, Integer> indegree = new HashMap<>();

    for (NodeId node : graph.keySet()) {
        indegree.putIfAbsent(node, 0);

        for (NodeId next : graph.getOrDefault(node, List.of())) {
            indegree.merge(next, 1, Integer::sum);
        }
    }

    Queue<NodeId> ready = new ArrayDeque<>();

    for (Map.Entry<NodeId, Integer> entry : indegree.entrySet()) {
        if (entry.getValue() == 0) {
            ready.offer(entry.getKey());
        }
    }

    List<NodeId> result = new ArrayList<>();

    while (!ready.isEmpty()) {
        NodeId node = ready.poll();

        result.add(node);

        for (NodeId next : graph.getOrDefault(node, List.of())) {
            int newDegree = indegree.merge(next, -1, Integer::sum);

            if (newDegree == 0) {
                ready.offer(next);
            }
        }
    }

    if (result.size() != indegree.size()) {
        throw new IllegalStateException("Graph has cycle");
    }

    return result;
}
```

---

# 28. State Transition Table Pattern

## Problem

Workflow state transition harus eksplisit dan mudah dicek.

## Pattern

```java
public enum CaseStatus {
    DRAFT,
    SUBMITTED,
    UNDER_REVIEW,
    APPROVED,
    REJECTED,
    CANCELLED
}
```

```java
private static final Map<CaseStatus, Set<CaseStatus>> ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS =
    Map.of(
        CaseStatus.DRAFT, EnumSet.of(CaseStatus.SUBMITTED, CaseStatus.CANCELLED),
        CaseStatus.SUBMITTED, EnumSet.of(CaseStatus.UNDER_REVIEW, CaseStatus.CANCELLED),
        CaseStatus.UNDER_REVIEW, EnumSet.of(CaseStatus.APPROVED, CaseStatus.REJECTED),
        CaseStatus.APPROVED, EnumSet.noneOf(CaseStatus.class),
        CaseStatus.REJECTED, EnumSet.noneOf(CaseStatus.class),
        CaseStatus.CANCELLED, EnumSet.noneOf(CaseStatus.class)
    );
```

Check:

```java
public static boolean canTransition(CaseStatus from, CaseStatus to) {
    return ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS
        .getOrDefault(from, Set.of())
        .contains(to);
}
```

## Benefit

```text
transition visible
easy to test
easy to generate documentation
avoids scattered if/else
```

---

# 29. Dispatch Table Pattern

## Problem

Avoid huge `if/else` or `switch` for command handling.

## Pattern

```java
public enum CommandType {
    SUBMIT_ORDER,
    CANCEL_ORDER,
    APPROVE_ORDER
}
```

```java
Map<CommandType, Consumer<Command>> handlers = Map.of(
    CommandType.SUBMIT_ORDER, this::handleSubmit,
    CommandType.CANCEL_ORDER, this::handleCancel,
    CommandType.APPROVE_ORDER, this::handleApprove
);
```

Use:

```java
public void dispatch(Command command) {
    Consumer<Command> handler = handlers.get(command.type());

    if (handler == null) {
        throw new UnsupportedOperationException("Unsupported command: " + command.type());
    }

    handler.accept(command);
}
```

## Better with Typed Commands

```java
public sealed interface OrderCommand
    permits SubmitOrder, CancelOrder, ApproveOrder {
}

public record SubmitOrder(OrderId orderId) implements OrderCommand {}

public record CancelOrder(OrderId orderId, String reason) implements OrderCommand {}

public record ApproveOrder(OrderId orderId, UserId approverId) implements OrderCommand {}
```

Switch:

```java
public void handle(OrderCommand command) {
    switch (command) {
        case SubmitOrder submit -> handle(submit);
        case CancelOrder cancel -> handle(cancel);
        case ApproveOrder approve -> handle(approve);
    }
}
```

---

# 30. Sparse Matrix Pattern

## Problem

Two-dimensional relation where most cells are empty.

Example:

```text
role x permission
user x feature
product x region
tenant x config
```

## Pattern with Nested Map

```java
Map<RoleId, Map<PermissionId, Boolean>> matrix = new HashMap<>();
```

Set:

```java
matrix
    .computeIfAbsent(roleId, ignored -> new HashMap<>())
    .put(permissionId, true);
```

Get:

```java
boolean allowed = matrix
    .getOrDefault(roleId, Map.of())
    .getOrDefault(permissionId, false);
```

## Alternative Composite Key

```java
record RolePermissionKey(RoleId roleId, PermissionId permissionId) {}

Set<RolePermissionKey> allowedPermissions = new HashSet<>();
```

Check:

```java
boolean allowed = allowedPermissions.contains(
    new RolePermissionKey(roleId, permissionId)
);
```

## Decision

| Need | Use |
|---|---|
| Lookup by both dimensions | composite key set/map |
| Frequently get all permissions by role | nested map |
| Frequently get all roles by permission | bidirectional index |

---

# 31. Interval / Range Pattern

## Problem

Need represent validity period, SLA window, price period, effective date.

## Pattern

```java
public record DateRange(
    LocalDate startInclusive,
    LocalDate endExclusive
) {
    public DateRange {
        if (!startInclusive.isBefore(endExclusive)) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("Invalid date range");
        }
    }

    public boolean contains(LocalDate date) {
        return !date.isBefore(startInclusive)
            && date.isBefore(endExclusive);
    }

    public boolean overlaps(DateRange other) {
        return startInclusive.isBefore(other.endExclusive)
            && other.startInclusive.isBefore(endExclusive);
    }
}
```

## Use Case

```text
price validity
contract period
subscription period
license period
case SLA range
reporting period
```

## Rule

```text
Prefer [startInclusive, endExclusive)
Avoid inclusive end timestamp for date-time ranges.
```

---

# 32. Range Lookup with TreeMap

## Problem

Find value effective at date/time.

Example:

```text
pricing by effective date
configuration version by timestamp
tax rate by date
```

## Pattern

```java
TreeMap<LocalDate, Price> pricesByStartDate = new TreeMap<>();

pricesByStartDate.put(LocalDate.of(2026, 1, 1), priceA);
pricesByStartDate.put(LocalDate.of(2026, 7, 1), priceB);
```

Lookup effective price:

```java
public Optional<Price> priceAt(LocalDate date) {
    Map.Entry<LocalDate, Price> entry = pricesByStartDate.floorEntry(date);

    if (entry == null) {
        return Optional.empty();
    }

    return Optional.of(entry.getValue());
}
```

## Warning

This works when periods are continuous or when end date is implicit. If gaps/overlaps matter, use explicit `DateRange`.

---

# 33. Trie Pattern

## Problem

Need prefix search.

Use case:

```text
autocomplete
route matching
prefix command
postal code prefix
catalog search prefix
```

## Basic Trie

```java
public final class Trie {
    private final Node root = new Node();

    private static final class Node {
        private final Map<Character, Node> children = new HashMap<>();
        private boolean word;
    }

    public void insert(String text) {
        Node current = root;

        for (char c : text.toCharArray()) {
            current = current.children.computeIfAbsent(c, ignored -> new Node());
        }

        current.word = true;
    }

    public boolean contains(String text) {
        Node current = root;

        for (char c : text.toCharArray()) {
            current = current.children.get(c);

            if (current == null) {
                return false;
            }
        }

        return current.word;
    }

    public boolean startsWith(String prefix) {
        Node current = root;

        for (char c : prefix.toCharArray()) {
            current = current.children.get(c);

            if (current == null) {
                return false;
            }
        }

        return true;
    }
}
```

## Warning

This simple version uses `char`, not full Unicode code points. For serious multilingual search, use proper search/indexing tools.

---

# 34. Disjoint Set / Union-Find Pattern

## Problem

Need manage connected components.

Use case:

```text
group related entities
detect connectivity
merge duplicate identities
network grouping
cluster relation
```

## Pattern

```java
public final class UnionFind<T> {
    private final Map<T, T> parent = new HashMap<>();
    private final Map<T, Integer> rank = new HashMap<>();

    public void add(T value) {
        parent.putIfAbsent(value, value);
        rank.putIfAbsent(value, 0);
    }

    public T find(T value) {
        add(value);

        T p = parent.get(value);

        if (!p.equals(value)) {
            parent.put(value, find(p));
        }

        return parent.get(value);
    }

    public void union(T a, T b) {
        T rootA = find(a);
        T rootB = find(b);

        if (rootA.equals(rootB)) {
            return;
        }

        int rankA = rank.get(rootA);
        int rankB = rank.get(rootB);

        if (rankA < rankB) {
            parent.put(rootA, rootB);
        } else if (rankA > rankB) {
            parent.put(rootB, rootA);
        } else {
            parent.put(rootB, rootA);
            rank.put(rootA, rankA + 1);
        }
    }

    public boolean connected(T a, T b) {
        return find(a).equals(find(b));
    }
}
```

---

# 35. Bit Flag / EnumSet Pattern

## Problem

Need compact set of enum values.

## Prefer EnumSet

```java
EnumSet<Permission> permissions = EnumSet.of(
    Permission.READ,
    Permission.WRITE
);
```

Check:

```java
boolean canRead = permissions.contains(Permission.READ);
```

## Better Than Int Bitmask for Domain Code

Readable:

```java
EnumSet<Permission> permissions;
```

Less readable:

```java
int permissionMask;
```

## Use Bitmask When

```text
binary protocol
database compact column
interoperability with legacy system
extreme memory optimization
```

For most domain code, prefer `EnumSet`.

---

# 36. Tuple Avoidance Pattern

## Problem

Java has no standard tuple. Avoid vague `Pair<A,B>` in domain code.

Buruk:

```java
Map<Pair<CustomerId, ProductId>, Price> prices;
```

Baik:

```java
public record CustomerProductKey(
    CustomerId customerId,
    ProductId productId
) {}
```

Use:

```java
Map<CustomerProductKey, Price> prices;
```

## Rule

```text
If a tuple has business meaning, name it with a record.
```

---

# 37. DTO Projection Pattern

## Problem

Do not pass heavy entity when only a few fields are needed.

## Pattern

```java
public record OrderSummary(
    OrderId id,
    OrderStatus status,
    BigDecimal totalAmount,
    Instant createdAt
) {}
```

Projection:

```java
List<OrderSummary> summaries = orders.stream()
    .map(order -> new OrderSummary(
        order.id(),
        order.status(),
        order.totalAmount(),
        order.createdAt()
    ))
    .toList();
```

## Benefit

```text
clear API shape
less accidental mutation
less memory retained
easier serialization
better boundary modelling
```

---

# 38. Aggregate Root Internal Structure Pattern

## Problem

Domain aggregate needs internal collection but must protect invariants.

Buruk:

```java
public class Order {
    private List<OrderLine> lines;

    public List<OrderLine> getLines() {
        return lines;
    }
}
```

Caller can mutate directly.

## Better

```java
public final class Order {
    private final List<OrderLine> lines = new ArrayList<>();

    public List<OrderLine> lines() {
        return List.copyOf(lines);
    }

    public void addLine(ProductId productId, int quantity, BigDecimal unitPrice) {
        if (quantity <= 0) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("quantity must be positive");
        }

        lines.add(new OrderLine(productId, quantity, unitPrice));
    }
}
```

## Rule

```text
Aggregate owns its internal collection.
External code should not mutate aggregate internals directly.
```

---

# 39. Domain Collection Wrapper Pattern

## Problem

Raw `List<OrderLine>` hides domain rules.

## Pattern

```java
public final class OrderLines {
    private final List<OrderLine> lines;

    public OrderLines(List<OrderLine> lines) {
        if (lines == null || lines.isEmpty()) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("order must have at least one line");
        }

        this.lines = List.copyOf(lines);
    }

    public BigDecimal totalAmount() {
        return lines.stream()
            .map(OrderLine::amount)
            .reduce(BigDecimal.ZERO, BigDecimal::add);
    }

    public List<OrderLine> asList() {
        return lines;
    }
}
```

Use:

```java
public record Order(OrderId id, OrderLines lines) {}
```

## Benefit

```text
collection invariant is centralized
business behavior has home
less primitive obsession
```

---

# 40. Null Object / Empty Collection Pattern

## Problem

Returning `null` collection causes NPE.

Buruk:

```java
return null;
```

Baik:

```java
return List.of();
```

For map:

```java
return Map.of();
```

For set:

```java
return Set.of();
```

## Rule

```text
Return empty collection, not null.
```

---

# 41. Defensive Copy Pattern

## Problem

Mutable input can be changed after object creation.

Buruk:

```java
public record Order(List<OrderLine> lines) {}
```

Caller can mutate original list reference before/after.

## Better

```java
public record Order(List<OrderLine> lines) {
    public Order {
        lines = List.copyOf(lines);
    }
}
```

For mutable class:

```java
public final class Order {
    private final List<OrderLine> lines;

    public Order(List<OrderLine> lines) {
        this.lines = List.copyOf(lines);
    }

    public List<OrderLine> lines() {
        return lines;
    }
}
```

`List.copyOf()` returns an unmodifiable copy and rejects null elements.

---

# 42. Mutable Builder, Immutable Result Pattern

## Problem

Need convenient construction but immutable final object.

## Pattern

```java
public record OrderDraft(
    CustomerId customerId,
    List<OrderLine> lines
) {
    public OrderDraft {
        lines = List.copyOf(lines);
    }
}
```

Builder:

```java
public final class OrderDraftBuilder {
    private CustomerId customerId;
    private final List<OrderLine> lines = new ArrayList<>();

    public OrderDraftBuilder customerId(CustomerId customerId) {
        this.customerId = customerId;
        return this;
    }

    public OrderDraftBuilder addLine(OrderLine line) {
        this.lines.add(line);
        return this;
    }

    public OrderDraft build() {
        return new OrderDraft(customerId, lines);
    }
}
```

## Rule

```text
Mutation during construction is okay.
Published object should be immutable where possible.
```

---

# 43. Pagination Window Pattern

## Problem

Represent paged result cleanly.

## Pattern

```java
public record PageRequest(
    int page,
    int size
) {
    public PageRequest {
        if (page < 0) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("page must be >= 0");
        }

        if (size <= 0 || size > 500) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("invalid page size");
        }
    }

    public int offset() {
        return page * size;
    }
}
```

Result:

```java
public record Page<T>(
    List<T> items,
    int page,
    int size,
    long totalItems
) {
    public Page {
        items = List.copyOf(items);
    }
}
```

## Cursor Pattern

```java
public record CursorPage<T>(
    List<T> items,
    String nextCursor
) {
    public CursorPage {
        items = List.copyOf(items);
    }

    public boolean hasNext() {
        return nextCursor != null && !nextCursor.isBlank();
    }
}
```

## Decision

| Need | Use |
|---|---|
| Simple UI page number | offset pagination |
| Large changing dataset | cursor pagination |
| Stable audit/history list | keyset/cursor |
| Small admin table | offset okay |

---

# 44. Keyset Pagination Data Pattern

## Problem

Offset pagination gets slow and unstable for large tables.

## Pattern

```java
public record OrderCursor(
    Instant createdAt,
    OrderId orderId
) {}
```

Query logic concept:

```sql
WHERE (created_at, id) < (:createdAt, :orderId)
ORDER BY created_at DESC, id DESC
LIMIT :size
```

Java result:

```java
public record OrderPage(
    List<OrderSummary> items,
    OrderCursor nextCursor
) {
    public OrderPage {
        items = List.copyOf(items);
    }
}
```

## Rule

```text
Cursor should include stable tie-breaker.
Usually timestamp + ID.
```

---

# 45. Error Accumulation Pattern

## Problem

Need collect multiple validation errors.

## Pattern

```java
public record ValidationError(
    String field,
    String code,
    String message
) {}
```

```java
public final class ValidationErrors {
    private final List<ValidationError> errors = new ArrayList<>();

    public void add(String field, String code, String message) {
        errors.add(new ValidationError(field, code, message));
    }

    public boolean hasErrors() {
        return !errors.isEmpty();
    }

    public List<ValidationError> asList() {
        return List.copyOf(errors);
    }
}
```

Use:

```java
ValidationErrors errors = new ValidationErrors();

if (request.name() == null || request.name().isBlank()) {
    errors.add("name", "REQUIRED", "Name is required");
}

if (errors.hasErrors()) {
    throw new ValidationException(errors.asList());
}
```

---

# 46. Result Type Pattern

## Problem

Method can succeed or fail with typed outcome.

## Pattern

```java
public sealed interface Result<T>
    permits Success, Failure {
}

public record Success<T>(T value) implements Result<T> {}

public record Failure<T>(String code, String message) implements Result<T> {}
```

Use:

```java
Result<Order> result = orderService.submit(command);

switch (result) {
    case Success<Order> success -> handleSuccess(success.value());
    case Failure<Order> failure -> handleFailure(failure.code(), failure.message());
}
```

## Use Case

```text
domain operation result
validation result
external call result
workflow step result
```

---

# 47. Command Queue Data Pattern

## Problem

Need represent tasks cleanly.

## Pattern

```java
public sealed interface OrderCommand
    permits SubmitOrderCommand, CancelOrderCommand {
    OrderId orderId();
}

public record SubmitOrderCommand(OrderId orderId, UserId submittedBy)
    implements OrderCommand {}

public record CancelOrderCommand(OrderId orderId, String reason)
    implements OrderCommand {}
```

Queue:

```java
BlockingQueue<OrderCommand> queue = new LinkedBlockingQueue<>();
```

Handler:

```java
public void handle(OrderCommand command) {
    switch (command) {
        case SubmitOrderCommand submit -> submitOrder(submit);
        case CancelOrderCommand cancel -> cancelOrder(cancel);
    }
}
```

---

# 48. Event Store In-Memory Pattern

## Problem

Need group events by aggregate ID.

## Pattern

```java
Map<OrderId, List<OrderEvent>> eventsByOrder = new HashMap<>();

public void append(OrderId orderId, OrderEvent event) {
    eventsByOrder
        .computeIfAbsent(orderId, ignored -> new ArrayList<>())
        .add(event);
}

public List<OrderEvent> eventsOf(OrderId orderId) {
    return List.copyOf(eventsByOrder.getOrDefault(orderId, List.of()));
}
```

## Event Type

```java
public sealed interface OrderEvent
    permits OrderSubmitted, OrderCancelled {
    OrderId orderId();
    Instant occurredAt();
}

public record OrderSubmitted(
    OrderId orderId,
    Instant occurredAt
) implements OrderEvent {}

public record OrderCancelled(
    OrderId orderId,
    Instant occurredAt,
    String reason
) implements OrderEvent {}
```

---

# 49. Index + Source-of-Truth Pattern

## Problem

Need several access paths to same data.

Example:

```text
orders by ID
orders by customer
orders by status
```

## Pattern

```java
public final class OrderReadModel {
    private final Map<OrderId, Order> byId = new HashMap<>();
    private final Map<CustomerId, Set<OrderId>> byCustomer = new HashMap<>();
    private final Map<OrderStatus, Set<OrderId>> byStatus = new EnumMap<>(OrderStatus.class);

    public void add(Order order) {
        byId.put(order.id(), order);

        byCustomer
            .computeIfAbsent(order.customerId(), ignored -> new HashSet<>())
            .add(order.id());

        byStatus
            .computeIfAbsent(order.status(), ignored -> new HashSet<>())
            .add(order.id());
    }

    public Optional<Order> byId(OrderId id) {
        return Optional.ofNullable(byId.get(id));
    }

    public List<Order> byCustomer(CustomerId customerId) {
        return byCustomer.getOrDefault(customerId, Set.of())
            .stream()
            .map(byId::get)
            .filter(Objects::nonNull)
            .toList();
    }
}
```

## Invariant

```text
All indexes must be updated atomically relative to each other.
```

If concurrent, guard with lock or rebuild immutable snapshot.

---

# 50. Rebuild Index Pattern

## Problem

Incrementally maintaining multiple indexes is error-prone.

## Pattern

```java
public record OrderIndexes(
    Map<OrderId, Order> byId,
    Map<CustomerId, List<Order>> byCustomer,
    Map<OrderStatus, List<Order>> byStatus
) {
    public OrderIndexes {
        byId = Map.copyOf(byId);
        byCustomer = deepCopy(byCustomer);
        byStatus = deepCopy(byStatus);
    }

    private static <K, V> Map<K, List<V>> deepCopy(Map<K, List<V>> input) {
        return input.entrySet().stream()
            .collect(Collectors.toUnmodifiableMap(
                Map.Entry::getKey,
                entry -> List.copyOf(entry.getValue())
            ));
    }

    public static OrderIndexes rebuild(List<Order> orders) {
        Map<OrderId, Order> byId = orders.stream()
            .collect(Collectors.toMap(Order::id, Function.identity()));

        Map<CustomerId, List<Order>> byCustomer = orders.stream()
            .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(Order::customerId));

        Map<OrderStatus, List<Order>> byStatus = orders.stream()
            .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(Order::status));

        return new OrderIndexes(byId, byCustomer, byStatus);
    }
}
```

Holder:

```java
private volatile OrderIndexes indexes = OrderIndexes.rebuild(List.of());

public void refresh(List<Order> orders) {
    indexes = OrderIndexes.rebuild(orders);
}
```

## Benefit

```text
simpler correctness
immutable snapshot
no partial index update
great for reference/read-mostly data
```

---

# 51. Per-Tenant Data Partition Pattern

## Problem

Data must be isolated by tenant.

## Pattern

```java
Map<TenantId, Map<OrderId, Order>> ordersByTenant = new HashMap<>();
```

Put:

```java
ordersByTenant
    .computeIfAbsent(tenantId, ignored -> new HashMap<>())
    .put(order.id(), order);
```

Get:

```java
Order order = ordersByTenant
    .getOrDefault(tenantId, Map.of())
    .get(orderId);
```

## Better Encapsulation

```java
public final class TenantOrderStore {
    private final Map<TenantId, Map<OrderId, Order>> ordersByTenant = new HashMap<>();

    public void put(TenantId tenantId, Order order) {
        ordersByTenant
            .computeIfAbsent(tenantId, ignored -> new HashMap<>())
            .put(order.id(), order);
    }

    public Optional<Order> get(TenantId tenantId, OrderId orderId) {
        return Optional.ofNullable(
            ordersByTenant
                .getOrDefault(tenantId, Map.of())
                .get(orderId)
        );
    }
}
```

## Rule

```text
Do not let tenantId be an optional filter.
Make tenant partition explicit in the data structure.
```

---

# 52. Per-Key Queue Pattern

## Problem

Need queue tasks per aggregate/customer/order.

## Pattern

```java
Map<OrderId, Queue<OrderCommand>> queuesByOrder = new HashMap<>();

public void enqueue(OrderId orderId, OrderCommand command) {
    queuesByOrder
        .computeIfAbsent(orderId, ignored -> new ArrayDeque<>())
        .offer(command);
}
```

Use when:

```text
commands must be ordered per order
different orders can process independently
```

For concurrent/production design, combine with sharded executor or actor/mailbox pattern.

---

# 53. Sharded Structure Pattern

## Problem

Global map/queue/lock becomes bottleneck.

## Pattern

```java
public final class ShardedMap<K, V> {
    private final List<ConcurrentHashMap<K, V>> shards;

    public ShardedMap(int shardCount) {
        this.shards = IntStream.range(0, shardCount)
            .mapToObj(i -> new ConcurrentHashMap<K, V>())
            .toList();
    }

    public V get(K key) {
        return shard(key).get(key);
    }

    public void put(K key, V value) {
        shard(key).put(key, value);
    }

    private ConcurrentHashMap<K, V> shard(K key) {
        int index = Math.floorMod(key.hashCode(), shards.size());
        return shards.get(index);
    }
}
```

## Use Case

```text
high-concurrency cache
per-tenant partition
per-aggregate processing
reduce lock contention
```

---

# 54. Normalized vs Denormalized Structure Pattern

## Normalized

```java
Map<CustomerId, Customer> customersById;
Map<OrderId, Order> ordersById;
```

Pros:

```text
single source of truth
less duplication
easier update
```

Cons:

```text
needs joins/lookups
more complex read path
```

## Denormalized Read Model

```java
public record OrderView(
    OrderId orderId,
    CustomerId customerId,
    String customerName,
    BigDecimal totalAmount
) {}
```

Pros:

```text
fast read
simple API response
good for query side
```

Cons:

```text
duplication
staleness
update consistency concern
```

## Rule

```text
Use normalized structure for write model.
Use denormalized structure for read model when query performance/simplicity matters.
```

---

# 55. Structure Choice by Operation

| Dominant Operation | Recommended Structure |
|---|---|
| Append and iterate | `ArrayList` |
| Add/remove both ends | `ArrayDeque` |
| Lookup by key | `HashMap` |
| Membership check | `HashSet` |
| Preserve insertion order | `LinkedHashMap` / `LinkedHashSet` |
| Sorted traversal | `TreeMap` / `TreeSet` |
| Range lookup | `TreeMap` |
| Top priority | `PriorityQueue` |
| Grouping | `Map<K, List<V>>` |
| Unique grouping | `Map<K, Set<V>>` |
| Concurrent lookup/update | `ConcurrentHashMap` |
| Producer-consumer | `BlockingQueue` |
| Read-mostly list | `CopyOnWriteArrayList` |
| Enum set | `EnumSet` |
| Enum key map | `EnumMap` |
| Many flags | `BitSet` |
| Graph | `Map<Node, List<Node>>` |
| Tree | node + children list |
| Prefix lookup | Trie |
| Connected components | Union-Find |

---

# 56. Common Anti-Patterns

| Anti-Pattern | Problem |
|---|---|
| Repeated `List.stream().filter()` lookup | O(n) repeatedly |
| Raw `Map<String, Object>` | No type safety |
| Composite key as string concat | Collision/refactor risk |
| `List` for membership check | O(n), unclear intent |
| Exposing mutable internal list | Invariant leak |
| Returning null collection | NPE risk |
| `HashSet` with mutable key fields | Lookup corruption |
| `HashMap` shared across threads | Race/corruption |
| Multiple indexes updated separately without invariant | Inconsistent read model |
| `TreeSet` comparator inconsistent with equals | Missing/duplicate weirdness |
| `PriorityQueue` expected to be sorted list | Only head guaranteed |
| `EnumSet` ignored for enum flags | Less efficient/readable |
| Parent-child tree without cycle validation | Infinite recursion |
| Global lock for per-key operations | Unnecessary contention |
| Denormalized data treated as source of truth | Consistency bugs |

---

# 57. Review Checklist

```text
Data Access
[ ] What is the dominant access path?
[ ] Is repeated lookup using List scan?
[ ] Should this be Map or Set?
[ ] Is ordering actually required?
[ ] Is sorting one-time or maintained?

Correctness
[ ] Are keys strongly typed?
[ ] Are composite keys modelled as records?
[ ] Are equals/hashCode stable?
[ ] Are mutable objects used as HashMap keys?
[ ] Are duplicate keys handled?

Mutability
[ ] Are collections defensively copied?
[ ] Is internal collection exposed?
[ ] Should the result be immutable?
[ ] Is empty collection returned instead of null?

Relationship
[ ] Is this one-to-one, one-to-many, many-to-many?
[ ] Is a bidirectional index needed?
[ ] Are all indexes updated atomically?
[ ] Can indexes be rebuilt instead of incrementally maintained?

Hierarchy / Graph
[ ] Is the data actually a tree or graph?
[ ] Are cycles possible?
[ ] Is traversal DFS or BFS?
[ ] Is topological sort needed?

Concurrency
[ ] Is the structure shared across threads?
[ ] Should it be immutable snapshot?
[ ] Should it use ConcurrentHashMap?
[ ] Is CopyOnWrite suitable?
[ ] Is lock needed for multi-index invariant?

Enterprise
[ ] Is tenant partition explicit?
[ ] Is read model denormalized intentionally?
[ ] Is write model authoritative?
[ ] Is cache invalidation defined?
[ ] Is stale data acceptable?
```

---

# 58. Practical Rules

```text
1. Use List for order, not lookup.
2. Use Set for membership and deduplication.
3. Use Map for identity lookup.
4. Use record for keys, especially composite keys.
5. Use EnumSet and EnumMap for enum-based structures.
6. Use Map<K, List<V>> for one-to-many.
7. Use Map<K, Set<V>> when one-to-many must be unique.
8. Use LinkedHashMap/LinkedHashSet when stable iteration order matters.
9. Use TreeMap when range/floor/ceiling lookup matters.
10. Use PriorityQueue for repeated min/max extraction.
11. Use immutable snapshots for read-mostly reference data.
12. Use defensive copies at object boundaries.
13. Never expose mutable aggregate internals directly.
14. Use ConcurrentHashMap for concurrent independent key updates.
15. Use lock or rebuild snapshot for multi-index consistency.
16. Use graph patterns when relation is not strict hierarchy.
17. Validate cycles before recursive tree traversal.
18. Make tenant partition explicit in structure.
19. Prefer typed domain structures over Map<String,Object>.
20. Choose structure based on operation, not habit.
```

---

# 59. Mini Summary

```text
Sequence
-> List

Membership
-> Set / EnumSet

Lookup
-> Map / EnumMap / ConcurrentHashMap

Grouping
-> Map<K, List<V>>

Unique grouping
-> Map<K, Set<V>>

Composite lookup
-> record Key(...)

Ordering
-> LinkedHashMap / LinkedHashSet

Sorting / range
-> TreeMap / TreeSet

Priority
-> PriorityQueue

Hierarchy
-> Tree node / parent-child index

Graph
-> adjacency list

Cache
-> Map + eviction / immutable snapshot / in-flight dedup

Workflow state
-> transition table / graph

Concurrency
-> immutable snapshot, ConcurrentHashMap, CopyOnWrite, lock-protected indexes

Domain safety
-> typed IDs, domain collection wrapper, defensive copy
```

Kesimpulan penting: **struktur data yang baik membuat operasi utama menjadi murah dan invariant menjadi sulit dilanggar.** Di codebase enterprise, bug besar sering muncul bukan karena algoritma rumit, tetapi karena data disimpan dalam struktur yang salah: `List` dipakai untuk lookup, `String` dipakai sebagai composite key, mutable collection bocor keluar aggregate, atau banyak index tidak dijaga konsistensinya.

## Response:
7/12/2026, 8:47:00 PM

Saya akan susun dari level paling dasar sampai production concern: standard streams, byte/char IO, console interaktif, encoding, buffering, redirection, testing, dan anti-pattern yang sering muncul di CLI/backend.

Berikut **cheat sheet Java Standard & Console I/O** untuk Java 17+.

Fokusnya: `System.in`, `System.out`, `System.err`, `Console`, `Scanner`, `BufferedReader`, encoding, buffering, redirect, terminal behavior, dan pattern yang aman untuk CLI maupun backend tooling.

# Java Standard & Console I/O Cheat Sheet

---

# 1. Mental Model

```text
Standard I/O
├── stdin   -> input stream standar
├── stdout  -> output normal
└── stderr  -> output error/diagnostic

Java API
├── System.in       -> InputStream
├── System.out      -> PrintStream
├── System.err      -> PrintStream
├── Console         -> interactive terminal abstraction
├── Scanner         -> token-based input parser
├── BufferedReader  -> line-based character input
├── PrintWriter     -> formatted character output
└── Charset         -> byte <-> text encoding
```

Rule utama:

```text
stdin/stdout/stderr adalah stream proses.
Console adalah terminal interaktif.
Tidak semua environment punya Console.
```

---

# 2. Standard Streams

Java punya tiga stream global:

```java
System.in;
System.out;
System.err;
```

| Stream | Type | Fungsi |
|---|---|---|
| `System.in` | `InputStream` | input standar |
| `System.out` | `PrintStream` | output normal |
| `System.err` | `PrintStream` | error / diagnostic output |

Contoh:

```java
System.out.println("Hello");
System.err.println("Something went wrong");
```

Input mentah:

```java
int value = System.in.read();
```

`System.in.read()` membaca **1 byte**, bukan satu line.

---

# 3. stdout vs stderr

Gunakan `stdout` untuk output normal:

```java
System.out.println("Processing completed");
```

Gunakan `stderr` untuk error, warning, diagnostic:

```java
System.err.println("Invalid argument: file path is required");
```

Kenapa penting?

```bash
java App > output.txt 2> error.log
```

Artinya:

```text
stdout -> output.txt
stderr -> error.log
```

Rule:

| Output | Stream |
|---|---|
| Data normal | `System.out` |
| Result CLI | `System.out` |
| Error message | `System.err` |
| Diagnostic/debug manual | `System.err` |
| Log production app | logging framework, bukan `System.out` |

---

# 4. PrintStream Basics

`System.out` dan `System.err` adalah `PrintStream`.

```java
System.out.print("Hello ");
System.out.println("World");
System.out.printf("Name=%s, Age=%d%n", "Fajar", 29);
```

Method penting:

| Method | Fungsi |
|---|---|
| `print()` | print tanpa newline |
| `println()` | print dengan newline |
| `printf()` | formatted output |
| `format()` | sama seperti `printf()` |
| `flush()` | paksa buffer keluar |

Contoh:

```java
System.out.printf("Total: %.2f%n", 123.456);
```

Output:

```text
Total: 123.46
```

Gunakan `%n`, bukan `\n`, untuk newline portable:

```java
System.out.printf("Hello%n");
```

---

# 5. Common Format Specifiers

| Specifier | Meaning | Example |
|---|---|---|
| `%s` | string | `"abc"` |
| `%d` | decimal integer | `123` |
| `%f` | floating point | `12.34` |
| `%.2f` | floating 2 decimals | `12.35` |
| `%b` | boolean | `true` |
| `%c` | char | `'A'` |
| `%x` | hex | `ff` |
| `%n` | platform newline | newline |

Example:

```java
String name = "Order";
int count = 10;
BigDecimal total = new BigDecimal("123.45");

System.out.printf("%s count=%d total=%s%n", name, count, total);
```

---

# 6. Reading from stdin: Raw Byte

```java
int b = System.in.read();

if (b != -1) {
    System.out.println("Byte: " + b);
}
```

Important:

```text
System.in.read() returns int.
-1 means end of stream.
```

Read all bytes:

```java
byte[] bytes = System.in.readAllBytes();
```

Use case:

```text
piped binary data
small input
test utility
simple CLI
```

Warning:

```text
readAllBytes() can consume large memory if stdin is huge.
```

---

# 7. Byte vs Character Input

Low-level byte input:

```java
InputStream input = System.in;
```

Character input needs decoding:

```java
Reader reader = new InputStreamReader(System.in, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
```

Line input:

```java
BufferedReader reader = new BufferedReader(
    new InputStreamReader(System.in, StandardCharsets.UTF_8)
);
```

Mental model:

```text
byte[] -- Charset --> String / char
String / char -- Charset --> byte[]
```

Always specify charset for deterministic behavior.

---

# 8. BufferedReader Pattern

Best default untuk line-based CLI input.

```java
BufferedReader reader = new BufferedReader(
    new InputStreamReader(System.in, StandardCharsets.UTF_8)
);

System.out.print("Name: ");
System.out.flush();

String name = reader.readLine();

System.out.println("Hello, " + name);
```

`readLine()`:

| Return | Meaning |
|---|---|
| String | line input without newline |
| `null` | end of stream |

Example loop:

```java
String line;

while ((line = reader.readLine()) != null) {
    System.out.println("Input: " + line);
}
```

Cocok untuk:

```text
reading command lines
piped text input
interactive prompt
simple CLI tools
```

---

# 9. Scanner Pattern

`Scanner` cocok untuk token parsing.

```java
Scanner scanner = new Scanner(System.in, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);

System.out.print("Age: ");
int age = scanner.nextInt();

System.out.println("Age = " + age);
```

Read tokens:

```java
String token = scanner.next();
int number = scanner.nextInt();
long id = scanner.nextLong();
BigDecimal amount = scanner.nextBigDecimal();
```

Read full line:

```java
String line = scanner.nextLine();
```

---

# 10. Scanner Pitfall: `nextInt()` + `nextLine()`

Problem:

```java
Scanner scanner = new Scanner(System.in);

System.out.print("Age: ");
int age = scanner.nextInt();

System.out.print("Name: ");
String name = scanner.nextLine(); // often reads leftover newline
```

Fix:

```java
Scanner scanner = new Scanner(System.in, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);

System.out.print("Age: ");
int age = scanner.nextInt();
scanner.nextLine(); // consume leftover newline

System.out.print("Name: ");
String name = scanner.nextLine();
```

Better for robust CLI: read line first, then parse.

```java
BufferedReader reader = new BufferedReader(
    new InputStreamReader(System.in, StandardCharsets.UTF_8)
);

System.out.print("Age: ");
System.out.flush();

String input = reader.readLine();
int age = Integer.parseInt(input);
```

---

# 11. Scanner vs BufferedReader

| Aspect | Scanner | BufferedReader |
|---|---|---|
| Model | token parser | line reader |
| Parsing built-in | yes | manual |
| Performance | slower | faster |
| Control | medium | high |
| Good for | simple demos | robust CLI |
| Handles huge input | less ideal | better |
| Common pitfall | newline issue | manual parse needed |

Practical rule:

```text
Use Scanner for learning/demo/simple token input.
Use BufferedReader for robust CLI or line-based processing.
```

---

# 12. Console API

```java
Console console = System.console();
```

Important:

```java
if (console == null) {
    System.err.println("No interactive console available");
}
```

`System.console()` can return `null` in:

```text
IDE run window
some containers
some CI jobs
piped input/output
background process
application server
```

---

# 13. Console Read Line

```java
Console console = System.console();

if (console != null) {
    String username = console.readLine("Username: ");
    console.printf("Hello, %s%n", username);
}
```

Formatted prompt:

```java
String environment = console.readLine(
    "Target environment [%s/%s/%s]: ",
    "dev",
    "uat",
    "prod"
);
```

---

# 14. Console Password Input

Use `readPassword()` to avoid echoing sensitive input.

```java
Console console = System.console();

if (console == null) {
    throw new IllegalStateException("No console available");
}

char[] password = console.readPassword("Password: ");

try {
    authenticate(password);
} finally {
    Arrays.fill(password, '\0');
}
```

Why `char[]`, not `String`?

```text
String is immutable and may remain in memory.
char[] can be cleared after use.
```

Rule:

```text
Use Console.readPassword() for passwords/secrets.
Clear char[] after use.
Never log secrets.
```

---

# 15. Console with Fallback

Because `System.console()` can be null, provide fallback.

```java
public final class ConsoleInput {
    private final Console console;
    private final BufferedReader reader;

    public ConsoleInput() {
        this.console = System.console();
        this.reader = new BufferedReader(
            new InputStreamReader(System.in, StandardCharsets.UTF_8)
        );
    }

    public String readLine(String prompt) throws IOException {
        if (console != null) {
            return console.readLine(prompt);
        }

        System.out.print(prompt);
        System.out.flush();
        return reader.readLine();
    }

    public char[] readPassword(String prompt) throws IOException {
        if (console != null) {
            return console.readPassword(prompt);
        }

        System.out.print(prompt);
        System.out.flush();

        String value = reader.readLine();

        return value == null ? null : value.toCharArray();
    }
}
```

Warning:

```text
Fallback password input will echo characters.
Use only when acceptable, or fail if secure console is required.
```

---

# 16. Encoding / Charset

Always be explicit when converting bytes to text.

```java
String text = new String(bytes, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
```

```java
byte[] bytes = text.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
```

For stdin:

```java
Reader reader = new InputStreamReader(System.in, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
```

For stdout custom writer:

```java
Writer writer = new OutputStreamWriter(System.out, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
```

Important version note:

```text
Java 17: default charset may depend on environment.
Java 18+: default charset is UTF-8 by default.
```

Even so, explicit charset is still better for CLI tools and integration code.

---

# 17. PrintWriter Pattern

`PrintWriter` is useful for character output.

```java
PrintWriter writer = new PrintWriter(
    new OutputStreamWriter(System.out, StandardCharsets.UTF_8),
    true
);

writer.println("Hello");
writer.printf("Count=%d%n", 10);
```

With `autoFlush = true`:

```java
new PrintWriter(writer, true);
```

Use when you want writer-style abstraction.

---

# 18. Flushing

When prompting user, flush output before waiting input.

```java
System.out.print("Enter name: ");
System.out.flush();

String name = reader.readLine();
```

Why?

```text
Without flush, prompt may stay buffered and user sees nothing.
```

Useful for:

```text
interactive CLI
long-running output
progress display
manual prompt
```

---

# 19. Closing Standard Streams

Usually do **not** close `System.in`, `System.out`, or `System.err` in application code.

Avoid:

```java
System.out.close();
```

Avoid:

```java
try (Scanner scanner = new Scanner(System.in)) {
    // ...
}
```

Because closing `Scanner` also closes `System.in`.

Better:

```java
Scanner scanner = new Scanner(System.in, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
// use scanner, do not close if wrapping System.in
```

Rule:

```text
Do not close standard streams unless you own the whole process lifecycle and intentionally want to close them.
```

---

# 20. CLI Arguments

Standard input is not the same as command-line arguments.

```java
public static void main(String[] args) {
    for (String arg : args) {
        System.out.println(arg);
    }
}
```

Run:

```bash
java App dev users.csv
```

Then:

```text
args[0] = dev
args[1] = users.csv
```

Pattern:

```java
public static void main(String[] args) {
    if (args.length < 1) {
        System.err.println("Usage: java App <file>");
        System.exit(2);
    }

    String file = args[0];
}
```

---

# 21. Environment Variables

```java
String home = System.getenv("HOME");
String javaHome = System.getenv("JAVA_HOME");
```

All env:

```java
Map<String, String> env = System.getenv();
```

Use case:

```text
CLI configuration
container configuration
CI/CD variables
runtime environment
```

Do not print all env in production logs; env may contain secrets.

---

# 22. System Properties

```java
String userDir = System.getProperty("user.dir");
String osName = System.getProperty("os.name");
String lineSeparator = System.lineSeparator();
```

Common properties:

| Property | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `user.dir` | current working directory |
| `user.home` | user home |
| `java.version` | Java version |
| `os.name` | OS name |
| `file.separator` | `/` or `\` |
| `line.separator` | OS newline |

Set property:

```bash
java -Dapp.env=dev App
```

Read:

```java
String env = System.getProperty("app.env", "local");
```

---

# 23. Exit Codes

CLI apps should return meaningful exit codes.

```java
public static void main(String[] args) {
    try {
        run(args);
        System.exit(0);
    } catch (InvalidArgumentsException e) {
        System.err.println(e.getMessage());
        System.exit(2);
    } catch (Exception e) {
        e.printStackTrace(System.err);
        System.exit(1);
    }
}
```

Common convention:

| Code | Meaning |
|---:|---|
| `0` | success |
| `1` | generic failure |
| `2` | invalid usage/arguments |
| `126` | command found but not executable |
| `127` | command not found |
| `130` | terminated by Ctrl+C |

For normal Java service apps, avoid random `System.exit()` deep inside business code.

---

# 24. Reading Piped Input

Example:

```bash
cat input.txt | java App
```

Java:

```java
BufferedReader reader = new BufferedReader(
    new InputStreamReader(System.in, StandardCharsets.UTF_8)
);

String line;

while ((line = reader.readLine()) != null) {
    process(line);
}
```

Important:

```text
When input is piped, System.console() is often null.
Use System.in for piped data.
```

---

# 25. Writing Output for Piping

CLI output should be machine-readable when intended for pipelines.

```java
System.out.println(result);
```

Example:

```bash
java App | grep "SUCCESS"
```

Rule:

```text
stdout should contain data.
stderr should contain diagnostics.
```

Good:

```java
System.out.println(orderId);
System.err.println("Processed 100 records");
```

This allows:

```bash
java App > ids.txt
```

without diagnostic noise polluting `ids.txt`.

---

# 26. Interactive Prompt Pattern

```java
public final class Prompt {
    private final BufferedReader reader;
    private final PrintStream out;

    public Prompt(InputStream in, PrintStream out) {
        this.reader = new BufferedReader(
            new InputStreamReader(in, StandardCharsets.UTF_8)
        );
        this.out = out;
    }

    public String ask(String message) throws IOException {
        out.print(message);
        out.flush();

        return reader.readLine();
    }
}
```

Use:

```java
Prompt prompt = new Prompt(System.in, System.out);

String name = prompt.ask("Name: ");
System.out.println("Hello, " + name);
```

Benefit:

```text
testable
does not hardcode System.in/out everywhere
centralized prompt behavior
```

---

# 27. Yes/No Prompt Pattern

```java
public boolean askYesNo(String message) throws IOException {
    while (true) {
        System.out.print(message + " [y/n]: ");
        System.out.flush();

        String input = reader.readLine();

        if (input == null) {
            return false;
        }

        String normalized = input.trim().toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT);

        switch (normalized) {
            case "y", "yes" -> {
                return true;
            }
            case "n", "no" -> {
                return false;
            }
            default -> System.err.println("Please answer y or n.");
        }
    }
}
```

---

# 28. Numeric Input Pattern

Prefer read line then parse.

```java
public int askInt(String message) throws IOException {
    while (true) {
        System.out.print(message);
        System.out.flush();

        String input = reader.readLine();

        if (input == null) {
            throw new EOFException("No input");
        }

        try {
            return Integer.parseInt(input.trim());
        } catch (NumberFormatException e) {
            System.err.println("Please enter a valid integer.");
        }
    }
}
```

For money:

```java
public BigDecimal askAmount(String message) throws IOException {
    while (true) {
        System.out.print(message);
        System.out.flush();

        String input = reader.readLine();

        try {
            return new BigDecimal(input.trim());
        } catch (NumberFormatException e) {
            System.err.println("Please enter a valid decimal amount.");
        }
    }
}
```

---

# 29. Menu Pattern

```java
public void runMenu() throws IOException {
    while (true) {
        System.out.println();
        System.out.println("1. Create order");
        System.out.println("2. Cancel order");
        System.out.println("3. Exit");
        System.out.print("Choose: ");
        System.out.flush();

        String choice = reader.readLine();

        switch (choice) {
            case "1" -> createOrder();
            case "2" -> cancelOrder();
            case "3" -> {
                return;
            }
            default -> System.err.println("Invalid choice");
        }
    }
}
```

Rule:

```text
Keep menu handling separate from business logic.
CLI layer should call application service/use case.
```

---

# 30. Command Loop / REPL Pattern

```java
public void runCommandLoop() throws IOException {
    while (true) {
        System.out.print("> ");
        System.out.flush();

        String line = reader.readLine();

        if (line == null) {
            break;
        }

        String command = line.trim();

        if (command.equals("exit") || command.equals("quit")) {
            break;
        }

        handleCommand(command);
    }
}
```

Command parser:

```java
private void handleCommand(String command) {
    String[] parts = command.split("\\s+");

    switch (parts[0]) {
        case "help" -> printHelp();
        case "status" -> printStatus();
        default -> System.err.println("Unknown command: " + parts[0]);
    }
}
```

---

# 31. Reading Multi-Line Input

Sentinel-based:

```java
System.out.println("Enter text. Type END on its own line to finish.");

List<String> lines = new ArrayList<>();

String line;

while ((line = reader.readLine()) != null) {
    if (line.equals("END")) {
        break;
    }

    lines.add(line);
}

String text = String.join(System.lineSeparator(), lines);
```

Use case:

```text
manual message input
SQL snippet
template input
configuration input
```

---

# 32. Terminal Progress Output

Simple progress:

```java
for (int i = 0; i <= 100; i++) {
    System.out.printf("\rProgress: %d%%", i);
    System.out.flush();

    Thread.sleep(50);
}

System.out.println();
```

Important:

```text
\r returns cursor to beginning of current line.
Works in terminals, may look odd in logs/CI.
```

For backend services, prefer metrics/logging over console progress.

---

# 33. ANSI Colors

Basic ANSI:

```java
String red = "\u001B[31m";
String reset = "\u001B[0m";

System.out.println(red + "ERROR" + reset);
```

Common codes:

| Code | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `\u001B[31m` | red |
| `\u001B[32m` | green |
| `\u001B[33m` | yellow |
| `\u001B[34m` | blue |
| `\u001B[0m` | reset |

Warning:

```text
Not all output destinations support ANSI.
Disable colors when output is redirected or in CI if needed.
```

Java standard library has limited terminal capability detection. For rich CLI, libraries like Picocli/JLine are commonly used, but they are outside Java SE standard API.

---

# 34. Console Availability Pattern

```java
public static boolean isInteractive() {
    return System.console() != null;
}
```

Use:

```java
if (isInteractive()) {
    runInteractiveMode();
} else {
    runBatchMode();
}
```

But be careful:

```text
System.console() == null does not always mean no stdin.
It often means no attached terminal.
Piped input can still exist.
```

---

# 35. Standard I/O in Containers

In Docker/Kubernetes:

```text
stdout/stderr are captured by container runtime.
Logs are usually collected from stdout/stderr.
```

For simple apps:

```java
System.out.println("Application started");
System.err.println("Application failed");
```

For real services:

```text
Use logging framework.
Logs go to stdout/stderr via appender.
Do not write random System.out from business code.
```

In Kubernetes:

```bash
kubectl logs pod-name
```

usually reads container stdout/stderr.

---

# 36. System.out vs Logging Framework

Use `System.out` for:

```text
small CLI tools
demo programs
script output
machine-readable command output
interactive prompts
```

Use logging framework for:

```text
backend services
JAX-RS apps
workers
Camunda delegates
batch jobs
production diagnostics
structured logs
```

Bad in backend:

```java
System.out.println("Order submitted: " + orderId);
```

Better:

```java
log.info("Order submitted: orderId={}", orderId);
```

Reason:

```text
log levels
timestamps
thread names
correlation id
structured fields
routing
rotation
sampling
centralized logging
```

---

# 37. Testing Console I/O

Avoid hardcoding `System.in/out` deep inside logic.

Bad:

```java
public void run() {
    Scanner scanner = new Scanner(System.in);
    System.out.println(scanner.nextLine());
}
```

Better inject streams:

```java
public final class CliApp {
    private final BufferedReader in;
    private final PrintWriter out;
    private final PrintWriter err;

    public CliApp(InputStream in, OutputStream out, OutputStream err) {
        this.in = new BufferedReader(
            new InputStreamReader(in, StandardCharsets.UTF_8)
        );
        this.out = new PrintWriter(
            new OutputStreamWriter(out, StandardCharsets.UTF_8),
            true
        );
        this.err = new PrintWriter(
            new OutputStreamWriter(err, StandardCharsets.UTF_8),
            true
        );
    }

    public void run() throws IOException {
        out.print("Name: ");
        out.flush();

        String name = in.readLine();

        out.println("Hello, " + name);
    }
}
```

Test:

```java
@Test
void shouldGreetUser() throws Exception {
    byte[] input = "Fajar\n".getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);

    ByteArrayInputStream in = new ByteArrayInputStream(input);
    ByteArrayOutputStream out = new ByteArrayOutputStream();
    ByteArrayOutputStream err = new ByteArrayOutputStream();

    CliApp app = new CliApp(in, out, err);

    app.run();

    String output = out.toString(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);

    assertTrue(output.contains("Hello, Fajar"));
}
```

---

# 38. Capturing System.out in Tests

Sometimes useful, but less clean than dependency injection.

```java
PrintStream originalOut = System.out;

ByteArrayOutputStream buffer = new ByteArrayOutputStream();

try {
    System.setOut(new PrintStream(buffer, true, StandardCharsets.UTF_8));

    System.out.println("Hello");

    String output = buffer.toString(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);

    assertEquals("Hello" + System.lineSeparator(), output);
} finally {
    System.setOut(originalOut);
}
```

Warning:

```text
System.setOut changes global process state.
Tests can interfere with each other.
Avoid in parallel tests unless isolated.
```

---

# 39. Replacing Standard Streams

Java allows:

```java
System.setOut(PrintStream out);
System.setErr(PrintStream err);
System.setIn(InputStream in);
```

Example:

```java
System.setErr(new PrintStream(
    new FileOutputStream("error.log"),
    true,
    StandardCharsets.UTF_8
));
```

Use sparingly.

Risks:

```text
global mutable state
test interference
framework/logging confusion
hard-to-debug behavior
```

---

# 40. Process I/O

When running another process:

```java
Process process = new ProcessBuilder("java", "-version")
    .redirectErrorStream(true)
    .start();

try (BufferedReader reader = new BufferedReader(
    new InputStreamReader(process.getInputStream(), StandardCharsets.UTF_8)
)) {
    String line;

    while ((line = reader.readLine()) != null) {
        System.out.println(line);
    }
}

int exitCode = process.waitFor();
```

Important:

```text
Always consume process stdout/stderr.
If buffers fill, child process can block.
```

Separate stdout/stderr:

```java
Process process = new ProcessBuilder("cmd")
    .start();

InputStream stdout = process.getInputStream();
InputStream stderr = process.getErrorStream();
```

For robust process handling, consume both streams concurrently or redirect.

---

# 41. Reading Until EOF Pattern

```java
public static List<String> readAllLinesFromStdin() throws IOException {
    BufferedReader reader = new BufferedReader(
        new InputStreamReader(System.in, StandardCharsets.UTF_8)
    );

    List<String> lines = new ArrayList<>();

    String line;

    while ((line = reader.readLine()) != null) {
        lines.add(line);
    }

    return lines;
}
```

For large input, process streaming instead of collecting all:

```java
String line;

while ((line = reader.readLine()) != null) {
    process(line);
}
```

---

# 42. Handling Ctrl+C

When user presses Ctrl+C, JVM usually receives interrupt/termination signal. For graceful cleanup, use shutdown hook.

```java
Runtime.getRuntime().addShutdownHook(new Thread(() -> {
    System.err.println("Shutting down...");
    cleanup();
}));
```

Example:

```java
private static void cleanup() {
    // close resources
    // flush output
    // stop workers
}
```

Warning:

```text
Shutdown hooks should be short.
Do not rely on them for complex business transactions.
```

---

# 43. Standard I/O and Threads

`System.out.println()` is synchronized internally enough to avoid some corruption, but multi-threaded output can still interleave at logical level.

Example:

```java
executor.submit(() -> System.out.println("A"));
executor.submit(() -> System.out.println("B"));
```

Order is not guaranteed.

For structured multi-thread logs, use logging framework.

For CLI progress from multiple threads, centralize output:

```text
worker threads -> queue events -> one printer thread
```

---

# 44. Prompt + Validation Utility

```java
public final class CliPrompt {
    private final BufferedReader reader;
    private final PrintStream out;
    private final PrintStream err;

    public CliPrompt(InputStream in, PrintStream out, PrintStream err) {
        this.reader = new BufferedReader(
            new InputStreamReader(in, StandardCharsets.UTF_8)
        );
        this.out = out;
        this.err = err;
    }

    public String requiredString(String prompt) throws IOException {
        while (true) {
            out.print(prompt);
            out.flush();

            String value = reader.readLine();

            if (value == null) {
                throw new EOFException("End of input");
            }

            value = value.trim();

            if (!value.isBlank()) {
                return value;
            }

            err.println("Value is required.");
        }
    }

    public int requiredInt(String prompt) throws IOException {
        while (true) {
            String value = requiredString(prompt);

            try {
                return Integer.parseInt(value);
            } catch (NumberFormatException e) {
                err.println("Value must be an integer.");
            }
        }
    }
}
```

Use:

```java
CliPrompt prompt = new CliPrompt(System.in, System.out, System.err);

String name = prompt.requiredString("Name: ");
int age = prompt.requiredInt("Age: ");
```

---

# 45. CLI App Skeleton

```java
public final class Main {
    public static void main(String[] args) {
        int exitCode = new Main().run(args);
        System.exit(exitCode);
    }

    public int run(String[] args) {
        try {
            if (args.length == 0) {
                printUsage();
                return 2;
            }

            String command = args[0];

            return switch (command) {
                case "run" -> runCommand(args);
                case "help" -> {
                    printUsage();
                    yield 0;
                }
                default -> {
                    System.err.println("Unknown command: " + command);
                    printUsage();
                    yield 2;
                }
            };
        } catch (Exception e) {
            System.err.println("Fatal error: " + e.getMessage());
            return 1;
        }
    }

    private int runCommand(String[] args) {
        System.out.println("Running...");
        return 0;
    }

    private void printUsage() {
        System.err.println("""
            Usage:
              java Main run
              java Main help
            """);
    }
}
```

Rule:

```text
main should be thin.
Return exit code from run().
Keep business logic outside CLI parsing.
```

---

# 46. Machine-Readable Output Pattern

For CLI used by scripts, output should be predictable.

Example plain output:

```java
System.out.println(orderId);
```

Example JSON output manually:

```java
System.out.printf(
    "{\"orderId\":\"%s\",\"status\":\"%s\"}%n",
    escapeJson(orderId.toString()),
    escapeJson(status.name())
);
```

In real apps, use JSON library. But principle remains:

```text
stdout = data contract
stderr = diagnostics
exit code = success/failure
```

---

# 47. Human vs Machine Output

| Mode | Output Style |
|---|---|
| Human interactive | prompts, colors, progress |
| Machine/script | stable plain text / JSON / CSV |
| Diagnostic | stderr |
| Logs | logging framework |

Do not mix human prompt into machine-readable stdout.

Bad:

```java
System.out.println("Please wait...");
System.out.println(orderId);
```

Better:

```java
System.err.println("Please wait...");
System.out.println(orderId);
```

---

# 48. CSV-Like Console Output

Simple CSV:

```java
System.out.println("id,status,total");

for (Order order : orders) {
    System.out.printf(
        "%s,%s,%s%n",
        order.id(),
        order.status(),
        order.totalAmount()
    );
}
```

Warning:

```text
Real CSV needs escaping for comma, quote, newline.
Use a CSV library for serious cases.
```

---

# 49. Handling Large Output

Bad:

```java
StringBuilder builder = new StringBuilder();

for (Record record : records) {
    builder.append(record).append('\n');
}

System.out.print(builder);
```

Better stream output:

```java
for (Record record : records) {
    System.out.println(record);
}
```

For better abstraction:

```java
PrintWriter writer = new PrintWriter(
    new OutputStreamWriter(System.out, StandardCharsets.UTF_8),
    false
);

for (Record record : records) {
    writer.println(record);
}

writer.flush();
```

---

# 50. Handling Large Input

Bad:

```java
List<String> lines = reader.lines().toList();
```

This loads all lines into memory.

Better:

```java
String line;

while ((line = reader.readLine()) != null) {
    process(line);
}
```

Or:

```java
try (Stream<String> lines = reader.lines()) {
    lines.forEach(this::process);
}
```

For `System.in`, be careful with closing the reader if the process still needs stdin later.

---

# 51. `InputStream.available()` Pitfall

Do not use `available()` to determine total input size.

Bad:

```java
int size = System.in.available();
byte[] data = System.in.readNBytes(size);
```

Problem:

```text
available() means bytes that can be read without blocking now.
It does not mean total stream size.
```

Better:

```java
byte[] data = System.in.readAllBytes();
```

Or process streaming.

---

# 52. Standard Stream Redirection

Shell examples:

```bash
java App < input.txt
```

stdin from file.

```bash
java App > output.txt
```

stdout to file.

```bash
java App 2> error.log
```

stderr to file.

```bash
java App > output.txt 2>&1
```

stdout and stderr to same file.

```bash
cat input.txt | java App | sort
```

pipeline.

Design implication:

```text
CLI apps should not assume interactive terminal.
They may run with redirected stdin/stdout/stderr.
```

---

# 53. Platform Newline

Use:

```java
String newline = System.lineSeparator();
```

For formatted output:

```java
System.out.printf("Hello%n");
```

Avoid hardcoding unless protocol requires specific newline.

Important:

| Context | Newline |
|---|---|
| Human console | `System.lineSeparator()` / `%n` |
| HTTP protocol | `\r\n` where specified |
| Unix script output | often `\n` acceptable |
| Cross-platform text | `%n` |

---

# 54. Encoding Problems You Will See

| Symptom | Likely Cause |
|---|---|
| `Ã©` instead of `é` | UTF-8 decoded as ISO-8859-1 |
| `�` replacement char | invalid byte sequence |
| Indonesian/Japanese chars broken | wrong console encoding |
| Works locally, broken in CI | default charset differs |
| JSON payload broken | byte/text conversion wrong |

Defensive pattern:

```java
new InputStreamReader(System.in, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
new OutputStreamWriter(System.out, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
```

Also ensure terminal/environment uses UTF-8.

---

# 55. Secure Input Rules

```text
Never echo secrets.
Never log secrets.
Prefer Console.readPassword().
Clear char[] after use.
Do not store secrets in String when avoidable.
Do not print env/system properties blindly.
```

Example:

```java
char[] password = console.readPassword("Password: ");

try {
    login(password);
} finally {
    Arrays.fill(password, '\0');
}
```

---

# 56. Error Handling Pattern

Bad:

```java
catch (Exception e) {
    System.out.println("Error");
}
```

Better CLI:

```java
catch (InvalidArgumentsException e) {
    System.err.println("Invalid usage: " + e.getMessage());
    return 2;
} catch (IOException e) {
    System.err.println("I/O error: " + e.getMessage());
    return 1;
} catch (Exception e) {
    System.err.println("Unexpected error: " + e.getMessage());
    return 1;
}
```

For debug mode:

```java
if (debug) {
    e.printStackTrace(System.err);
}
```

Avoid printing stack trace for normal user-facing CLI unless debug/verbose mode is enabled.

---

# 57. Standard I/O in Enterprise Apps

In JAX-RS / Jakarta / Spring / Camunda services:

```text
Avoid System.out in business logic.
Use logging framework.
Use request correlation ID.
Use structured logs.
```

Bad:

```java
System.out.println("Processing case " + caseId);
```

Better:

```java
log.info("Processing case: caseId={}", caseId);
```

For command-line maintenance utilities, `System.out/err` is fine.

---

# 58. Pattern: Separate CLI Adapter from Use Case

Bad:

```java
public void submitOrder() {
    Scanner scanner = new Scanner(System.in);
    String id = scanner.nextLine();

    // business logic here
}
```

Better:

```java
public record SubmitOrderCommand(OrderId orderId) {}
```

Use case:

```java
public final class SubmitOrderUseCase {
    public void execute(SubmitOrderCommand command) {
        // business logic
    }
}
```

CLI adapter:

```java
public final class SubmitOrderCli {
    private final CliPrompt prompt;
    private final SubmitOrderUseCase useCase;

    public void run() throws IOException {
        String rawOrderId = prompt.requiredString("Order ID: ");

        useCase.execute(new SubmitOrderCommand(
            new OrderId(UUID.fromString(rawOrderId))
        ));

        System.out.println("Order submitted");
    }
}
```

Benefit:

```text
business logic testable
CLI can change without changing domain
same use case usable from REST, CLI, batch, Camunda
```

---

# 59. Common Anti-Patterns

| Anti-pattern | Problem |
|---|---|
| `System.out` in backend business logic | poor observability |
| Logging to stdout manually | no levels/context |
| Error messages to stdout | breaks scripting |
| Data output to stderr | breaks pipelines |
| Assuming `System.console()` exists | fails in IDE/CI/container |
| Closing `System.in` accidentally | later input fails |
| `Scanner.nextInt()` then `nextLine()` | newline bug |
| Not flushing prompt | prompt may not appear |
| Default charset | environment-dependent |
| `InputStream.available()` as file size | incorrect |
| Reading all stdin into memory | memory risk |
| Echoing password fallback | security risk |
| Printing stack trace always | poor UX/security |
| Using `System.exit()` deep in library code | kills host app |
| Mixing human logs with machine stdout | breaks automation |
| No exit code discipline | scripts cannot reason |

---

# 60. Decision Matrix

| Need | Use |
|---|---|
| Print normal CLI output | `System.out` |
| Print errors/diagnostics | `System.err` |
| Read line-based input | `BufferedReader` |
| Parse simple tokens | `Scanner` |
| Read password securely | `Console.readPassword()` |
| Write formatted output | `printf`, `PrintWriter` |
| Read piped input | `System.in` + `BufferedReader` |
| Machine-readable output | stdout only |
| Diagnostic output | stderr only |
| Production service logs | logging framework |
| Testable CLI | inject `InputStream` / `OutputStream` |
| Encoding-safe text | explicit `StandardCharsets.UTF_8` |
| Large input | streaming loop |
| Large output | streaming writer |
| Interactive prompt | print + flush + readLine |
| Exit status | return code / `System.exit()` in `main` only |

---

# 61. Review Checklist

```text
Standard Stream
[ ] stdout only contains normal data/result?
[ ] stderr contains errors/diagnostics?
[ ] prompts are flushed?
[ ] standard streams are not accidentally closed?

Input
[ ] input parsing handles EOF?
[ ] invalid input handled cleanly?
[ ] large input processed streaming?
[ ] Scanner newline issue avoided?

Console
[ ] System.console() null is handled?
[ ] password uses readPassword when possible?
[ ] secrets are not logged?
[ ] char[] password cleared?

Encoding
[ ] charset is explicit?
[ ] UTF-8 assumed intentionally?
[ ] byte/text conversion is clear?

CLI Behavior
[ ] usage message exists?
[ ] exit codes are meaningful?
[ ] machine output is stable?
[ ] human diagnostics do not pollute stdout?

Testing
[ ] CLI logic can be tested with fake streams?
[ ] System.setOut/System.setIn avoided unless necessary?
[ ] global stream changes restored in finally?

Enterprise
[ ] backend code uses logger, not System.out?
[ ] business logic separated from CLI adapter?
[ ] no System.exit deep inside library/use case?
```

---

# 62. Compact Summary

```text
System.in
- raw standard input
- InputStream
- read bytes
- wrap with InputStreamReader + BufferedReader for text

System.out
- normal output
- PrintStream
- use for CLI result/data

System.err
- diagnostic/error output
- PrintStream
- use for errors, warnings, progress, debug

Console
- interactive terminal abstraction
- may be null
- best for password input

Scanner
- simple token parser
- convenient but has nextInt/nextLine pitfall

BufferedReader
- best default for robust line input
- fast and predictable

PrintWriter
- useful formatted character output
- can wrap stdout with charset

Charset
- always specify UTF-8 for deterministic byte/text conversion

Flush
- needed before interactive reads

Exit Code
- 0 success
- non-zero failure
- main owns System.exit

Production Rule
- CLI uses stdout/stderr
- services use logging framework
```

Kesimpulan praktis: **standard I/O adalah kontrak proses.** Untuk CLI yang baik, pisahkan `stdout` sebagai data, `stderr` sebagai diagnostic, gunakan charset eksplisit, jangan mengasumsikan console selalu tersedia, dan jangan mencampur business logic dengan input/output terminal.
