Berikut **cheat sheet Java I/O File & Filesystem** untuk **Java 17+**, mencakup **classic IO**, **NIO**, dan **NIO.2**.

# Java I/O File & Filesystem Cheat Sheet  
## IO, NIO, NIO.2

---

# 1. Mental Model

```text
Java File I/O
├── java.io
│   ├── File
│   ├── InputStream / OutputStream
│   ├── Reader / Writer
│   └── BufferedInputStream / BufferedReader / PrintWriter
│
├── java.nio
│   ├── Buffer
│   ├── ByteBuffer
│   ├── Channel
│   ├── FileChannel
│   └── MappedByteBuffer
│
└── java.nio.file
    ├── Path
    ├── Paths
    ├── Files
    ├── FileSystem
    ├── WatchService
    ├── FileVisitor
    └── FileAttributeView
```

Simplified:

| API | Fokus |
|---|---|
| `java.io` | Stream-based classic I/O |
| `java.nio` | Buffer + channel-based I/O |
| `java.nio.file` / NIO.2 | Modern filesystem API |
| `File` | Legacy file abstraction |
| `Path` | Modern path abstraction |
| `Files` | Utility utama untuk file operation |

Rule modern:

```text
Prefer Path + Files for filesystem work.
Use InputStream/OutputStream for byte streams.
Use Reader/Writer for text streams.
Use Channels/Buffers for high-performance or low-level I/O.
```

---

# 2. Core Decision Matrix

| Need | Use |
|---|---|
| Read small text file | `Files.readString(path)` |
| Write small text file | `Files.writeString(path, text)` |
| Read large text file line-by-line | `Files.newBufferedReader(path)` |
| Stream lines | `Files.lines(path)` |
| Read binary file small | `Files.readAllBytes(path)` |
| Copy file | `Files.copy(source, target)` |
| Move/rename file | `Files.move(source, target)` |
| Delete file | `Files.delete(path)` |
| Create directories | `Files.createDirectories(path)` |
| Walk directory recursively | `Files.walkFileTree(...)` |
| Monitor directory changes | `WatchService` |
| High-performance file I/O | `FileChannel` |
| Memory-mapped file | `MappedByteBuffer` |
| Temporary file | `Files.createTempFile()` |
| File metadata | `Files.readAttributes()` |
| Legacy interop | `File.toPath()` / `Path.toFile()` |

---

# 3. `File` vs `Path`

## 3.1 Legacy `java.io.File`

```java
File file = new File("data/input.txt");

boolean exists = file.exists();
boolean isFile = file.isFile();
boolean isDirectory = file.isDirectory();

String absolutePath = file.getAbsolutePath();
```

Problem dengan `File`:

```text
legacy API
error handling often boolean-based
less expressive
less metadata support
less filesystem abstraction
```

---

## 3.2 Modern `java.nio.file.Path`

```java
Path path = Path.of("data", "input.txt");
```

Atau legacy style:

```java
Path path = Paths.get("data", "input.txt");
```

Prefer:

```java
Path path = Path.of("data", "input.txt");
```

Useful methods:

```java
Path fileName = path.getFileName();
Path parent = path.getParent();
Path root = path.getRoot();

boolean absolute = path.isAbsolute();

Path absolutePath = path.toAbsolutePath();
Path normalized = path.normalize();
```

---

## 3.3 Convert File and Path

```java
File file = new File("data/input.txt");

Path path = file.toPath();
```

```java
Path path = Path.of("data/input.txt");

File file = path.toFile();
```

Rule:

```text
Use File only when required by old APIs.
Convert to Path as soon as possible.
```

---

# 4. Path Basics

## 4.1 Build Path

```java
Path path = Path.of("logs", "app.log");
```

Cross-platform safe:

```java
Path path = Path.of("data", "orders", "2026", "orders.csv");
```

Avoid:

```java
Path path = Path.of("data/orders/2026/orders.csv");
```

It works, but manual separator can be risky across OS.

---

## 4.2 Resolve Path

```java
Path baseDir = Path.of("/app/data");

Path file = baseDir.resolve("input.csv");
```

Result:

```text
/app/data/input.csv
```

Nested:

```java
Path file = baseDir
    .resolve("orders")
    .resolve("2026")
    .resolve("orders.csv");
```

---

## 4.3 Normalize Path

```java
Path path = Path.of("/app/data/../logs/app.log");

Path normalized = path.normalize();
```

Result:

```text
/app/logs/app.log
```

Important:

```text
normalize() is lexical.
It does not access filesystem.
It does not resolve symlinks.
```

---

## 4.4 Absolute Path

```java
Path absolute = path.toAbsolutePath();
```

Important:

```text
toAbsolutePath() does not guarantee file exists.
```

---

## 4.5 Real Path

```java
Path real = path.toRealPath();
```

`toRealPath()`:

```text
requires file exists
resolves symbolic links
returns canonical real filesystem path
```

Useful for security validation.

---

# 5. `Files` Utility

`Files` adalah utility utama untuk modern file operation.

```java
Files.exists(path);
Files.isRegularFile(path);
Files.isDirectory(path);
Files.isReadable(path);
Files.isWritable(path);
Files.isExecutable(path);
```

Example:

```java
Path path = Path.of("data/input.txt");

if (Files.exists(path) && Files.isRegularFile(path)) {
    System.out.println("File exists");
}
```

---

# 6. Existence Checks

```java
boolean exists = Files.exists(path);
boolean notExists = Files.notExists(path);
```

Important:

```text
exists(path) == false does not always mean file definitely does not exist.
Permission/error can make existence unknown.
```

Better for real operation:

```java
try {
    String content = Files.readString(path);
} catch (NoSuchFileException e) {
    // file does not exist
} catch (AccessDeniedException e) {
    // no permission
}
```

Rule:

```text
Prefer doing the operation and handling exception.
Avoid check-then-act race condition for critical file operations.
```

---

# 7. Read Small Text File

```java
Path path = Path.of("data/input.txt");

String content = Files.readString(path, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
```

Without charset:

```java
String content = Files.readString(path);
```

But explicit is better:

```java
Files.readString(path, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
```

Use when:

```text
file is small
config file
test fixture
small JSON/XML
small SQL script
```

Avoid for huge files.

---

# 8. Write Small Text File

```java
Path path = Path.of("data/output.txt");

Files.writeString(
    path,
    "Hello\n",
    StandardCharsets.UTF_8
);
```

With options:

```java
Files.writeString(
    path,
    "Hello\n",
    StandardCharsets.UTF_8,
    StandardOpenOption.CREATE,
    StandardOpenOption.TRUNCATE_EXISTING,
    StandardOpenOption.WRITE
);
```

Append:

```java
Files.writeString(
    path,
    "new line\n",
    StandardCharsets.UTF_8,
    StandardOpenOption.CREATE,
    StandardOpenOption.APPEND
);
```

---

# 9. Read Small Binary File

```java
byte[] bytes = Files.readAllBytes(path);
```

Use for:

```text
small image
small binary payload
test fixture
certificate
small attachment
```

Avoid for very large files.

---

# 10. Write Binary File

```java
byte[] bytes = loadBytes();

Files.write(path, bytes);
```

With options:

```java
Files.write(
    path,
    bytes,
    StandardOpenOption.CREATE,
    StandardOpenOption.TRUNCATE_EXISTING
);
```

---

# 11. Buffered Text Reading

Best default for large text files.

```java
Path path = Path.of("data/orders.csv");

try (BufferedReader reader = Files.newBufferedReader(
    path,
    StandardCharsets.UTF_8
)) {
    String line;

    while ((line = reader.readLine()) != null) {
        process(line);
    }
}
```

Benefits:

```text
memory efficient
line-by-line
good control over parsing/error handling
```

---

# 12. Buffered Text Writing

```java
Path path = Path.of("data/output.txt");

try (BufferedWriter writer = Files.newBufferedWriter(
    path,
    StandardCharsets.UTF_8,
    StandardOpenOption.CREATE,
    StandardOpenOption.TRUNCATE_EXISTING
)) {
    writer.write("hello");
    writer.newLine();
    writer.write("world");
}
```

---

# 13. `Files.lines()`

```java
Path path = Path.of("data/orders.csv");

try (Stream<String> lines = Files.lines(path, StandardCharsets.UTF_8)) {
    lines
        .filter(line -> !line.isBlank())
        .forEach(this::process);
}
```

Important:

```text
Files.lines() returns a stream backed by an open file.
Always close it with try-with-resources.
```

Good:

```java
try (Stream<String> lines = Files.lines(path)) {
    long count = lines.count();
}
```

Bad:

```java
Stream<String> lines = Files.lines(path);
// file may remain open
```

---

# 14. Read All Lines

```java
List<String> lines = Files.readAllLines(path, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
```

Use only when file is small or reasonably bounded.

For huge files, prefer:

```java
try (BufferedReader reader = Files.newBufferedReader(path)) {
    // streaming
}
```

---

# 15. InputStream / OutputStream

## 15.1 Read with InputStream

```java
try (InputStream input = Files.newInputStream(path)) {
    byte[] buffer = new byte[8192];

    int bytesRead;

    while ((bytesRead = input.read(buffer)) != -1) {
        process(buffer, bytesRead);
    }
}
```

## 15.2 Write with OutputStream

```java
try (OutputStream output = Files.newOutputStream(
    path,
    StandardOpenOption.CREATE,
    StandardOpenOption.TRUNCATE_EXISTING
)) {
    output.write(bytes);
}
```

Use streams for:

```text
binary data
network/file copy
large payload
framework APIs
low-level integration
```

---

# 16. Reader / Writer

Use `Reader` / `Writer` for text.

```java
try (Reader reader = Files.newBufferedReader(path, StandardCharsets.UTF_8)) {
    char[] buffer = new char[4096];

    int charsRead;

    while ((charsRead = reader.read(buffer)) != -1) {
        process(buffer, charsRead);
    }
}
```

Writer:

```java
try (Writer writer = Files.newBufferedWriter(path, StandardCharsets.UTF_8)) {
    writer.write("hello");
}
```

Rule:

```text
InputStream/OutputStream = bytes.
Reader/Writer = characters/text.
```

---

# 17. Copy File

```java
Path source = Path.of("data/input.txt");
Path target = Path.of("backup/input.txt");

Files.copy(source, target);
```

Replace existing:

```java
Files.copy(
    source,
    target,
    StandardCopyOption.REPLACE_EXISTING
);
```

Copy attributes:

```java
Files.copy(
    source,
    target,
    StandardCopyOption.REPLACE_EXISTING,
    StandardCopyOption.COPY_ATTRIBUTES
);
```

---

# 18. Copy Stream to File

```java
try (InputStream input = externalSource()) {
    Files.copy(
        input,
        Path.of("data/output.bin"),
        StandardCopyOption.REPLACE_EXISTING
    );
}
```

File to stream:

```java
try (OutputStream output = externalTarget()) {
    Files.copy(path, output);
}
```

---

# 19. Move / Rename File

```java
Files.move(source, target);
```

Replace existing:

```java
Files.move(
    source,
    target,
    StandardCopyOption.REPLACE_EXISTING
);
```

Atomic move if supported:

```java
Files.move(
    source,
    target,
    StandardCopyOption.ATOMIC_MOVE
);
```

Important:

```text
ATOMIC_MOVE may not be supported across different filesystems.
It can throw AtomicMoveNotSupportedException.
```

---

# 20. Delete File

```java
Files.delete(path);
```

If not exists, throws `NoSuchFileException`.

Delete if exists:

```java
boolean deleted = Files.deleteIfExists(path);
```

Directory must be empty:

```java
Files.delete(emptyDirectory);
```

To delete recursively, use `walkFileTree`.

---

# 21. Create File

```java
Files.createFile(path);
```

Throws if already exists.

Safer for parent directory:

```java
Files.createDirectories(path.getParent());
Files.createFile(path);
```

---

# 22. Create Directory

Single directory:

```java
Files.createDirectory(path);
```

Create parent chain:

```java
Files.createDirectories(path);
```

Prefer:

```java
Files.createDirectories(Path.of("data", "exports", "2026"));
```

`createDirectories()` does not fail if directory already exists.

---

# 23. Temporary Files and Directories

Temp file:

```java
Path tempFile = Files.createTempFile("upload-", ".tmp");
```

Temp directory:

```java
Path tempDir = Files.createTempDirectory("job-");
```

Specific parent:

```java
Path tempFile = Files.createTempFile(
    Path.of("/app/tmp"),
    "upload-",
    ".tmp"
);
```

Common pattern:

```java
Path temp = Files.createTempFile("report-", ".tmp");

try {
    Files.writeString(temp, content, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);

    Files.move(
        temp,
        target,
        StandardCopyOption.REPLACE_EXISTING,
        StandardCopyOption.ATOMIC_MOVE
    );
} finally {
    Files.deleteIfExists(temp);
}
```

---

# 24. Atomic Write Pattern

## Problem

Writing directly to target can leave corrupted partial file if process crashes.

Bad:

```java
Files.writeString(target, content);
```

Better:

```java
Path target = Path.of("data/config.json");
Path temp = Files.createTempFile(target.getParent(), "config-", ".tmp");

try {
    Files.writeString(temp, content, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);

    Files.move(
        temp,
        target,
        StandardCopyOption.REPLACE_EXISTING,
        StandardCopyOption.ATOMIC_MOVE
    );
} catch (AtomicMoveNotSupportedException e) {
    Files.move(
        temp,
        target,
        StandardCopyOption.REPLACE_EXISTING
    );
} finally {
    Files.deleteIfExists(temp);
}
```

Use for:

```text
config update
state checkpoint
cache snapshot
export result
generated report
```

---

# 25. StandardOpenOption

Common options:

| Option | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `READ` | open for reading |
| `WRITE` | open for writing |
| `APPEND` | append to end |
| `CREATE` | create if missing |
| `CREATE_NEW` | create only if missing |
| `TRUNCATE_EXISTING` | clear existing file |
| `DELETE_ON_CLOSE` | delete when closed |
| `SPARSE` | hint sparse file |
| `SYNC` | sync content + metadata |
| `DSYNC` | sync content only |

Examples:

```java
Files.newOutputStream(
    path,
    StandardOpenOption.CREATE,
    StandardOpenOption.APPEND
);
```

Create new only:

```java
Files.writeString(
    path,
    content,
    StandardCharsets.UTF_8,
    StandardOpenOption.CREATE_NEW
);
```

This fails if file already exists.

---

# 26. File Metadata

## 26.1 Basic Attributes

```java
BasicFileAttributes attrs = Files.readAttributes(
    path,
    BasicFileAttributes.class
);

long size = attrs.size();
FileTime created = attrs.creationTime();
FileTime modified = attrs.lastModifiedTime();

boolean regular = attrs.isRegularFile();
boolean directory = attrs.isDirectory();
boolean symbolicLink = attrs.isSymbolicLink();
```

---

## 26.2 Last Modified Time

```java
FileTime modified = Files.getLastModifiedTime(path);
```

Set:

```java
Files.setLastModifiedTime(
    path,
    FileTime.from(Instant.now())
);
```

---

## 26.3 Size

```java
long size = Files.size(path);
```

---

# 27. POSIX Permissions

On POSIX-compatible filesystems:

```java
Set<PosixFilePermission> permissions =
    Files.getPosixFilePermissions(path);
```

Set permissions:

```java
Set<PosixFilePermission> permissions = PosixFilePermissions.fromString("rw-r-----");

Files.setPosixFilePermissions(path, permissions);
```

Create file with permissions:

```java
FileAttribute<Set<PosixFilePermission>> attrs =
    PosixFilePermissions.asFileAttribute(
        PosixFilePermissions.fromString("rw-------")
    );

Path file = Files.createFile(path, attrs);
```

Warning:

```text
POSIX permissions are not supported on all filesystems, especially Windows.
```

---

# 28. File Owner

```java
UserPrincipal owner = Files.getOwner(path);
```

Set owner:

```java
UserPrincipalLookupService lookup =
    path.getFileSystem().getUserPrincipalLookupService();

UserPrincipal user = lookup.lookupPrincipalByName("appuser");

Files.setOwner(path, user);
```

---

# 29. Symbolic Links

Create symlink:

```java
Path link = Path.of("current");
Path target = Path.of("releases/v1");

Files.createSymbolicLink(link, target);
```

Check symlink:

```java
boolean isSymlink = Files.isSymbolicLink(link);
```

Read symlink target:

```java
Path symlinkTarget = Files.readSymbolicLink(link);
```

Avoid following links:

```java
Files.isDirectory(path, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS);
```

Read attributes without following links:

```java
BasicFileAttributes attrs = Files.readAttributes(
    path,
    BasicFileAttributes.class,
    LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS
);
```

---

# 30. Directory Listing

```java
try (DirectoryStream<Path> stream = Files.newDirectoryStream(dir)) {
    for (Path entry : stream) {
        System.out.println(entry);
    }
}
```

With glob:

```java
try (DirectoryStream<Path> stream = Files.newDirectoryStream(dir, "*.csv")) {
    for (Path entry : stream) {
        process(entry);
    }
}
```

Modern alternative:

```java
try (Stream<Path> paths = Files.list(dir)) {
    paths.forEach(System.out::println);
}
```

Important:

```text
Files.list() returns a stream backed by open directory resource.
Always close it.
```

---

# 31. Walk Directory

```java
try (Stream<Path> paths = Files.walk(root)) {
    paths
        .filter(Files::isRegularFile)
        .forEach(this::process);
}
```

Limit depth:

```java
try (Stream<Path> paths = Files.walk(root, 2)) {
    paths.forEach(System.out::println);
}
```

Warning:

```text
Files.walk() can be expensive for large trees.
It also returns a closable Stream.
```

---

# 32. Find Files

```java
try (Stream<Path> paths = Files.find(
    root,
    10,
    (path, attrs) -> attrs.isRegularFile() && path.toString().endsWith(".csv")
)) {
    paths.forEach(this::process);
}
```

Use when you need attributes while searching.

---

# 33. Recursive File Visitor

Best for robust recursive operations.

```java
Files.walkFileTree(root, new SimpleFileVisitor<>() {
    @Override
    public FileVisitResult visitFile(
        Path file,
        BasicFileAttributes attrs
    ) throws IOException {
        process(file);
        return FileVisitResult.CONTINUE;
    }

    @Override
    public FileVisitResult visitFileFailed(
        Path file,
        IOException exc
    ) throws IOException {
        System.err.println("Failed to visit: " + file + " error=" + exc);
        return FileVisitResult.CONTINUE;
    }
});
```

---

# 34. Recursive Delete Pattern

```java
public static void deleteRecursively(Path root) throws IOException {
    if (!Files.exists(root)) {
        return;
    }

    Files.walkFileTree(root, new SimpleFileVisitor<>() {
        @Override
        public FileVisitResult visitFile(
            Path file,
            BasicFileAttributes attrs
        ) throws IOException {
            Files.delete(file);
            return FileVisitResult.CONTINUE;
        }

        @Override
        public FileVisitResult postVisitDirectory(
            Path dir,
            IOException exc
        ) throws IOException {
            if (exc != null) {
                throw exc;
            }

            Files.delete(dir);
            return FileVisitResult.CONTINUE;
        }
    });
}
```

Important:

```text
Delete files first, then directories.
```

---

# 35. Recursive Copy Pattern

```java
public static void copyRecursively(Path source, Path target) throws IOException {
    Files.walkFileTree(source, new SimpleFileVisitor<>() {
        @Override
        public FileVisitResult preVisitDirectory(
            Path dir,
            BasicFileAttributes attrs
        ) throws IOException {
            Path relative = source.relativize(dir);
            Path targetDir = target.resolve(relative);

            Files.createDirectories(targetDir);

            return FileVisitResult.CONTINUE;
        }

        @Override
        public FileVisitResult visitFile(
            Path file,
            BasicFileAttributes attrs
        ) throws IOException {
            Path relative = source.relativize(file);
            Path targetFile = target.resolve(relative);

            Files.copy(
                file,
                targetFile,
                StandardCopyOption.REPLACE_EXISTING,
                StandardCopyOption.COPY_ATTRIBUTES
            );

            return FileVisitResult.CONTINUE;
        }
    });
}
```

---

# 36. FileChannel

`FileChannel` adalah NIO channel untuk file.

```java
try (FileChannel channel = FileChannel.open(
    path,
    StandardOpenOption.READ
)) {
    ByteBuffer buffer = ByteBuffer.allocate(8192);

    while (channel.read(buffer) != -1) {
        buffer.flip();

        while (buffer.hasRemaining()) {
            process(buffer.get());
        }

        buffer.clear();
    }
}
```

Buffer lifecycle:

```text
write into buffer
flip()
read from buffer
clear() / compact()
```

---

# 37. ByteBuffer Basics

```java
ByteBuffer buffer = ByteBuffer.allocate(1024);
```

Put:

```java
buffer.put((byte) 1);
buffer.putInt(123);
buffer.putLong(999L);
```

Prepare to read:

```java
buffer.flip();
```

Read:

```java
int value = buffer.getInt();
```

Clear:

```java
buffer.clear();
```

Important fields:

| Concept | Meaning |
|---|---|
| capacity | total buffer size |
| position | current read/write index |
| limit | boundary for read/write |
| mark | optional saved position |

---

# 38. FileChannel Write

```java
try (FileChannel channel = FileChannel.open(
    path,
    StandardOpenOption.CREATE,
    StandardOpenOption.WRITE,
    StandardOpenOption.TRUNCATE_EXISTING
)) {
    ByteBuffer buffer = ByteBuffer.wrap("hello".getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));

    while (buffer.hasRemaining()) {
        channel.write(buffer);
    }
}
```

Important:

```text
channel.write(buffer) may write fewer bytes than remaining.
Loop until buffer has no remaining bytes.
```

---

# 39. Transfer Between Channels

Efficient copy:

```java
try (
    FileChannel source = FileChannel.open(sourcePath, StandardOpenOption.READ);
    FileChannel target = FileChannel.open(
        targetPath,
        StandardOpenOption.CREATE,
        StandardOpenOption.WRITE,
        StandardOpenOption.TRUNCATE_EXISTING
    )
) {
    long size = source.size();
    long position = 0;

    while (position < size) {
        position += source.transferTo(position, size - position, target);
    }
}
```

Useful for large files.

---

# 40. Memory-Mapped File

```java
try (FileChannel channel = FileChannel.open(path, StandardOpenOption.READ)) {
    MappedByteBuffer buffer = channel.map(
        FileChannel.MapMode.READ_ONLY,
        0,
        channel.size()
    );

    while (buffer.hasRemaining()) {
        byte b = buffer.get();
        process(b);
    }
}
```

Use for:

```text
large file random access
read-heavy binary format
index file
high-performance scanning
```

Caution:

```text
memory-mapped files use off-heap/native memory
unmapping behavior can be tricky
not always worth complexity
```

---

# 41. File Locking

```java
try (
    FileChannel channel = FileChannel.open(
        path,
        StandardOpenOption.CREATE,
        StandardOpenOption.WRITE
    );
    FileLock lock = channel.lock()
) {
    // exclusive lock
    writeData(channel);
}
```

Try lock:

```java
FileLock lock = channel.tryLock();

if (lock != null) {
    try {
        // locked
    } finally {
        lock.release();
    }
}
```

Warning:

```text
File locking behavior can vary by OS/filesystem.
It is often advisory, not mandatory.
Be careful on network filesystems.
```

---

# 42. AsynchronousFileChannel

```java
try (AsynchronousFileChannel channel = AsynchronousFileChannel.open(
    path,
    StandardOpenOption.READ
)) {
    ByteBuffer buffer = ByteBuffer.allocate(1024);

    Future<Integer> result = channel.read(buffer, 0);

    int bytesRead = result.get();
}
```

With completion handler:

```java
channel.read(buffer, 0, buffer, new CompletionHandler<>() {
    @Override
    public void completed(Integer result, ByteBuffer attachment) {
        attachment.flip();
        process(attachment);
    }

    @Override
    public void failed(Throwable exc, ByteBuffer attachment) {
        exc.printStackTrace();
    }
});
```

Use rarely in typical backend apps. Often normal blocking I/O with virtual threads is simpler.

---

# 43. WatchService

Monitor directory changes.

```java
WatchService watchService = FileSystems.getDefault().newWatchService();

Path dir = Path.of("data");

dir.register(
    watchService,
    StandardWatchEventKinds.ENTRY_CREATE,
    StandardWatchEventKinds.ENTRY_MODIFY,
    StandardWatchEventKinds.ENTRY_DELETE
);
```

Loop:

```java
while (true) {
    WatchKey key = watchService.take();

    for (WatchEvent<?> event : key.pollEvents()) {
        WatchEvent.Kind<?> kind = event.kind();

        if (kind == StandardWatchEventKinds.OVERFLOW) {
            continue;
        }

        Path fileName = (Path) event.context();
        Path changed = dir.resolve(fileName);

        System.out.println(kind.name() + ": " + changed);
    }

    boolean valid = key.reset();

    if (!valid) {
        break;
    }
}
```

Use case:

```text
reload config
watch import directory
local development tooling
file ingestion
```

Warning:

```text
WatchService behavior is OS/filesystem dependent.
Events can be coalesced.
Always handle OVERFLOW.
Do not use as sole correctness mechanism for critical processing.
```

---

# 44. FileSystem API

Default filesystem:

```java
FileSystem fs = FileSystems.getDefault();
```

Separator:

```java
String separator = fs.getSeparator();
```

Root directories:

```java
for (Path root : fs.getRootDirectories()) {
    System.out.println(root);
}
```

File stores:

```java
for (FileStore store : fs.getFileStores()) {
    System.out.println(store.name());
    System.out.println(store.getTotalSpace());
    System.out.println(store.getUsableSpace());
}
```

---

# 45. Zip FileSystem

Java can treat ZIP/JAR as filesystem.

```java
Path zipPath = Path.of("archive.zip");

URI uri = URI.create("jar:" + zipPath.toUri());

Map<String, String> env = Map.of("create", "true");

try (FileSystem zipFs = FileSystems.newFileSystem(uri, env)) {
    Path fileInZip = zipFs.getPath("/hello.txt");

    Files.writeString(
        fileInZip,
        "Hello from zip",
        StandardCharsets.UTF_8
    );
}
```

Use for:

```text
ZIP creation
reading JAR contents
packaging files
archive inspection
```

---

# 46. Path Security

## Problem: Path Traversal

Dangerous input:

```text
../../etc/passwd
```

Bad:

```java
Path file = uploadDir.resolve(userInput);
```

Better:

```java
Path uploadDir = Path.of("/app/uploads").toRealPath();

Path target = uploadDir.resolve(userInput).normalize();

if (!target.startsWith(uploadDir)) {
    throw new SecurityException("Invalid path");
}
```

Even stronger if target exists:

```java
Path realTarget = target.toRealPath();

if (!realTarget.startsWith(uploadDir)) {
    throw new SecurityException("Invalid path");
}
```

Rule:

```text
Never trust user-supplied paths.
Normalize and validate against allowed base directory.
Be careful with symlinks.
```

---

# 47. Safe Filename Pattern

Avoid allowing arbitrary filename.

```java
public static String safeFileName(String input) {
    String normalized = input.strip();

    normalized = normalized.replaceAll("[^a-zA-Z0-9._-]", "_");

    if (normalized.isBlank()) {
        throw new IllegalArgumentException("Invalid filename");
    }

    return normalized;
}
```

Use:

```java
Path target = uploadDir.resolve(safeFileName(originalFileName));
```

Warning:

```text
Filename sanitization alone is not enough.
Still validate final resolved path against base directory.
```

---

# 48. Resource Management

Always close I/O resources.

```java
try (InputStream input = Files.newInputStream(path)) {
    // use input
}
```

Multiple resources:

```java
try (
    InputStream input = Files.newInputStream(source);
    OutputStream output = Files.newOutputStream(target)
) {
    input.transferTo(output);
}
```

Never rely on finalizers/GC to close files.

---

# 49. `InputStream.transferTo`

Simple copy:

```java
try (
    InputStream input = Files.newInputStream(source);
    OutputStream output = Files.newOutputStream(target)
) {
    input.transferTo(output);
}
```

Good for many normal copy cases.

---

# 50. Exception Types

Common file exceptions:

| Exception | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `NoSuchFileException` | file does not exist |
| `FileAlreadyExistsException` | file already exists |
| `AccessDeniedException` | permission denied |
| `DirectoryNotEmptyException` | delete non-empty directory |
| `NotDirectoryException` | expected directory |
| `FileSystemLoopException` | symlink loop |
| `InvalidPathException` | invalid path string |
| `AtomicMoveNotSupportedException` | atomic move unsupported |
| `IOException` | general I/O failure |

Example:

```java
try {
    Files.delete(path);
} catch (NoSuchFileException e) {
    // ignore or report missing
} catch (DirectoryNotEmptyException e) {
    // directory not empty
} catch (AccessDeniedException e) {
    // permission issue
} catch (IOException e) {
    // generic I/O failure
}
```

---

# 51. Check-Then-Act Race Condition

Bad:

```java
if (!Files.exists(path)) {
    Files.createFile(path);
}
```

Another process/thread can create file between check and create.

Better:

```java
try {
    Files.createFile(path);
} catch (FileAlreadyExistsException e) {
    // already exists
}
```

Rule:

```text
Prefer atomic operation + exception handling.
Avoid check-then-act for critical filesystem changes.
```

---

# 52. File Naming and Extensions

```java
Path path = Path.of("report.csv");

String fileName = path.getFileName().toString();

boolean csv = fileName.endsWith(".csv");
```

Extension helper:

```java
public static Optional<String> extension(Path path) {
    String fileName = path.getFileName().toString();

    int index = fileName.lastIndexOf('.');

    if (index <= 0 || index == fileName.length() - 1) {
        return Optional.empty();
    }

    return Optional.of(fileName.substring(index + 1));
}
```

Warning:

```text
Extension is not reliable security boundary.
Do not trust file extension for content type.
```

---

# 53. Probe Content Type

```java
String contentType = Files.probeContentType(path);
```

Example result:

```text
text/plain
image/png
application/pdf
```

Warning:

```text
probeContentType depends on OS/filesystem/configuration.
Do not rely on it alone for security-critical validation.
```

---

# 54. Large File Processing Pattern

Bad:

```java
List<String> lines = Files.readAllLines(path);
```

Better:

```java
try (BufferedReader reader = Files.newBufferedReader(path, StandardCharsets.UTF_8)) {
    String line;

    while ((line = reader.readLine()) != null) {
        process(line);
    }
}
```

With batching:

```java
List<String> batch = new ArrayList<>(1000);

try (BufferedReader reader = Files.newBufferedReader(path, StandardCharsets.UTF_8)) {
    String line;

    while ((line = reader.readLine()) != null) {
        batch.add(line);

        if (batch.size() == 1000) {
            processBatch(batch);
            batch.clear();
        }
    }

    if (!batch.isEmpty()) {
        processBatch(batch);
    }
}
```

---

# 55. CSV-ish Reading Pattern

Simple only:

```java
try (BufferedReader reader = Files.newBufferedReader(path, StandardCharsets.UTF_8)) {
    String header = reader.readLine();

    String line;

    while ((line = reader.readLine()) != null) {
        String[] columns = line.split(",", -1);
        process(columns);
    }
}
```

Warning:

```text
Real CSV can contain quoted commas, newlines, escaped quotes.
Use a proper CSV library for production CSV parsing.
```

---

# 56. File Upload Storage Pattern

Typical safe flow:

```text
1. receive upload stream
2. generate server-side filename
3. write to temp file
4. validate size/type/checksum
5. atomic move to final location
6. persist metadata
```

Example:

```java
public Path storeUpload(
    InputStream input,
    Path uploadDir,
    String originalFilename
) throws IOException {
    Files.createDirectories(uploadDir);

    String safeName = UUID.randomUUID() + "-" + safeFileName(originalFilename);

    Path temp = Files.createTempFile(uploadDir, "upload-", ".tmp");
    Path target = uploadDir.resolve(safeName).normalize();

    if (!target.startsWith(uploadDir)) {
        throw new SecurityException("Invalid upload path");
    }

    try {
        Files.copy(input, temp, StandardCopyOption.REPLACE_EXISTING);

        Files.move(
            temp,
            target,
            StandardCopyOption.ATOMIC_MOVE
        );

        return target;
    } catch (AtomicMoveNotSupportedException e) {
        Files.move(temp, target, StandardCopyOption.REPLACE_EXISTING);
        return target;
    } finally {
        Files.deleteIfExists(temp);
    }
}
```

---

# 57. Directory Ingestion Pattern

```java
try (Stream<Path> files = Files.list(inputDir)) {
    files
        .filter(Files::isRegularFile)
        .filter(path -> path.toString().endsWith(".csv"))
        .forEach(this::processFileSafely);
}
```

Safer processing:

```java
private void processFileSafely(Path input) {
    Path processing = input.resolveSibling(input.getFileName() + ".processing");
    Path done = input.resolveSibling(input.getFileName() + ".done");
    Path failed = input.resolveSibling(input.getFileName() + ".failed");

    try {
        Files.move(input, processing, StandardCopyOption.ATOMIC_MOVE);

        process(processing);

        Files.move(processing, done, StandardCopyOption.REPLACE_EXISTING);
    } catch (Exception e) {
        try {
            Files.move(processing, failed, StandardCopyOption.REPLACE_EXISTING);
        } catch (IOException moveError) {
            e.addSuppressed(moveError);
        }

        throw new RuntimeException(e);
    }
}
```

Purpose:

```text
avoid processing same file twice
make processing state visible
support restart recovery
```

---

# 58. Temporary Working Directory Pattern

```java
Path workDir = Files.createTempDirectory("job-");

try {
    Path input = workDir.resolve("input.dat");
    Path output = workDir.resolve("output.dat");

    // work with files
} finally {
    deleteRecursively(workDir);
}
```

Use for:

```text
batch job
report generation
archive extraction
data transformation
test isolation
```

---

# 59. Testing File I/O

JUnit temp directory:

```java
@TempDir
Path tempDir;
```

Example:

```java
@Test
void shouldWriteFile() throws IOException {
    Path file = tempDir.resolve("output.txt");

    Files.writeString(file, "hello", StandardCharsets.UTF_8);

    assertEquals("hello", Files.readString(file, StandardCharsets.UTF_8));
}
```

Benefits:

```text
isolated test files
auto cleanup
no dependency on real directories
```

---

# 60. File I/O in Containers

Important container concerns:

```text
filesystem may be ephemeral
working directory may be different
permissions may be restricted
root filesystem may be read-only
persistent data needs mounted volume
large temp files can fill container disk
```

Best practice:

```text
externalize writable directories
configure temp dir
monitor disk usage
do not assume /tmp is unlimited
do not write business data to container image layer
```

Java temp dir:

```java
String tempDir = System.getProperty("java.io.tmpdir");
```

Set:

```bash
java -Djava.io.tmpdir=/app/tmp -jar app.jar
```

---

# 61. File I/O in Kubernetes

Things to remember:

```text
container restart can delete local files
multiple pods do not share local filesystem
persistent volume needed for durable files
watch service on mounted volume may behave differently
file locks on network volumes can be unreliable
```

For enterprise backend:

```text
prefer object storage for durable shared files
prefer database for transactional metadata
prefer message/event for processing coordination
avoid relying on local filesystem for cross-pod coordination
```

---

# 62. Performance Rules

```text
Small file:
- Files.readString/readAllBytes okay

Large text:
- BufferedReader line-by-line

Large binary:
- InputStream buffer or FileChannel

Repeated small writes:
- BufferedWriter

Huge random access:
- FileChannel or MappedByteBuffer

Recursive traversal:
- walkFileTree for control
- Files.walk for simple stream

High concurrency:
- avoid too many simultaneous open files
- limit parallel file operations
```

---

# 63. Common Performance Pitfalls

| Pitfall | Problem |
|---|---|
| `readAllBytes()` on huge file | memory spike |
| `readAllLines()` on huge file | memory spike |
| no buffering | many small syscalls |
| not closing streams | file descriptor leak |
| too many open files | OS limit |
| recursive `Files.walk()` without close | resource leak |
| writing directly to final file | partial/corrupt result |
| excessive fsync | slow writes |
| relying on network filesystem locks | unreliable |
| parallel file processing without limit | disk thrashing |

---

# 64. Common Security Pitfalls

| Pitfall | Risk |
|---|---|
| user controls path directly | path traversal |
| trusting filename extension | spoofed file |
| writing upload to executable location | RCE risk |
| following symlink blindly | escape directory |
| world-readable temp file | secret leak |
| logging full file content | data leak |
| extracting ZIP without validation | zip slip |
| no size limit | disk exhaustion |
| no cleanup | storage exhaustion |

---

# 65. Zip Slip Protection

When extracting ZIP, validate target path.

```java
public static void extractZip(Path zipFile, Path targetDir) throws IOException {
    Files.createDirectories(targetDir);

    Path normalizedTargetDir = targetDir.toRealPath();

    try (FileSystem zipFs = FileSystems.newFileSystem(zipFile)) {
        for (Path root : zipFs.getRootDirectories()) {
            Files.walkFileTree(root, new SimpleFileVisitor<>() {
                @Override
                public FileVisitResult visitFile(
                    Path file,
                    BasicFileAttributes attrs
                ) throws IOException {
                    Path relative = root.relativize(file).toString().isBlank()
                        ? file.getFileName()
                        : root.relativize(file);

                    Path target = normalizedTargetDir
                        .resolve(relative.toString())
                        .normalize();

                    if (!target.startsWith(normalizedTargetDir)) {
                        throw new SecurityException("Zip entry escapes target dir: " + file);
                    }

                    Files.createDirectories(target.getParent());
                    Files.copy(file, target, StandardCopyOption.REPLACE_EXISTING);

                    return FileVisitResult.CONTINUE;
                }
            });
        }
    }
}
```

Core rule:

```text
After resolving archive entry, normalize and ensure it starts with target directory.
```

---

# 66. IO vs NIO vs NIO.2 Summary

| Area | API | Main Abstraction | Best For |
|---|---|---|---|
| Classic IO | `java.io` | Stream, Reader, Writer, File | simple stream processing, legacy APIs |
| NIO | `java.nio` | Buffer, Channel | high-performance, binary, non-blocking-ish primitives |
| NIO.2 | `java.nio.file` | Path, Files, FileSystem | modern filesystem operations |

---

# 67. Classic IO Cheat Sheet

```java
InputStream input;
OutputStream output;
Reader reader;
Writer writer;
BufferedReader bufferedReader;
BufferedWriter bufferedWriter;
File file;
```

Use:

```text
InputStream  -> byte input
OutputStream -> byte output
Reader       -> char/text input
Writer       -> char/text output
Buffered*    -> efficient wrapping
File         -> legacy file reference
```

---

# 68. NIO Cheat Sheet

```java
ByteBuffer buffer = ByteBuffer.allocate(8192);

FileChannel channel = FileChannel.open(path, StandardOpenOption.READ);
```

Pattern:

```java
int n = channel.read(buffer);
buffer.flip();
// read from buffer
buffer.clear();
```

Use:

```text
FileChannel
ByteBuffer
MappedByteBuffer
transferTo/transferFrom
file lock
binary protocol/file format
```

---

# 69. NIO.2 Cheat Sheet

```java
Path path = Path.of("data", "input.txt");

Files.exists(path);
Files.readString(path);
Files.writeString(path, text);
Files.copy(source, target);
Files.move(source, target);
Files.delete(path);
Files.walk(path);
Files.walkFileTree(path, visitor);
```

Use for almost all normal filesystem operations.

---

# 70. Production Checklist

```text
Correctness
[ ] Is Path used instead of legacy File?
[ ] Is charset explicit for text?
[ ] Is large file processed streaming?
[ ] Are streams closed with try-with-resources?
[ ] Are partial writes avoided with temp + atomic move?
[ ] Are recursive operations using walkFileTree when control matters?

Security
[ ] Is user-supplied path normalized and validated?
[ ] Are symlinks considered?
[ ] Are upload filenames sanitized?
[ ] Is file size limited?
[ ] Is ZIP extraction protected against zip slip?
[ ] Are permissions appropriate?
[ ] Are secrets not written/logged accidentally?

Reliability
[ ] Are IOException cases handled specifically?
[ ] Is cleanup done in finally?
[ ] Is temp directory cleaned?
[ ] Is disk-full scenario considered?
[ ] Are file locks not assumed portable?
[ ] Is network filesystem behavior considered?

Performance
[ ] Is buffering used?
[ ] Are huge files not loaded fully into memory?
[ ] Are file streams closed?
[ ] Are directory streams closed?
[ ] Is parallel file processing bounded?
[ ] Is memory-mapped file really needed?

Container/Cloud
[ ] Is writable directory configured?
[ ] Is local file persistence assumption valid?
[ ] Is mounted volume needed?
[ ] Is object storage better?
[ ] Is temp storage monitored?
```

---

# 71. Practical Rules

```text
1. Prefer Path + Files over File.
2. Use explicit UTF-8 for text.
3. Use try-with-resources for all streams/readers/channels.
4. Use readString/readAllBytes only for small files.
5. Use BufferedReader for large text files.
6. Use InputStream/OutputStream for binary streams.
7. Use FileChannel for high-performance or random-access file I/O.
8. Use temp file + atomic move for safe writes.
9. Use createDirectories for parent directory creation.
10. Use walkFileTree for recursive delete/copy.
11. Close Files.lines, Files.list, Files.walk streams.
12. Avoid check-then-act races; perform operation and catch exception.
13. Validate user paths against allowed base directory.
14. Treat symlinks as security-sensitive.
15. Do not assume local filesystem is durable in containers.
16. Do not use filesystem as distributed coordination across pods.
17. Use object storage or database for durable shared data.
18. Limit parallel file operations.
19. Avoid logging sensitive file content.
20. Test file logic with @TempDir.
```

---

# 72. Compact Summary

```text
File
- legacy
- use only for old APIs
- convert to Path

Path
- modern path representation
- resolve, normalize, relativize, toAbsolutePath, toRealPath

Files
- main utility for read/write/copy/move/delete/metadata/walk

InputStream / OutputStream
- byte I/O
- binary data
- streaming

Reader / Writer
- character/text I/O
- requires charset

BufferedReader / BufferedWriter
- efficient text I/O
- best default for large text files

FileChannel
- NIO file channel
- ByteBuffer-based
- transferTo, lock, map

ByteBuffer
- NIO byte container
- flip/clear lifecycle

MappedByteBuffer
- memory-mapped file
- useful but advanced

WatchService
- directory monitoring
- handle OVERFLOW
- OS-dependent

walkFileTree
- robust recursive filesystem operation

Atomic write
- write temp
- fsync if needed
- move atomically

Security
- normalize paths
- validate base directory
- sanitize filename
- protect against symlink/path traversal/zip slip
```

Kesimpulan paling penting: untuk Java modern, **`Path` + `Files` adalah default utama**. Gunakan classic IO untuk stream abstraction, gunakan NIO channel/buffer saat butuh kontrol/performance lebih rendah, dan selalu desain file operation dengan mempertimbangkan **encoding, resource closing, partial write, path traversal, symlink, large file memory usage, dan container filesystem behavior**.

## Response:
7/12/2026, 9:02:30 PM

Berikut **cheat sheet Java I/O Networking, Sockets, URI/URL Processing, dan IPC** untuk Java 17+.

Fokusnya: **TCP/UDP socket, HTTP client, URI/URL, encoding URL, DNS, timeout, TLS, NIO networking, dan komunikasi antar-process**.

# Java Networking, Sockets, URI/URL, IPC Cheat Sheet

---

# 1. Mental Model

```text
Java Networking / IPC
├── URI / URL
│   ├── URI = identifier / syntax
│   └── URL = locator + protocol handler
│
├── HTTP
│   └── java.net.http.HttpClient
│
├── TCP Socket
│   ├── Socket
│   ├── ServerSocket
│   ├── SocketChannel
│   └── ServerSocketChannel
│
├── UDP
│   ├── DatagramSocket
│   └── DatagramPacket
│
├── DNS / Address
│   ├── InetAddress
│   ├── InetSocketAddress
│   └── NetworkInterface
│
├── TLS / HTTPS
│   ├── SSLContext
│   ├── SSLSocket
│   └── HttpsURLConnection / HttpClient
│
└── IPC
    ├── Process stdin/stdout/stderr
    ├── loopback TCP
    ├── Unix domain socket
    ├── files / lock files
    ├── memory-mapped files
    └── OS-specific named pipes
```

Rule utama:

```text
URI = representation of address.
HTTP = application protocol.
TCP socket = reliable byte stream.
UDP socket = unreliable datagram.
IPC = communication between processes, not necessarily over network.
```

---

# 2. Package Map

| Package | Fungsi |
|---|---|
| `java.net` | URI, URL, Socket, ServerSocket, DatagramSocket, InetAddress |
| `java.net.http` | Modern HTTP client sejak Java 11 |
| `java.nio.channels` | NIO socket/channel/selectors |
| `javax.net.ssl` | TLS/SSL socket, SSLContext |
| `java.io` | Stream input/output untuk socket/process |
| `java.lang.ProcessBuilder` | Start process dan IPC via stdin/stdout |
| `java.lang.ProcessHandle` | Inspect/manage process |
| `java.nio.file` | File-based IPC, lock file, mapped file |

---

# 3. URI vs URL

## URI

`URI` adalah representasi sintaks alamat/resource identifier.

```java
URI uri = URI.create("https://api.example.com/orders?id=123");
```

URI parts:

```java
URI uri = URI.create("https://user:pass@example.com:443/orders/123?debug=true#section");

System.out.println(uri.getScheme());      // https
System.out.println(uri.getHost());        // example.com
System.out.println(uri.getPort());        // 443
System.out.println(uri.getPath());        // /orders/123
System.out.println(uri.getQuery());       // debug=true
System.out.println(uri.getFragment());    // section
```

## URL

`URL` adalah locator yang punya protocol handler dan bisa membuka connection.

```java
URL url = new URL("https://example.com");
URLConnection connection = url.openConnection();
```

Modern Java code biasanya lebih baik pakai:

```java
URI uri = URI.create("https://example.com");
```

Lalu gunakan `HttpClient`.

Rule:

```text
Prefer URI for parsing/building/storing addresses.
Use URL only when required by legacy APIs.
```

---

# 4. URI Building

Hindari string concatenation sembarangan:

```java
String url = "https://api.example.com/orders?name=" + name;
```

Masalah:

```text
space
&
?
=
unicode
slash
query escaping
injection-like URL bugs
```

Minimal safe approach untuk query value:

```java
String encodedName = URLEncoder.encode(name, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);

URI uri = URI.create("https://api.example.com/orders?name=" + encodedName);
```

Important:

```text
URLEncoder is for form/query parameter encoding.
It is not general URI path encoding.
```

---

# 5. URI Resolve

```java
URI base = URI.create("https://api.example.com/v1/");

URI orders = base.resolve("orders");
URI order123 = base.resolve("orders/123");
```

Result:

```text
https://api.example.com/v1/orders
https://api.example.com/v1/orders/123
```

Hati-hati trailing slash:

```java
URI base = URI.create("https://api.example.com/v1");
URI resolved = base.resolve("orders");
```

Result bisa menjadi:

```text
https://api.example.com/orders
```

Rule:

```text
Base URI for directories should usually end with "/".
```

---

# 6. URI Normalize

```java
URI uri = URI.create("https://example.com/a/b/../c");

URI normalized = uri.normalize();
```

Result:

```text
https://example.com/a/c
```

Important:

```text
normalize() is lexical.
It does not perform network access.
It does not validate security by itself.
```

---

# 7. HTTP Client Basic

Modern Java HTTP client:

```java
HttpClient client = HttpClient.newHttpClient();

HttpRequest request = HttpRequest.newBuilder()
    .uri(URI.create("https://api.example.com/orders"))
    .GET()
    .build();

HttpResponse<String> response = client.send(
    request,
    HttpResponse.BodyHandlers.ofString()
);

System.out.println(response.statusCode());
System.out.println(response.body());
```

Imports:

```java
import java.net.URI;
import java.net.http.HttpClient;
import java.net.http.HttpRequest;
import java.net.http.HttpResponse;
```

---

# 8. HTTP Client with Timeout

Always set timeout.

```java
HttpClient client = HttpClient.newBuilder()
    .connectTimeout(Duration.ofSeconds(5))
    .build();

HttpRequest request = HttpRequest.newBuilder()
    .uri(URI.create("https://api.example.com/orders"))
    .timeout(Duration.ofSeconds(10))
    .GET()
    .build();
```

Two timeout layers:

| Timeout | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `connectTimeout` | timeout saat membuat koneksi |
| request `timeout` | timeout total request |

Rule:

```text
Every outbound network call must have timeout.
No timeout = possible hanging thread/request.
```

---

# 9. HTTP GET

```java
HttpRequest request = HttpRequest.newBuilder()
    .uri(URI.create("https://api.example.com/orders/123"))
    .header("Accept", "application/json")
    .GET()
    .build();

HttpResponse<String> response = client.send(
    request,
    HttpResponse.BodyHandlers.ofString(StandardCharsets.UTF_8)
);
```

---

# 10. HTTP POST JSON

```java
String json = """
    {
      "customerId": "123",
      "amount": 100.50
    }
    """;

HttpRequest request = HttpRequest.newBuilder()
    .uri(URI.create("https://api.example.com/orders"))
    .header("Content-Type", "application/json")
    .header("Accept", "application/json")
    .timeout(Duration.ofSeconds(10))
    .POST(HttpRequest.BodyPublishers.ofString(json, StandardCharsets.UTF_8))
    .build();

HttpResponse<String> response = client.send(
    request,
    HttpResponse.BodyHandlers.ofString(StandardCharsets.UTF_8)
);
```

---

# 11. HTTP Async

```java
CompletableFuture<HttpResponse<String>> future =
    client.sendAsync(
        request,
        HttpResponse.BodyHandlers.ofString(StandardCharsets.UTF_8)
    );

future.thenApply(HttpResponse::body)
    .thenAccept(System.out::println);
```

With timeout:

```java
CompletableFuture<HttpResponse<String>> future =
    client.sendAsync(request, HttpResponse.BodyHandlers.ofString())
        .orTimeout(5, TimeUnit.SECONDS);
```

---

# 12. HTTP Status Handling

```java
HttpResponse<String> response = client.send(
    request,
    HttpResponse.BodyHandlers.ofString()
);

int status = response.statusCode();

if (status >= 200 && status < 300) {
    return response.body();
}

if (status == 404) {
    throw new NotFoundException("Resource not found");
}

if (status >= 500) {
    throw new DownstreamException("Server error: " + status);
}

throw new DownstreamException("Unexpected status: " + status);
```

Decision:

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `2xx` | success |
| `3xx` | redirect |
| `4xx` | client/request problem |
| `5xx` | downstream/server problem |

---

# 13. HTTP Headers

Set headers:

```java
HttpRequest request = HttpRequest.newBuilder()
    .uri(uri)
    .header("Accept", "application/json")
    .header("Authorization", "Bearer " + token)
    .build();
```

Read headers:

```java
HttpHeaders headers = response.headers();

Optional<String> contentType = headers.firstValue("Content-Type");

Map<String, List<String>> allHeaders = headers.map();
```

Security rule:

```text
Never log Authorization, Cookie, Set-Cookie, API keys, or tokens.
```

---

# 14. HTTP Redirect

```java
HttpClient client = HttpClient.newBuilder()
    .followRedirects(HttpClient.Redirect.NORMAL)
    .build();
```

Options:

| Option | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `NEVER` | do not follow |
| `NORMAL` | follow normal redirects |
| `ALWAYS` | follow all redirects |

Rule:

```text
Be careful following redirects when Authorization headers are involved.
```

---

# 15. HTTP Version

```java
HttpClient client = HttpClient.newBuilder()
    .version(HttpClient.Version.HTTP_2)
    .build();
```

Request can also specify:

```java
HttpRequest request = HttpRequest.newBuilder()
    .uri(uri)
    .version(HttpClient.Version.HTTP_2)
    .GET()
    .build();
```

---

# 16. HTTP Proxy

```java
HttpClient client = HttpClient.newBuilder()
    .proxy(ProxySelector.of(new InetSocketAddress("proxy.local", 8080)))
    .build();
```

Use case:

```text
corporate proxy
egress control
on-prem network
restricted environment
```

---

# 17. HTTP Body Handlers

| Handler | Use |
|---|---|
| `ofString()` | text response |
| `ofByteArray()` | binary response |
| `ofFile(path)` | download to file |
| `discarding()` | ignore body |
| `ofInputStream()` | streaming body |

Download file:

```java
HttpResponse<Path> response = client.send(
    request,
    HttpResponse.BodyHandlers.ofFile(Path.of("download.bin"))
);
```

Binary:

```java
HttpResponse<byte[]> response = client.send(
    request,
    HttpResponse.BodyHandlers.ofByteArray()
);
```

---

# 18. HTTP Request Body Publishers

| Publisher | Use |
|---|---|
| `noBody()` | no body |
| `ofString()` | text/json |
| `ofByteArray()` | binary |
| `ofFile()` | upload file |
| `ofInputStream()` | streaming input |

Upload file:

```java
HttpRequest request = HttpRequest.newBuilder()
    .uri(uri)
    .header("Content-Type", "application/octet-stream")
    .POST(HttpRequest.BodyPublishers.ofFile(Path.of("data.bin")))
    .build();
```

---

# 19. TCP Client Socket

TCP gives reliable byte stream.

```java
try (Socket socket = new Socket()) {
    socket.connect(
        new InetSocketAddress("example.com", 80),
        5_000
    );

    socket.setSoTimeout(10_000);

    OutputStream output = socket.getOutputStream();
    InputStream input = socket.getInputStream();

    output.write("hello\n".getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));
    output.flush();

    byte[] buffer = new byte[1024];
    int n = input.read(buffer);

    if (n != -1) {
        String response = new String(buffer, 0, n, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
        System.out.println(response);
    }
}
```

Important:

```text
connect timeout != read timeout.
Set both.
```

---

# 20. TCP Server Socket

```java
try (ServerSocket serverSocket = new ServerSocket(8080)) {
    while (true) {
        Socket client = serverSocket.accept();

        Thread.startVirtualThread(() -> handleClient(client));
    }
}
```

Handler:

```java
private static void handleClient(Socket socket) {
    try (socket;
         BufferedReader reader = new BufferedReader(
             new InputStreamReader(socket.getInputStream(), StandardCharsets.UTF_8)
         );
         BufferedWriter writer = new BufferedWriter(
             new OutputStreamWriter(socket.getOutputStream(), StandardCharsets.UTF_8)
         )) {

        String line;

        while ((line = reader.readLine()) != null) {
            writer.write("echo: " + line);
            writer.newLine();
            writer.flush();
        }

    } catch (IOException e) {
        e.printStackTrace();
    }
}
```

For Java 17 without virtual threads:

```java
ExecutorService executor = Executors.newFixedThreadPool(100);

while (true) {
    Socket client = serverSocket.accept();
    executor.submit(() -> handleClient(client));
}
```

---

# 21. Socket Timeout

Connect timeout:

```java
Socket socket = new Socket();

socket.connect(
    new InetSocketAddress("host", 1234),
    5_000
);
```

Read timeout:

```java
socket.setSoTimeout(10_000);
```

If read timeout occurs:

```java
catch (SocketTimeoutException e) {
    // read timed out
}
```

Rule:

```text
Always set connect timeout and read timeout for raw sockets.
```

---

# 22. TCP Stream Framing

TCP is a byte stream, not message stream.

Wrong assumption:

```text
1 write = 1 read
```

Not guaranteed.

If sender writes:

```java
output.write("hello".getBytes());
output.write("world".getBytes());
```

Receiver may read:

```text
helloworld
```

or:

```text
hel
lowor
ld
```

You need framing.

Common framing patterns:

| Pattern | Example |
|---|---|
| newline-delimited | `message\n` |
| length-prefixed | `length + bytes` |
| fixed-size frame | exactly N bytes |
| protocol parser | HTTP, SMTP, custom |

---

# 23. Newline-Delimited Protocol

Writer:

```java
BufferedWriter writer = new BufferedWriter(
    new OutputStreamWriter(socket.getOutputStream(), StandardCharsets.UTF_8)
);

writer.write("PING");
writer.newLine();
writer.flush();
```

Reader:

```java
BufferedReader reader = new BufferedReader(
    new InputStreamReader(socket.getInputStream(), StandardCharsets.UTF_8)
);

String message = reader.readLine();
```

Good for simple text protocols.

Warning:

```text
readLine() waits until newline or EOF.
If peer never sends newline, your thread can block until timeout.
```

---

# 24. Length-Prefixed Protocol

Sender:

```java
byte[] payload = json.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);

DataOutputStream out = new DataOutputStream(socket.getOutputStream());

out.writeInt(payload.length);
out.write(payload);
out.flush();
```

Receiver:

```java
DataInputStream in = new DataInputStream(socket.getInputStream());

int length = in.readInt();

if (length < 0 || length > 10_000_000) {
    throw new IOException("Invalid frame length: " + length);
}

byte[] payload = in.readNBytes(length);

String json = new String(payload, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
```

Security rule:

```text
Always validate frame length.
Never trust remote peer length blindly.
```

---

# 25. Half-Close Socket

You can signal no more outgoing data:

```java
socket.shutdownOutput();
```

Read can still continue.

Use case:

```text
client sends request body then closes output side
server responds then closes connection
```

---

# 26. Keepalive / TCP Options

```java
socket.setKeepAlive(true);
socket.setTcpNoDelay(true);
socket.setReuseAddress(true);
socket.setReceiveBufferSize(64 * 1024);
socket.setSendBufferSize(64 * 1024);
```

Common options:

| Option | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `setKeepAlive(true)` | TCP keepalive |
| `setTcpNoDelay(true)` | disable Nagle algorithm |
| `setSoTimeout(ms)` | read timeout |
| `setReuseAddress(true)` | reuse local address |
| `setReceiveBufferSize()` | socket receive buffer |
| `setSendBufferSize()` | socket send buffer |

Rule:

```text
Do not tune socket options blindly.
Start with timeout and protocol correctness.
```

---

# 27. UDP Datagram

UDP is message/datagram-oriented, unreliable, unordered.

## UDP Sender

```java
try (DatagramSocket socket = new DatagramSocket()) {
    byte[] data = "hello".getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);

    DatagramPacket packet = new DatagramPacket(
        data,
        data.length,
        InetAddress.getByName("localhost"),
        9999
    );

    socket.send(packet);
}
```

## UDP Receiver

```java
try (DatagramSocket socket = new DatagramSocket(9999)) {
    byte[] buffer = new byte[1024];

    DatagramPacket packet = new DatagramPacket(buffer, buffer.length);

    socket.receive(packet);

    String message = new String(
        packet.getData(),
        packet.getOffset(),
        packet.getLength(),
        StandardCharsets.UTF_8
    );

    System.out.println(message);
}
```

UDP risks:

```text
packet loss
duplicate packets
out-of-order packets
size limits
no built-in connection
no backpressure
```

Use UDP for:

```text
metrics
discovery
low-latency lossy communication
custom protocol
DNS-like query
```

---

# 28. UDP Timeout

```java
DatagramSocket socket = new DatagramSocket(9999);
socket.setSoTimeout(5_000);

try {
    socket.receive(packet);
} catch (SocketTimeoutException e) {
    // no packet received in time
}
```

---

# 29. Multicast

```java
InetAddress group = InetAddress.getByName("230.0.0.0");

try (MulticastSocket socket = new MulticastSocket(4446)) {
    socket.joinGroup(group);

    byte[] buffer = new byte[1024];
    DatagramPacket packet = new DatagramPacket(buffer, buffer.length);

    socket.receive(packet);

    socket.leaveGroup(group);
}
```

Warning:

```text
Multicast support depends on network environment, firewall, routing, and OS settings.
```

# 30. DNS / InetAddress

Resolve hostname:

```java
InetAddress address = InetAddress.getByName("example.com");

System.out.println(address.getHostAddress());
```

All addresses:

```java
InetAddress[] addresses = InetAddress.getAllByName("example.com");

for (InetAddress addr : addresses) {
    System.out.println(addr);
}
```

Local host:

```java
InetAddress local = InetAddress.getLocalHost();
```

Important:

```text
DNS resolution can block.
DNS caching behavior depends on JVM/security settings.
Avoid resolving host repeatedly in hot path.
```

# 31. InetSocketAddress

Represents host + port.

```java
InetSocketAddress address =
    new InetSocketAddress("api.example.com", 443);
```

Unresolved address:

```java
InetSocketAddress unresolved =
    InetSocketAddress.createUnresolved("api.example.com", 443);
```

Use for socket connect:

```java
socket.connect(address, 5_000);
```

# 32. NetworkInterface

List interfaces:

```java
Enumeration<NetworkInterface> interfaces =
    NetworkInterface.getNetworkInterfaces();

while (interfaces.hasMoreElements()) {
    NetworkInterface ni = interfaces.nextElement();

    System.out.println(ni.getName());
    System.out.println(ni.getDisplayName());

    Enumeration<InetAddress> addresses = ni.getInetAddresses();

    while (addresses.hasMoreElements()) {
        System.out.println("  " + addresses.nextElement());
    }
}
```

Use case:

```text
diagnostic tooling
binding to specific interface
multicast
container/network debugging
```
