| Missing intermediate cert in server chain | client validation failure |
| Wrong alias import | private key not matched with cert |
| Keystore password committed to Git | secret leak |
| Same private key reused across many services | blast radius |
| No revocation policy | compromised cert remains trusted |
| mTLS without authorization mapping | over-trust |
| Certificate pinning without rotation plan | outage risk |
| Leaf cert imported as CA trust randomly | brittle trust |
| No SAN | hostname verification failure |
| `CN=localhost` used everywhere | broken identity |
| Long-lived certs | larger compromise window |

---

# 49. Production Checklist

```text
PKI Design
[ ] Who is the CA?
[ ] Public CA or private CA?
[ ] Root/intermediate hierarchy defined?
[ ] Certificate policy defined?
[ ] Validity period defined?
[ ] Revocation policy defined?

Keystore
[ ] Private key stored securely?
[ ] Keystore type PKCS12?
[ ] Alias documented?
[ ] Certificate chain complete?
[ ] Password managed as secret?
[ ] File permissions restricted?

Truststore
[ ] Trust anchors intentional?
[ ] No random unrelated certs?
[ ] Truststore versioned/deployed repeatably?
[ ] Rotation overlap supported?
[ ] Custom trust separated from global cacerts?

Certificate
[ ] SAN correct?
[ ] EKU correct?
[ ] Key usage correct?
[ ] Expiry monitored?
[ ] Serial/fingerprint recorded?
[ ] Owner team recorded?

TLS / Java
[ ] Hostname verification enabled?
[ ] Certificate validation enabled?
[ ] No trust-all code?
[ ] SSLContext explicit where needed?
[ ] mTLS client cert selected correctly?
[ ] Same JDK/container tested?

Operations
[ ] Renewal process tested?
[ ] Expiry alerts configured?
[ ] Emergency revoke/replace process exists?
[ ] TLS debug procedure documented?
[ ] Logs do not leak private key/passwords?
```

---

# 50. Debugging Checklist

When PKI/TLS fails:

```text
[ ] Is hostname in SAN?
[ ] Is certificate expired?
[ ] Is system clock correct?
[ ] Is root/intermediate CA in truststore?
[ ] Is server sending intermediate chain?
[ ] Is alias correct?
[ ] Is keystore password correct?
[ ] Is key password correct?
[ ] Is keystore type correct?
[ ] Is client cert required?
[ ] Is client sending cert?
[ ] Does client cert have clientAuth EKU?
[ ] Does server cert have serverAuth EKU?
[ ] Is TLS terminated by proxy/gateway?
[ ] Is corporate proxy intercepting TLS?
[ ] Is algorithm disabled by Java security policy?
```

---

# 51. Java PKI Quick Commands

```bash
# list keystore
keytool -list -v -keystore app-keystore.p12 -storetype PKCS12

# generate key pair
keytool -genkeypair \
  -alias app-server \
  -keyalg RSA \
  -keysize 3072 \
  -keystore app-keystore.p12 \
  -storetype PKCS12 \
  -dname "CN=app.internal.example.com,O=Example,C=ID" \
  -ext "SAN=dns:app.internal.example.com"

# create CSR
keytool -certreq \
  -alias app-server \
  -file app-server.csr \
  -keystore app-keystore.p12 \
  -storetype PKCS12 \
  -ext "SAN=dns:app.internal.example.com"

# import trusted CA
keytool -importcert \
  -alias example-root-ca \
  -file root-ca.crt \
  -keystore app-truststore.p12 \
  -storetype PKCS12 \
  -trustcacerts

# import signed certificate to private-key alias
keytool -importcert \
  -alias app-server \
  -file app-server.crt \
  -keystore app-keystore.p12 \
  -storetype PKCS12

# export public certificate
keytool -exportcert \
  -alias app-server \
  -rfc \
  -file app-server.crt \
  -keystore app-keystore.p12 \
  -storetype PKCS12

# print certificate
keytool -printcert -file app-server.crt
```

---

# 52. Compact Summary

```text
PKI
- trust system based on public/private keys and certificates

Private key
- secret
- proves identity
- signs/decrypts depending use

Certificate
- public key + identity + issuer + validity + extensions
- signed by CA

CA
- issues certificates
- root CA is trust anchor
- intermediate CA signs leaf certs

Keystore
- contains your private key and certificate chain
- used by KeyManager

Truststore
- contains trusted CA/certs
- used by TrustManager

CSR
- request to CA to sign public key + identity

Certificate Chain
- leaf -> intermediate -> root
- must validate signatures, validity, usage, hostname, and optionally revocation

SAN
- modern TLS hostname identity
- must match requested DNS/IP

mTLS
- server authenticates client cert
- client authenticates server cert
- app still performs authorization

Java
- keytool for key/cert operations
- KeyStore for loading stores
- TrustManagerFactory for trust
- KeyManagerFactory for identity
- SSLContext for TLS
- CertPathValidator for chain validation

Production Rules
- do not disable hostname verification
- do not use trust-all TrustManager
- monitor expiry
- rotate before expiry
- keep private keys secret
- manage truststores deliberately
```

Kesimpulan praktis: **PKI di Java adalah soal trust management, bukan sekadar file `.p12` atau `.crt`.** Yang paling penting adalah memastikan **private key aman, SAN benar, chain lengkap, truststore terkendali, hostname verification aktif, mTLS identity dipetakan ke authorization, revocation policy jelas, dan certificate rotation tidak menunggu outage.**

---

**Sources:**

- [RFC 5280 - Internet X.509 Public Key Infrastructure ...](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc5280?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- [javax.net.ssl (Java SE 21 & JDK 21)](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/api/java.base/javax/net/ssl/package-summary.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- [The keytool Command](https://cr.openjdk.org/~jjg/8261930/docs/specs/man/keytool.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- [SP 800-57 Part 1 Rev. 5, Recommendation for Key Management](https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/57/pt1/r5/final?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

## Response:
7/12/2026, 9:49:34 PM

Berikut **cheat sheet Java Authentication & Authorization Service** untuk **Java 17+**, dengan konteks enterprise backend: **Jakarta REST/JAX-RS, service-to-service API, JWT/OAuth/OIDC, session, password, RBAC/ABAC, tenant isolation, audit, dan production security**.

# Java Authentication & Authorization Service Cheat Sheet

---

# 1. Mental Model

```text
Authentication / AuthN
= membuktikan "siapa kamu?"

Authorization / AuthZ
= menentukan "kamu boleh melakukan apa?"

Identity
= representasi user/service/client setelah berhasil diautentikasi

Credential
= bukti yang dipakai untuk login
  password, OTP, passkey, certificate, token, API key

Principal
= identitas terautentikasi di aplikasi

Permission
= hak melakukan action terhadap resource

Policy
= aturan untuk menentukan allow/deny
```

Authentication dan authorization adalah konsep berbeda: authentication memverifikasi identitas, sedangkan authorization menentukan akses apa yang dimiliki identitas tersebut. NIST juga mendefinisikan authorization sebagai permission/right yang diberikan ke entity untuk mengakses resource. ([Auth0](https://auth0.com/docs/get-started/identity-fundamentals/authentication-and-authorization?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 2. AuthN vs AuthZ

| Area | Authentication | Authorization |
|---|---|---|
| Pertanyaan | “Siapa kamu?” | “Kamu boleh apa?” |
| Input | credential | identity + resource + action |
| Output | principal/session/token | allow/deny |
| Contoh | login password, OIDC login, mTLS | `order:read`, `order:approve` |
| Failure | `401 Unauthorized` | `403 Forbidden` |
| Risiko utama | account takeover | privilege escalation |

Rule:

```text
401 = belum/ gagal terautentikasi
403 = sudah terautentikasi tapi tidak boleh
```

---

# 3. Service Architecture

```text
Client
  ↓
API Gateway / Ingress
  ↓
Authentication Layer
  - validate token/session/API key/mTLS
  - build Principal
  ↓
Authorization Layer
  - check permission/policy
  - enforce tenant/resource ownership
  ↓
Application Service
  ↓
Database / Downstream
```

Untuk enterprise system, jangan hanya mengandalkan auth di gateway. Resource server tetap perlu melakukan **local validation** minimal terhadap token/session/context dan **authorization check** pada boundary aplikasi.

---

# 4. Auth Service Responsibilities

```text
Authentication Service / Identity Service:
[ ] user registration
[ ] login
[ ] password verification
[ ] MFA challenge
[ ] issue access token
[ ] issue refresh token
[ ] revoke token/session
[ ] rotate keys
[ ] expose JWKS
[ ] audit login events
[ ] risk checks
[ ] password reset
[ ] account lock / throttling
```

Resource Service:

```text
Resource Service:
[ ] validate access token/session
[ ] extract principal
[ ] check role/permission/policy
[ ] enforce tenant isolation
[ ] enforce object ownership
[ ] audit sensitive actions
```

---

# 5. Authentication Method Matrix

| Method | Best For | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Username/password | human login | must use password hashing |
| MFA | higher assurance login | TOTP, WebAuthn/passkey, push |
| OIDC | SSO / federated login | identity layer on OAuth |
| OAuth2 access token | API authorization | resource server validates token |
| Session cookie | browser web app | needs CSRF protection |
| JWT bearer token | API stateless-ish auth | validate signature + claims |
| API key | simple machine/client auth | rotate, scope, hash at rest |
| mTLS | service-to-service / partner | still needs authorization |
| HMAC request signing | webhook/B2B | timestamp + nonce required |
| Service account token | workload auth | short-lived preferred |

OpenID Connect is an identity layer on top of OAuth 2.0; it introduces the ID Token as a security token containing claims about end-user authentication. OAuth 2.0 and its current security best practices are for delegated authorization, not direct user authentication by themselves. ([OpenID Foundation](https://openid.net/specs/openid-connect-core-1_0.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 6. Token Types

| Token | Purpose | Audience |
|---|---|---|
| ID Token | tells client who the user is | client application |
| Access Token | authorizes API access | resource server/API |
| Refresh Token | obtains new access token | authorization server |
| Session ID | browser session lookup | web app backend |
| API Key | identifies calling app/client | API gateway/service |
| CSRF Token | prevents cross-site request forgery | browser app |

Rule penting:

```text
Do not use ID Token as API access token.
Do not send refresh token to resource APIs.
Do not put bearer tokens in URL query string.
```

OAuth 2.1 guidance moves toward safer defaults such as PKCE for authorization code clients, omitting implicit grant and resource owner password credentials grant, avoiding bearer tokens in query strings, and requiring refresh-token protections for public clients. ([OAuth Community Site](https://oauth.net/2.1/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 7. Principal Model

```java
public record AuthenticatedPrincipal(
    String subject,
    String username,
    Set<String> roles,
    Set<String> permissions,
    String tenantId,
    String authMethod,
    Instant authenticatedAt
) {
    public boolean hasPermission(String permission) {
        return permissions.contains(permission);
    }

    public boolean hasRole(String role) {
        return roles.contains(role);
    }
}
```

Example:

```java
AuthenticatedPrincipal principal = new AuthenticatedPrincipal(
    "user-123",
    "fajar",
    Set.of("ORDER_MANAGER"),
    Set.of("order:read", "order:approve"),
    "tenant-a",
    "OIDC",
    Instant.now()
);
```

Rule:

```text
Principal should contain identity and security context.
Principal should not contain raw credential, password, token secret, or private key.
```

---

# 8. Permission Naming

Good permission names:

```text
order:create
order:read
order:update
order:delete
order:approve
order:cancel

quote:read
quote:submit
quote:approve

user:read
user:manage

workflow:start
workflow:cancel
workflow:retry
```

Pattern:

```text
<resource>:<action>
```

Avoid vague permissions:

```text
admin
full_access
super
can_do_stuff
manage
```

Better:

```text
order:approve
billing:refund
tenant:user:create
workflow:incident:resolve
```

---

# 9. RBAC Pattern

Role-Based Access Control:

```text
User -> Role -> Permission
```

Example:

```java
public enum Role {
    VIEWER,
    ORDER_OPERATOR,
    ORDER_MANAGER,
    ADMIN
}
```

```java
public enum Permission {
    ORDER_READ,
    ORDER_CREATE,
    ORDER_APPROVE,
    ORDER_CANCEL,
    USER_MANAGE
}
```

Role mapping:

```java
private static final Map<Role, Set<Permission>> ROLE_PERMISSIONS = Map.of(
    Role.VIEWER, EnumSet.of(Permission.ORDER_READ),
    Role.ORDER_OPERATOR, EnumSet.of(
        Permission.ORDER_READ,
        Permission.ORDER_CREATE,
        Permission.ORDER_CANCEL
    ),
    Role.ORDER_MANAGER, EnumSet.of(
        Permission.ORDER_READ,
        Permission.ORDER_CREATE,
        Permission.ORDER_APPROVE,
        Permission.ORDER_CANCEL
    ),
    Role.ADMIN, EnumSet.allOf(Permission.class)
);
```

Flatten permissions:

```java
public static Set<Permission> permissionsOf(Set<Role> roles) {
    EnumSet<Permission> result = EnumSet.noneOf(Permission.class);

    for (Role role : roles) {
        result.addAll(ROLE_PERMISSIONS.getOrDefault(role, Set.of()));
    }

    return result;
}
```

RBAC cocok untuk enterprise karena mudah dipahami dan dikelola, tetapi sering kurang cukup untuk aturan yang bergantung pada tenant, resource ownership, waktu, status workflow, atau atribut domain.

---

# 10. ABAC / Policy-Based Access

Attribute-Based Access Control:

```text
Decision = subject attributes + resource attributes + action + environment
```

Example:

```java
public record AuthorizationRequest(
    AuthenticatedPrincipal principal,
    String action,
    String resourceType,
    String resourceId,
    String tenantId,
    Map<String, Object> resourceAttributes
) {}
```

Policy:

```java
public interface AuthorizationPolicy {
    boolean isAllowed(AuthorizationRequest request);
}
```

Tenant policy:

```java
public final class SameTenantPolicy implements AuthorizationPolicy {
    @Override
    public boolean isAllowed(AuthorizationRequest request) {
        return Objects.equals(
            request.principal().tenantId(),
            request.tenantId()
        );
    }
}
```

Owner policy:

```java
public final class OwnerCanReadOrderPolicy implements AuthorizationPolicy {
    @Override
    public boolean isAllowed(AuthorizationRequest request) {
        if (!"order:read".equals(request.action())) {
            return false;
        }

        Object ownerId = request.resourceAttributes().get("ownerId");

        return Objects.equals(request.principal().subject(), ownerId);
    }
}
```

Use ABAC when:

```text
tenant isolation matters
resource owner matters
workflow status matters
risk score matters
environment/time/location matters
delegated approval matters
```

---

# 11. Authorization Decision

```java
public enum AuthorizationDecision {
    ALLOW,
    DENY
}
```

```java
public record AuthorizationResult(
    AuthorizationDecision decision,
    String reasonCode
) {
    public boolean allowed() {
        return decision == AuthorizationDecision.ALLOW;
    }
}
```

Service:

```java
public final class AuthorizationService {
    public AuthorizationResult requirePermission(
        AuthenticatedPrincipal principal,
        String requiredPermission
    ) {
        if (principal.permissions().contains(requiredPermission)) {
            return new AuthorizationResult(
                AuthorizationDecision.ALLOW,
                "PERMISSION_GRANTED"
            );
        }

        return new AuthorizationResult(
            AuthorizationDecision.DENY,
            "MISSING_PERMISSION"
        );
    }
}
```

Usage:

```java
AuthorizationResult result =
    authorizationService.requirePermission(principal, "order:approve");

if (!result.allowed()) {
    throw new ForbiddenException(result.reasonCode());
}
```

Rule:

```text
Default deny.
Allow only when explicitly permitted.
```

---

# 12. Jakarta REST Authentication Filter

```java
@Provider
@Priority(Priorities.AUTHENTICATION)
public final class AuthenticationFilter implements ContainerRequestFilter {
    private final TokenVerifier tokenVerifier;

    public AuthenticationFilter(TokenVerifier tokenVerifier) {
        this.tokenVerifier = tokenVerifier;
    }

    @Override
    public void filter(ContainerRequestContext requestContext) {
        String authorization =
            requestContext.getHeaderString(HttpHeaders.AUTHORIZATION);

        if (authorization == null || !authorization.startsWith("Bearer ")) {
            abortUnauthorized(requestContext);
            return;
        }

        String token = authorization.substring("Bearer ".length());

        try {
            AuthenticatedPrincipal principal =
                tokenVerifier.verify(token);

            requestContext.setProperty("principal", principal);

        } catch (InvalidTokenException e) {
            abortUnauthorized(requestContext);
        }
    }

    private void abortUnauthorized(ContainerRequestContext context) {
        context.abortWith(
            Response.status(Response.Status.UNAUTHORIZED)
                .entity(Map.of("code", "UNAUTHORIZED"))
                .build()
        );
    }
}
```

---

# 13. Jakarta REST Authorization Filter

```java
@NameBinding
@Retention(RetentionPolicy.RUNTIME)
@Target({ElementType.TYPE, ElementType.METHOD})
public @interface RequiresPermission {
    String value();
}
```

Usage:

```java
@Path("/orders")
public class OrderResource {
    @POST
    @Path("/{id}/approve")
    @RequiresPermission("order:approve")
    public Response approve(@PathParam("id") String orderId) {
        return Response.ok().build();
    }
}
```

Filter:

```java
@Provider
@RequiresPermission("")
@Priority(Priorities.AUTHORIZATION)
public final class AuthorizationFilter implements ContainerRequestFilter {
    private final AuthorizationService authorizationService;

    @Context
    ResourceInfo resourceInfo;

    public AuthorizationFilter(AuthorizationService authorizationService) {
        this.authorizationService = authorizationService;
    }

    @Override
    public void filter(ContainerRequestContext context) {
        RequiresPermission annotation =
            resourceInfo.getResourceMethod()
                .getAnnotation(RequiresPermission.class);

        if (annotation == null) {
            annotation = resourceInfo.getResourceClass()
                .getAnnotation(RequiresPermission.class);
        }

        if (annotation == null) {
            return;
        }

        AuthenticatedPrincipal principal =
            (AuthenticatedPrincipal) context.getProperty("principal");

        if (principal == null) {
            context.abortWith(Response.status(401).build());
            return;
        }

        AuthorizationResult result =
            authorizationService.requirePermission(
                principal,
                annotation.value()
            );

        if (!result.allowed()) {
            context.abortWith(
                Response.status(Response.Status.FORBIDDEN)
                    .entity(Map.of("code", result.reasonCode()))
                    .build()
            );
        }
    }
}
```

---

# 14. Token Verification Interface

```java
public interface TokenVerifier {
    AuthenticatedPrincipal verify(String token)
        throws InvalidTokenException;
}
```

JWT verifier should check:

```text
signature
issuer
audience
expiration
not-before
issued-at sanity
algorithm allowlist
key id / JWKS key
token type
scope/permissions
tenant claim
subject
```

OWASP’s JWT for Java guidance focuses on common JWT implementation risks in Java, and OAuth security best current practice emphasizes avoiding weaker OAuth flows and validating tokens carefully in the intended context. ([OWASP Cheat Sheet Series](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/JSON_Web_Token_for_Java_Cheat_Sheet.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 15. JWT Claims Checklist

Common claims:

| Claim | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `iss` | issuer |
| `sub` | subject / user ID |
| `aud` | audience |
| `exp` | expiration time |
| `nbf` | not before |
| `iat` | issued at |
| `jti` | token id |
| `scope` | OAuth scopes |
| `roles` | roles |
| `permissions` | explicit permissions |
| `tenant_id` | tenant |
| `typ` | token type, if used |

Validation rule:

```text
Never accept JWT just because it decodes.
Decode is not validation.
```

---

# 16. JWT Anti-Pattern

Bad:

```java
String[] parts = token.split("\\.");
String payloadJson = new String(
    Base64.getUrlDecoder().decode(parts[1]),
    StandardCharsets.UTF_8
);

// WRONG: trusting decoded payload without verifying signature
```

Correct principle:

```text
Use a mature JWT library.
Validate signature and claims.
Allowlist algorithms.
Do not accept alg=none.
Do not confuse ID token and access token.
```

---

# 17. Session-Based Auth

Use session cookie for browser apps when server-side session control is needed.

```text
Browser
  ↓ Cookie: SESSION_ID=...
Backend
  ↓ lookup session
Session Store
```

Session object:

```java
public record Session(
    String sessionIdHash,
    String subject,
    String tenantId,
    Instant createdAt,
    Instant expiresAt,
    Instant lastSeenAt,
    boolean revoked
) {}
```

Cookie attributes:

```text
Set-Cookie: SESSION=...; HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Lax; Path=/; Max-Age=1800
```

OWASP recommends protecting session IDs with cookie attributes such as `HttpOnly`, which prevents JavaScript access to the cookie and helps reduce session theft through XSS. ([OWASP Cheat Sheet Series](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Session_Management_Cheat_Sheet.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 18. Session Cookie Rules

```text
HttpOnly = JS cannot read cookie
Secure = only send over HTTPS
SameSite = reduce CSRF risk
Path = limit cookie path
Max-Age / Expires = session lifetime
```

Recommended baseline:

```text
HttpOnly
Secure
SameSite=Lax or Strict
short idle timeout
absolute lifetime
server-side revoke
session rotation after login
```

For high-risk apps:

```text
rotate session after privilege escalation
re-authenticate for sensitive actions
bind session to risk signals carefully
support global logout
audit session creation/revocation
```

---

# 19. CSRF Protection

Needed when browser automatically sends credential, usually cookie.

```text
Cookie session + state-changing request
= CSRF risk
```

Mitigations:

```text
SameSite cookie
CSRF token
Origin/Referer validation
custom header for AJAX
no state change on GET
```

CSRF token model:

```java
public record CsrfToken(
    String sessionId,
    String tokenHash,
    Instant expiresAt
) {}
```

Rule:

```text
Bearer token in Authorization header is usually less CSRF-prone than cookie session,
but XSS can still steal token if stored badly.
```

---

# 20. Password Storage

Never store plaintext passwords and never use plain SHA-256/MD5 for passwords. OWASP recommends strong, slow password hashing algorithms such as Argon2id, bcrypt, or PBKDF2 with a unique salt; fast hashes such as SHA-256 are unsuitable for password storage because attackers can guess quickly offline. ([OWASP Cheat Sheet Series](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Password_Storage_Cheat_Sheet.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Preferred:

```text
Argon2id
bcrypt
PBKDF2 if Java SE / compliance constraints
```

Java standard library supports PBKDF2 via `SecretKeyFactory`.

---

# 21. PBKDF2 Password Hash

```java
public record PasswordHash(
    String algorithm,
    int iterations,
    String saltBase64,
    String hashBase64
) {}
```

Hash:

```java
public static PasswordHash hashPassword(char[] password)
        throws GeneralSecurityException {
    String algorithm = "PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256";
    int iterations = 310_000;
    int saltBytes = 16;
    int keyLengthBits = 256;

    byte[] salt = new byte[saltBytes];
    new SecureRandom().nextBytes(salt);

    PBEKeySpec spec = new PBEKeySpec(
        password,
        salt,
        iterations,
        keyLengthBits
    );

    SecretKeyFactory factory =
        SecretKeyFactory.getInstance(algorithm);

    byte[] hash = factory.generateSecret(spec).getEncoded();

    spec.clearPassword();

    return new PasswordHash(
        algorithm,
        iterations,
        Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(salt),
        Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(hash)
    );
}
```

Verify:

```java
public static boolean verifyPassword(
    char[] password,
    PasswordHash stored
) throws GeneralSecurityException {
    byte[] salt = Base64.getDecoder().decode(stored.saltBase64());
    byte[] expected = Base64.getDecoder().decode(stored.hashBase64());

    PBEKeySpec spec = new PBEKeySpec(
        password,
        salt,
        stored.iterations(),
        expected.length * 8
    );

    SecretKeyFactory factory =
        SecretKeyFactory.getInstance(stored.algorithm());

    byte[] actual = factory.generateSecret(spec).getEncoded();

    spec.clearPassword();

    return MessageDigest.isEqual(expected, actual);
}
```

Store:

```text
algorithm
parameters
salt
hash
created_at
password_changed_at
```

Do not store:

```text
password
old password
password hint
recoverable encrypted password
```

---

# 22. Password Policy

Good modern baseline:

```text
minimum length
block known breached passwords
allow password managers
allow long passphrases
do not require weird composition rules blindly
do not force periodic password change without reason
rate limit login attempts
MFA for sensitive accounts
```

NIST SP 800-63B is the U.S. Digital Identity Guidelines volume for authentication and authenticator management; its modern guidance emphasizes authenticator strength and moving away from outdated password practices such as arbitrary composition and unnecessary rotation. ([NIST Publications](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/SpecialPublications/NIST.SP.800-63B-4.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 23. Login Flow

```text
1. Receive username/password.
2. Normalize username.
3. Find account by username/email.
4. If missing, use generic error.
5. Verify password hash.
6. Check disabled/locked status.
7. Check MFA requirement.
8. Create session or token.
9. Audit login success/failure.
10. Return response.
```

Generic error:

```text
Invalid username or password.
```

Avoid:

```text
User does not exist.
Password is wrong.
Account exists but disabled.
```

This reduces account enumeration.

---

# 24. Login Attempt Throttling

```java
public interface LoginRateLimiter {
    boolean allowAttempt(String username, String ipAddress);

    void recordFailure(String username, String ipAddress);

    void recordSuccess(String username, String ipAddress);
}
```

Policy examples:

```text
per username
per IP
per username + IP
progressive delay
temporary lock
MFA step-up
captcha only as supporting control
```

Rule:

```text
Do not let attacker brute-force one account or enumerate many accounts cheaply.
```

---

# 25. Account Locking

```java
public record AccountSecurityState(
    int failedLoginCount,
    Instant lockedUntil,
    Instant lastFailedLoginAt,
    Instant lastSuccessfulLoginAt
) {
    public boolean locked(Clock clock) {
        return lockedUntil != null
            && lockedUntil.isAfter(clock.instant());
    }
}
```

Careful:

```text
Hard lockout can be abused for denial-of-service.
Prefer progressive delay or risk-based step-up where possible.
```

---

# 26. MFA / Step-Up Authentication

MFA factors:

```text
something you know      = password/PIN
something you have      = TOTP app, hardware key, passkey
something you are       = biometric
```

Better options:

```text
WebAuthn/passkeys
hardware security key
TOTP
push with number matching
```

Weaker:

```text
SMS OTP
email OTP
security questions
```

Step-up actions:

```text
change password
change MFA settings
approve payment/refund
export sensitive data
create admin user
change tenant settings
```

---

# 27. Password Reset Flow

```text
1. User submits email.
2. Always respond generically.
3. Generate high-entropy one-time token.
4. Store hash of token, not raw token.
5. Send reset link.
6. Token expires quickly.
7. Verify token hash.
8. Let user set new password.
9. Invalidate old sessions if policy requires.
10. Audit event.
```

Token generation:

```java
public static String randomUrlToken(int bytes) {
    byte[] value = new byte[bytes];
    new SecureRandom().nextBytes(value);

    return Base64.getUrlEncoder()
        .withoutPadding()
        .encodeToString(value);
}
```

Store hash:

```java
public static byte[] sha256(byte[] input)
        throws NoSuchAlgorithmException {
    return MessageDigest.getInstance("SHA-256")
        .digest(input);
}
```

---

# 28. API Key Auth

API keys are bearer secrets. Treat them like passwords.

```java
public record ApiKeyRecord(
    String keyId,
    String keyHashBase64,
    String clientName,
    Set<String> scopes,
    Instant createdAt,
    Instant expiresAt,
    boolean revoked
) {}
```

Generate:

```java
public record GeneratedApiKey(
    String keyId,
    String rawKey,
    String keyHashBase64
) {}

public static GeneratedApiKey generateApiKey()
        throws NoSuchAlgorithmException {
    String keyId = UUID.randomUUID().toString();

    byte[] secret = new byte[32];
    new SecureRandom().nextBytes(secret);

    String rawKey = "ak_" + keyId + "_"
        + Base64.getUrlEncoder()
            .withoutPadding()
            .encodeToString(secret);

    byte[] hash = MessageDigest.getInstance("SHA-256")
        .digest(rawKey.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));

    return new GeneratedApiKey(
        keyId,
        rawKey,
        Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(hash)
    );
}
```

Verify:

```java
public static boolean verifyApiKey(
    String rawKey,
    ApiKeyRecord record
) throws NoSuchAlgorithmException {
    byte[] actual = MessageDigest.getInstance("SHA-256")
        .digest(rawKey.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));

    byte[] expected = Base64.getDecoder()
        .decode(record.keyHashBase64());

    return MessageDigest.isEqual(expected, actual);
}
```

Rules:

```text
show raw API key only once
store only hash
support rotation
support expiration
support revoke
scope API key
rate limit API key
audit usage
```

---

# 29. Service-to-Service Auth

Options:

| Method | Use Case |
|---|---|
| mTLS | strong workload identity |
| OAuth2 client credentials | service-to-service API access |
| signed JWT assertion | enterprise federation |
| HMAC request signing | webhook/B2B |
| API key | simple integration |
| service mesh identity | Kubernetes/microservices |

Client credentials concept:

```text
service authenticates to authorization server
authorization server issues access token
service calls resource API with access token
resource API validates token and scope
```

Do not use user password flow for service-to-service. OAuth security best current practice deprecates or discourages weaker modes and updates the OAuth 2.0 threat model based on modern deployments. ([IETF Datatracker](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc9700?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 30. HMAC Request Signing

Use for webhook/B2B when both parties share a secret.

Canonical string:

```java
public static String canonicalRequest(
    String method,
    URI uri,
    String timestamp,
    String nonce,
    byte[] body
) throws GeneralSecurityException {
    byte[] bodyHash = MessageDigest.getInstance("SHA-256")
        .digest(body);

    return String.join(
        "\n",
        method.toUpperCase(Locale.ROOT),
        uri.getPath(),
        uri.getRawQuery() == null ? "" : uri.getRawQuery(),
        timestamp,
        nonce,
        HexFormat.of().formatHex(bodyHash)
    );
}
```

Sign:

```java
public static String hmacSha256Base64(
    byte[] secret,
    String message
) throws GeneralSecurityException {
    Mac mac = Mac.getInstance("HmacSHA256");

    mac.init(new SecretKeySpec(secret, "HmacSHA256"));

    byte[] tag = mac.doFinal(
        message.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8)
    );

    return Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(tag);
}
```

Replay protection:

```text
timestamp
nonce
short window
nonce cache
constant-time signature comparison
```

---

# 31. Authorization Enforcement Locations

```text
API Gateway
- coarse-grained auth
- route-level scope check
- rate limit

JAX-RS Filter
- authentication
- generic permission check

Resource Method
- endpoint-specific permission

Application Service
- domain authorization
- tenant/resource ownership
- workflow-state rules

Repository/Query Layer
- tenant filtering
- row-level data isolation
```

Rule:

```text
Do not rely only on UI hiding buttons.
Do not rely only on gateway.
Authorization must be enforced server-side near the protected action.
```

---

# 32. Domain Authorization Example

```java
public final class OrderAuthorization {
    public boolean canApprove(
        AuthenticatedPrincipal principal,
        Order order
    ) {
        if (!principal.hasPermission("order:approve")) {
            return false;
        }

        if (!Objects.equals(principal.tenantId(), order.tenantId())) {
            return false;
        }

        if (order.status() != OrderStatus.SUBMITTED) {
            return false;
        }

        if (Objects.equals(principal.subject(), order.createdBy())) {
            return false;
        }

        return true;
    }
}
```

Usage:

```java
if (!orderAuthorization.canApprove(principal, order)) {
    throw new ForbiddenException("ORDER_APPROVAL_NOT_ALLOWED");
}

order.approve(principal.subject(), clock.instant());
```

This is the key difference between permission check and business authorization.

---

# 33. Tenant Isolation

Bad:

```java
SELECT * FROM orders WHERE id = ?
```

Better:

```java
SELECT * FROM orders WHERE tenant_id = ? AND id = ?
```

Java repository:

```java
public Optional<Order> findByTenantAndId(
    String tenantId,
    String orderId
) {
    // SELECT ... WHERE tenant_id = ? AND id = ?
}
```

Rule:

```text
tenantId should come from trusted principal/context,
not from user-controlled request body.
```

Anti-pattern:

```java
String tenantId = request.tenantId(); // user-controlled
```

Better:

```java
String tenantId = principal.tenantId();
```

---

# 34. Object-Level Authorization

Common vulnerability:

```text
IDOR / Broken Object Level Authorization
```

Bad:

```java
GET /orders/123
```

Only checks user logged in.

Better:

```text
GET /orders/123
- user authenticated
- tenant matches
- user has order:read
- user can access this specific order
```

Pattern:

```java
Order order = orderRepository
    .findByTenantAndId(principal.tenantId(), orderId)
    .orElseThrow(NotFoundException::new);

if (!orderAuthorization.canRead(principal, order)) {
    throw new ForbiddenException("ORDER_READ_NOT_ALLOWED");
}
```

Return `404` vs `403` depends on information disclosure policy. For cross-tenant resources, many systems return `404`.

---

# 35. Policy Decision Point Pattern

```text
PEP = Policy Enforcement Point
PDP = Policy Decision Point
PIP = Policy Information Point
PAP = Policy Administration Point
```

In Java:

```java
public interface PolicyDecisionPoint {
    AuthorizationResult decide(AuthorizationRequest request);
}
```

Enforcement:

```java
AuthorizationResult result = pdp.decide(request);

if (!result.allowed()) {
    throw new ForbiddenException(result.reasonCode());
}
```

Use when authorization logic grows beyond simple role checks.

---

# 36. Database Schema Sketch

Users:

```sql
CREATE TABLE app_user (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    tenant_id UUID NOT NULL,
    username TEXT NOT NULL,
    email TEXT NOT NULL,
    password_hash TEXT,
    status TEXT NOT NULL,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,
    password_changed_at TIMESTAMPTZ
);
```

Roles:

```sql
CREATE TABLE role (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    tenant_id UUID,
    name TEXT NOT NULL
);
```

Permissions:

```sql
CREATE TABLE permission (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    name TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE
);
```

Role permissions:

```sql
CREATE TABLE role_permission (
    role_id UUID NOT NULL,
    permission_id UUID NOT NULL,
    PRIMARY KEY (role_id, permission_id)
);
```

User roles:

```sql
CREATE TABLE user_role (
    user_id UUID NOT NULL,
    role_id UUID NOT NULL,
    PRIMARY KEY (user_id, role_id)
);
```

Sessions:

```sql
CREATE TABLE user_session (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    session_hash TEXT NOT NULL,
    user_id UUID NOT NULL,
    tenant_id UUID NOT NULL,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,
    expires_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,
    revoked_at TIMESTAMPTZ
);
```

Audit:

```sql
CREATE TABLE security_audit_event (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    tenant_id UUID,
    subject TEXT,
    event_type TEXT NOT NULL,
    outcome TEXT NOT NULL,
    ip_address TEXT,
    user_agent TEXT,
    occurred_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,
    metadata JSONB
);
```

---

# 37. Audit Events

Audit these:

```text
login success
login failure
logout
password change
password reset requested
password reset completed
MFA enabled/disabled
token refresh
token revoked
role assigned/removed
permission changed
access denied
admin action
sensitive export
cross-tenant access attempt
API key created/revoked
```

Audit record:

```java
public record SecurityAuditEvent(
    UUID id,
    String tenantId,
    String subject,
    String eventType,
    String outcome,
    String ipAddress,
    String userAgent,
    Instant occurredAt,
    Map<String, Object> metadata
) {}
```

Do not audit-log secrets:

```text
password
raw token
raw API key
Authorization header
Cookie
private key
```

---

# 38. Token Lifetime

| Token | Typical Direction |
|---|---|
| Access token | short-lived |
| Refresh token | longer-lived, protected |
| ID token | short-lived |
| Password reset token | very short-lived |
| Email verification token | short-lived |
| API key | expiry + rotation policy |
| Session | idle + absolute timeout |

Practical rule:

```text
Access token should be short-lived.
Refresh token should be revocable, rotated, and stored securely.
```

OAuth 2.1 guidance says refresh tokens for public clients should either be sender-constrained or one-time use, reflecting modern hardening against token replay and theft. ([OAuth Community Site](https://oauth.net/2.1/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 39. Refresh Token Rotation

```text
1. Client sends refresh token.
2. Server validates token.
3. Server invalidates old refresh token.
4. Server issues new access token.
5. Server issues new refresh token.
6. Reuse of old refresh token indicates possible theft.
```

Schema:

```sql
CREATE TABLE refresh_token (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    token_hash TEXT NOT NULL,
    user_id UUID NOT NULL,
    client_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    issued_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,
    expires_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,
    revoked_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    replaced_by UUID
);
```

---

# 40. Authorization Error Mapping

| Condition | HTTP |
|---|---:|
| Missing token | 401 |
| Invalid token | 401 |
| Expired token | 401 |
| Valid token, missing permission | 403 |
| Valid token, wrong tenant | 404 or 403 |
| Account disabled | 403 or 401 depending policy |
| MFA required | 403 / 401 with challenge |
| Rate limited login | 429 |
| Locked account | 423 or generic 401/403 depending policy |

Response body:

```json
{
  "code": "FORBIDDEN",
  "message": "Access denied"
}
```

Avoid leaking:

```text
user exists
permission exact internal details
tenant exists
resource exists across tenant
```

---

# 41. Security Context Propagation

In synchronous code:

```java
public final class SecurityContext {
    private final AuthenticatedPrincipal principal;

    public SecurityContext(AuthenticatedPrincipal principal) {
        this.principal = principal;
    }

    public AuthenticatedPrincipal principal() {
        return principal;
    }
}
```

Pass explicitly:

```java
orderService.approveOrder(securityContext, orderId);
```

Avoid relying on `ThreadLocal` across async boundaries unless context propagation is explicit.

```text
ThreadLocal can break with executors, async flows, virtual threads, and callbacks.
Explicit context is clearer for service methods.
```

---

# 42. Camunda / Workflow Authorization

For workflow actions:

```text
who can start process?
who can claim task?
who can complete task?
who can cancel process?
who can retry failed job?
who can view process variables?
```

Pattern:

```java
public boolean canCompleteTask(
    AuthenticatedPrincipal principal,
    WorkflowTask task
) {
    if (!Objects.equals(principal.tenantId(), task.tenantId())) {
        return false;
    }

    if (task.assignee() != null
        && !Objects.equals(task.assignee(), principal.subject())) {
        return false;
    }

    return principal.hasPermission("workflow:task:complete");
}
```

Workflow variables may contain sensitive data. Do not expose them just because user can see task metadata.

---

# 43. Admin Privilege Pattern

Avoid one global `ADMIN` role for everything.

Better:

```text
user:manage
role:assign
billing:refund
order:force-cancel
workflow:incident:resolve
tenant:settings:update
audit:read
```

For dangerous admin actions:

```text
require MFA
require recent authentication
require maker-checker approval
audit with reason
limit by tenant/environment
```

---

# 44. Maker-Checker / Four-Eyes Authorization

Use for high-risk actions.

```text
Maker creates request.
Checker approves.
Checker must not equal maker.
Action executes after approval.
```

Example:

```java
public boolean canApproveAdminChange(
    AuthenticatedPrincipal principal,
    AdminChangeRequest request
) {
    if (!principal.hasPermission("admin-change:approve")) {
        return false;
    }

    if (Objects.equals(principal.subject(), request.createdBy())) {
        return false;
    }

    if (!Objects.equals(principal.tenantId(), request.tenantId())) {
        return false;
    }

    return request.status() == ChangeStatus.PENDING_APPROVAL;
}
```

---

# 45. Caching Permissions

Permissions can be cached, but invalidation matters.

Options:

```text
token contains permissions
server-side cache by user ID
cache by role version
cache with short TTL
event-based invalidation
```

Risk:

```text
User role revoked, but old token/cache still allows access.
```

Mitigations:

```text
short access token lifetime
role version claim
session/token revocation list
permission cache TTL
force re-auth for sensitive actions
```

---

# 46. Role Version Claim

```json
{
  "sub": "user-123",
  "tenant_id": "tenant-a",
  "roles": ["ORDER_MANAGER"],
  "role_version": 42,
  "exp": 1783900000
}
```

Server can compare token `role_version` with current value:

```java
if (tokenRoleVersion < currentRoleVersion) {
    throw new InvalidTokenException("Stale authorization context");
}
```

This helps invalidate old tokens after role changes.

---

# 47. Secrets and Config

Never hardcode:

```java
private static final String JWT_SECRET = "secret";
```

Better:

```java
String issuer = System.getenv("AUTH_ISSUER");
String jwksUri = System.getenv("AUTH_JWKS_URI");
```

For secrets:

```text
secret manager
KMS
Vault
Kubernetes Secret with RBAC
cloud key vault
HSM for high-value keys
```

---

# 48. JWKS / Signing Key Rotation

JWT public-key rotation pattern:

```text
1. Authorization server signs token with private key.
2. Token header has kid.
3. Resource server fetches JWKS.
4. Resource server selects key by kid.
5. Validate signature.
6. Cache JWKS with TTL.
7. Support old and new keys during rotation.
```

Do:

```text
cache JWKS
honor cache headers carefully
refresh on unknown kid
allowlist issuer/audience/algorithms
monitor key rotation failures
```

Do not:

```text
fetch JWKS for every request
trust arbitrary jku/x5u from token header
accept unknown algorithm
accept token from wrong issuer
```

---

# 49. API Gateway vs Service Auth

Gateway can do:

```text
TLS termination
JWT validation
coarse scope check
rate limit
IP allowlist
request size limit
routing
```

Service must still do:

```text
domain authorization
tenant isolation
object-level access
workflow-state rule
audit
defense-in-depth validation
```

Rule:

```text
Gateway protects the perimeter.
Service protects the domain.
```

---

# 50. Common Anti-Patterns

| Anti-pattern | Risk |
|---|---|
| Trust JWT payload without signature validation | account takeover |
| Use ID token as API access token | confused deputy |
| Store access token in localStorage | XSS token theft |
| Put token/API key in URL | logs/proxy/browser leak |
| Plain SHA-256 password hash | fast offline cracking |
| No rate limit on login | brute force |
| No object-level auth | IDOR |
| Only hide UI buttons | server-side bypass |
| Global `ADMIN` role everywhere | privilege overreach |
| Authorization only in gateway | domain bypass |
| Missing tenant filter | cross-tenant data leak |
| Long-lived access token | large blast radius |
| Refresh token not rotated | replay risk |
| No audit for role changes | poor incident response |
| Log Authorization header | credential leak |
| Disable TLS verification | MITM |
| Wildcard CORS with credentials | browser security issue |

---

# 51. Production Checklist

```text
Authentication
[ ] Passwords hashed with Argon2id/bcrypt/PBKDF2?
[ ] Unique salt per password?
[ ] Login throttling exists?
[ ] Generic login error?
[ ] MFA supported for sensitive users/actions?
[ ] Password reset token hashed and expires?
[ ] Account lifecycle handled?

Token / Session
[ ] Access token short-lived?
[ ] Refresh token rotated/revocable?
[ ] JWT signature and claims validated?
[ ] Issuer/audience checked?
[ ] Algorithm allowlist exists?
[ ] Cookies use HttpOnly/Secure/SameSite?
[ ] CSRF protection for cookie session?

Authorization
[ ] Default deny?
[ ] Permissions are explicit?
[ ] Object-level authorization enforced?
[ ] Tenant isolation enforced in queries?
[ ] Admin actions separated by permission?
[ ] Domain workflow state checked?
[ ] Maker-checker for risky operations?

Service Security
[ ] mTLS/OAuth/API key strategy clear?
[ ] Secrets not hardcoded?
[ ] Token/API key not logged?
[ ] Rate limits and bulkheads exist?
[ ] Audit events captured?
[ ] Revocation/role-change strategy exists?

Operations
[ ] Signing key rotation tested?
[ ] JWKS caching strategy exists?
[ ] Security audit logs monitored?
[ ] Access-denied spikes monitored?
[ ] Break-glass admin controlled?
[ ] Incident response can revoke sessions/tokens/keys?
```

---

# 52. Compact Summary

```text
Authentication
- prove identity
- password, MFA, OIDC, session, token, mTLS, API key

Authorization
- decide allowed action
- RBAC, ABAC, policy, object-level rules

Principal
- authenticated identity in app
- subject, tenant, roles, permissions

Password
- never plaintext
- never plain SHA-256
- Argon2id/bcrypt/PBKDF2
- unique salt

JWT
- decode is not validate
- validate signature, issuer, audience, exp, nbf, alg, kid
- do not use ID token as API access token

Session
- server-side control
- HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite
- CSRF protection
- rotate after login

API Key
- bearer secret
- store hash only
- rotate, scope, expire, audit

OAuth/OIDC
- OIDC for identity
- OAuth access token for API authorization
- PKCE for authorization code clients
- avoid implicit/password grants

Authorization Rules
- default deny
- check permission
- check tenant
- check resource ownership
- check domain state
- audit sensitive decisions

Enterprise Rule
- gateway can help,
  but service must enforce domain authorization.
```

Kesimpulan praktis: **authentication membuat sistem tahu siapa caller-nya; authorization memastikan caller hanya bisa melakukan hal yang benar pada resource yang benar.** Untuk Java enterprise, desain yang aman biasanya menggabungkan **OIDC/OAuth atau session**, **password hashing yang benar**, **token/session lifecycle**, **RBAC + ABAC/domain policy**, **tenant isolation**, **object-level authorization**, dan **audit yang kuat**.

---

**Sources:**

- [Authentication vs. Authorization - Auth0 Docs](https://auth0.com/docs/get-started/identity-fundamentals/authentication-and-authorization?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- [OpenID Connect Core 1.0 incorporating errata set 2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-connect-core-1_0.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- [OAuth 2.1](https://oauth.net/2.1/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- [JSON Web Token for Java](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/JSON_Web_Token_for_Java_Cheat_Sheet.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- [NIST.SP.800-63B-4.pdf](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/SpecialPublications/NIST.SP.800-63B-4.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- [RFC 9700 - Best Current Practice for OAuth 2.0 Security](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc9700?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

## Response:
7/12/2026, 9:53:20 PM

Berikut **cheat sheet Java XML Processing (JAXP)** untuk **Java 17+**.

Fokusnya: **JAXP, DOM, SAX, StAX, XPath, XSLT, XSD validation, secure XML processing, XXE prevention, namespace, performance, dan production patterns**.

# Java XML Processing JAXP Cheat Sheet

---

# 1. Mental Model

```text
Java XML Processing
├── JAXP
│   ├── common factory abstraction
│   ├── provider-based XML processing
│   └── security properties
│
├── DOM
│   ├── DocumentBuilderFactory
│   ├── DocumentBuilder
│   └── Document
│
├── SAX
│   ├── SAXParserFactory
│   ├── SAXParser
│   └── DefaultHandler
│
├── StAX
│   ├── XMLInputFactory
│   ├── XMLOutputFactory
│   ├── XMLStreamReader
│   └── XMLStreamWriter
│
├── XPath
│   ├── XPathFactory
│   └── XPath
│
├── XSLT
│   ├── TransformerFactory
│   ├── Transformer
│   └── Templates
│
└── XSD Validation
    ├── SchemaFactory
    ├── Schema
    └── Validator
```

Module `java.xml` mendefinisikan Java APIs for XML Processing, termasuk JAXP, StAX, SAX, dan W3C DOM API. ([Java Downloads](https://download.java.net/java/early_access/genzgc/docs/api/java.xml/module-summary.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 2. JAXP API Map

| Need | API |
|---|---|
| Parse small/medium XML into tree | DOM |
| Parse large XML streaming/event-based | SAX |
| Pull-based streaming parse | StAX |
| Write XML streaming | StAX |
| Query XML tree | XPath |
| Transform XML | XSLT |
| Validate XML with XSD | SchemaFactory + Validator |
| Create XML document | DOM or StAX |
| Secure parsing | factory features/properties |
| Prevent XXE | disable DTD/external entities/external access |

---

# 3. DOM vs SAX vs StAX

| API | Model | Best For |
|---|---|---|
| DOM | loads whole XML tree | small/medium XML, random access, modification |
| SAX | push parser callback | large XML, low memory, sequential parsing |
| StAX | pull parser | large XML, controlled streaming, easier than SAX |
| XPath | query tree | extracting values from DOM |
| XSLT | transform XML | XML-to-XML/HTML/text |
| XSD | schema validation | contract validation |

Rule praktis:

```text
Small XML + random access -> DOM
Large XML + stream processing -> StAX
Event callback pipeline -> SAX
Need query expressions -> XPath on DOM
Need transformation -> XSLT
Need contract validation -> XSD
```

---

# 4. Security First: XML Is Dangerous by Default

XML processing risk:

```text
XXE
SSRF via external entity
local file disclosure
Billion Laughs / entity expansion DoS
quadratic blowup
external DTD fetch
external schema fetch
external stylesheet fetch
XPath injection
XSLT extension function abuse
zip bomb if XML is compressed
huge XML memory exhaustion
```

Oracle’s JAXP Security Guide explains secure processing features and external access restrictions for JAXP, including external access properties and processing limits. ([Oracle Docs](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/17/security/java-api-xml-processing-jaxp-security-guide.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

OWASP specifically warns that Java XML parsers are commonly vulnerable to XXE unless external entity processing is explicitly disabled. ([OWASP Cheat Sheet Series](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/XML_External_Entity_Prevention_Cheat_Sheet.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 5. Secure Processing Baseline

Use this mental baseline:

```text
For untrusted XML:
1. Disable DOCTYPE if possible.
2. Disable external general entities.
3. Disable external parameter entities.
4. Disable external DTD loading.
5. Enable secure processing.
6. Restrict external DTD/schema/stylesheet access.
7. Set size/depth/entity limits where applicable.
8. Avoid resolving remote resources.
9. Never parse unbounded input into memory.
```

JEP 185 introduced standard JAXP external access restrictions because secure processing limits alone did not prevent fetching external resources. ([OpenJDK](https://openjdk.org/jeps/185?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 6. Secure DOM Parser

```java
public final class XmlSecurity {
    private XmlSecurity() {}

    public static DocumentBuilderFactory secureDocumentBuilderFactory()
            throws ParserConfigurationException {
        DocumentBuilderFactory factory = DocumentBuilderFactory.newInstance();

        factory.setNamespaceAware(true);
        factory.setXIncludeAware(false);
        factory.setExpandEntityReferences(false);

        factory.setFeature(XMLConstants.FEATURE_SECURE_PROCESSING, true);

        // Disable DOCTYPE declaration
        factory.setFeature(
            "http://apache.org/xml/features/disallow-doctype-decl",
            true
        );

        // Disable external entities
        factory.setFeature(
            "http://xml.org/sax/features/external-general-entities",
            false
        );
        factory.setFeature(
            "http://xml.org/sax/features/external-parameter-entities",
            false
        );

        // Disable external DTD loading
        factory.setFeature(
            "http://apache.org/xml/features/nonvalidating/load-external-dtd",
            false
        );

        // Restrict external access
        factory.setAttribute(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_DTD, "");
        factory.setAttribute(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_SCHEMA, "");

        return factory;
    }
}
```

Parse:

```java
public static Document parseSecureDom(InputStream input)
        throws Exception {
    DocumentBuilderFactory factory =
        XmlSecurity.secureDocumentBuilderFactory();

    DocumentBuilder builder = factory.newDocumentBuilder();

    builder.setErrorHandler(new StrictXmlErrorHandler());

    return builder.parse(input);
}
```

---

# 7. XMLConstants Security Properties

Common properties:

| Property | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `XMLConstants.FEATURE_SECURE_PROCESSING` | enables secure processing behavior |
| `XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_DTD` | restricts external DTD access |
| `XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_SCHEMA` | restricts external schema access |
| `XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_STYLESHEET` | restricts external XSLT stylesheet access |

Common values:

| Value | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `""` | allow no external access |
| `"file"` | allow file protocol |
| `"http"` | allow HTTP |
| `"file,http"` | allow file and HTTP |
| `"all"` | allow all, avoid for untrusted input |

Rule:

```text
For untrusted XML, use empty string for external access properties.
```

Oracle’s JAXP security guide separates external access restrictions from processing limits; both matter for secure XML processing. ([Oracle Docs](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/24/security/java-api-xml-processing-jaxp-security-guide.html?embed=1&utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 8. DOM Parsing

## 8.1 Parse XML String

```java
String xml = """
    <order id="O-123">
      <customer>Fajar</customer>
      <total>100.50</total>
    </order>
    """;

Document document = parseSecureDom(
    new ByteArrayInputStream(xml.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8))
);
```

## 8.2 Parse XML File

```java
try (InputStream input = Files.newInputStream(Path.of("order.xml"))) {
    Document document = parseSecureDom(input);
}
```

## 8.3 Normalize DOM

```java
document.getDocumentElement().normalize();
```

---

# 9. DOM Reading

```java
Element root = document.getDocumentElement();

String rootName = root.getTagName();

String orderId = root.getAttribute("id");
```

Get child elements:

```java
NodeList customers = root.getElementsByTagName("customer");

if (customers.getLength() > 0) {
    String customer = customers.item(0).getTextContent();
}
```

Helper:

```java
public static Optional<String> firstText(
    Element parent,
    String tagName
) {
    NodeList nodes = parent.getElementsByTagName(tagName);

    if (nodes.getLength() == 0) {
        return Optional.empty();
    }

    return Optional.ofNullable(nodes.item(0).getTextContent());
}
```

---

# 10. DOM Gotcha: `getElementsByTagName`

`getElementsByTagName()` searches descendants, not only direct children.

Example:

```xml
<order>
  <customer>
    <name>Fajar</name>
  </customer>
  <name>Order Name</name>
</order>
```

```java
root.getElementsByTagName("name");
```

This may return both nested and direct `name`.

Direct child helper:

```java
public static Optional<Element> directChild(
    Element parent,
    String tagName
) {
    NodeList children = parent.getChildNodes();

    for (int i = 0; i < children.getLength(); i++) {
        Node node = children.item(i);

        if (node instanceof Element element
                && element.getTagName().equals(tagName)) {
            return Optional.of(element);
        }
    }

    return Optional.empty();
}
```

---

# 11. DOM Writing

```java
DocumentBuilderFactory factory = DocumentBuilderFactory.newInstance();

DocumentBuilder builder = factory.newDocumentBuilder();

Document document = builder.newDocument();

Element order = document.createElement("order");
order.setAttribute("id", "O-123");
document.appendChild(order);

Element customer = document.createElement("customer");
customer.setTextContent("Fajar");
order.appendChild(customer);
```

Write to output:

```java
TransformerFactory transformerFactory = TransformerFactory.newInstance();
transformerFactory.setFeature(XMLConstants.FEATURE_SECURE_PROCESSING, true);
transformerFactory.setAttribute(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_DTD, "");
transformerFactory.setAttribute(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_STYLESHEET, "");

Transformer transformer = transformerFactory.newTransformer();
transformer.setOutputProperty(OutputKeys.INDENT, "yes");
transformer.setOutputProperty(OutputKeys.ENCODING, "UTF-8");

transformer.transform(
    new DOMSource(document),
    new StreamResult(outputStream)
);
```

---

# 12. SAX Parsing

SAX is event-based.

```java
public final class OrderSaxHandler extends DefaultHandler {
    private String currentElement;
    private String orderId;
    private String customer;

    @Override
    public void startElement(
        String uri,
        String localName,
        String qName,
        Attributes attributes
    ) {
        currentElement = qName;

        if ("order".equals(qName)) {
            orderId = attributes.getValue("id");
        }
    }

    @Override
    public void characters(
        char[] ch,
        int start,
        int length
    ) {
        String text = new String(ch, start, length).trim();

        if (text.isEmpty()) {
            return;
        }

        if ("customer".equals(currentElement)) {
            customer = text;
        }
    }

    public String orderId() {
        return orderId;
    }

    public String customer() {
        return customer;
    }
}
```

Parse:

```java
SAXParserFactory factory = secureSaxParserFactory();

SAXParser parser = factory.newSAXParser();

OrderSaxHandler handler = new OrderSaxHandler();

parser.parse(inputStream, handler);
```

---

# 13. Secure SAX Parser

```java
public static SAXParserFactory secureSaxParserFactory()
        throws ParserConfigurationException, SAXNotRecognizedException,
               SAXNotSupportedException {
    SAXParserFactory factory = SAXParserFactory.newInstance();

    factory.setNamespaceAware(true);
    factory.setXIncludeAware(false);

    factory.setFeature(XMLConstants.FEATURE_SECURE_PROCESSING, true);

    factory.setFeature(
        "http://apache.org/xml/features/disallow-doctype-decl",
        true
    );
    factory.setFeature(
        "http://xml.org/sax/features/external-general-entities",
        false
    );
    factory.setFeature(
        "http://xml.org/sax/features/external-parameter-entities",
        false
    );
    factory.setFeature(
        "http://apache.org/xml/features/nonvalidating/load-external-dtd",
        false
    );

    return factory;
}
```

OWASP’s XXE guidance lists disabling external entities and DOCTYPE processing for Java parser factories such as `DocumentBuilderFactory` and `SAXParserFactory`. ([OWASP Cheat Sheet Series](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/XML_External_Entity_Prevention_Cheat_Sheet.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 14. SAX Gotcha: `characters()` Can Be Called Multiple Times

Bad assumption:

```text
One XML text node = one characters() call
```

Wrong.

Correct pattern:

```java
public final class TextAccumulatingHandler extends DefaultHandler {
    private final StringBuilder text = new StringBuilder();
    private String customer;

    @Override
    public void startElement(
        String uri,
        String localName,
        String qName,
        Attributes attributes
    ) {
        text.setLength(0);
    }

    @Override
    public void characters(char[] ch, int start, int length) {
        text.append(ch, start, length);
    }

    @Override
    public void endElement(String uri, String localName, String qName) {
        if ("customer".equals(qName)) {
            customer = text.toString().trim();
        }
    }
}
```

Rule:

```text
In SAX, accumulate text between startElement and endElement.
```

---

# 15. StAX Parsing

StAX is pull-based streaming.

```java
public static XMLInputFactory secureXmlInputFactory() {
    XMLInputFactory factory = XMLInputFactory.newFactory();

    factory.setProperty(XMLInputFactory.SUPPORT_DTD, false);
    factory.setProperty("javax.xml.stream.isSupportingExternalEntities", false);
    factory.setProperty(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_DTD, "");
    factory.setProperty(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_SCHEMA, "");

    return factory;
}
```

Parse:

```java
XMLInputFactory factory = secureXmlInputFactory();

XMLStreamReader reader = factory.createXMLStreamReader(
    inputStream,
    StandardCharsets.UTF_8.name()
);

while (reader.hasNext()) {
    int event = reader.next();

    if (event == XMLStreamConstants.START_ELEMENT) {
        String name = reader.getLocalName();

        if ("order".equals(name)) {
            String id = reader.getAttributeValue(null, "id");
        }
    }

    if (event == XMLStreamConstants.CHARACTERS) {
        String text = reader.getText();
    }
}

reader.close();
```

Oracle’s older JAXP security guide notes that StAX does not support the secure-processing feature in the same way as other factories, so external access properties must be used for StAX. ([Oracle Docs](https://docs.oracle.com/javase/8/docs/technotes/guides/security/jaxp/jaxp.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 16. StAX Read Specific Elements

```java
public static List<String> readOrderIds(InputStream input)
        throws XMLStreamException {
    XMLInputFactory factory = secureXmlInputFactory();

    XMLStreamReader reader =
        factory.createXMLStreamReader(input, "UTF-8");

    List<String> ids = new ArrayList<>();

    try {
        while (reader.hasNext()) {
            int event = reader.next();

            if (event == XMLStreamConstants.START_ELEMENT
                    && "order".equals(reader.getLocalName())) {
                ids.add(reader.getAttributeValue(null, "id"));
            }
        }

        return ids;
    } finally {
        reader.close();
    }
}
```

Best for:

```text
large XML
partial extraction
streaming imports
message processing
low-memory parsing
```

---

# 17. StAX Writing

```java
XMLOutputFactory outputFactory = XMLOutputFactory.newFactory();

XMLStreamWriter writer = outputFactory.createXMLStreamWriter(
    outputStream,
    "UTF-8"
);

writer.writeStartDocument("UTF-8", "1.0");

writer.writeStartElement("order");
writer.writeAttribute("id", "O-123");

writer.writeStartElement("customer");
writer.writeCharacters("Fajar");
writer.writeEndElement();

writer.writeStartElement("total");
writer.writeCharacters("100.50");
writer.writeEndElement();

writer.writeEndElement();

writer.writeEndDocument();

writer.flush();
writer.close();
```

Output:

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<order id="O-123"><customer>Fajar</customer><total>100.50</total></order>
```

# 18. XPath

Use XPath for querying DOM.

```java
XPathFactory factory = XPathFactory.newInstance();

XPath xpath = factory.newXPath();

String customer = xpath.evaluate(
    "/order/customer/text()",
    document
);
```

Get node:

```java
Node node = (Node) xpath.evaluate(
    "/order/customer",
    document,
    XPathConstants.NODE
);
```

Get node list:

```java
NodeList nodes = (NodeList) xpath.evaluate(
    "/orders/order",
    document,
    XPathConstants.NODESET
);
```

---

# 19. XPath with Namespace

XML:

```xml
<ord:order xmlns:ord="urn:order">
  <ord:customer>Fajar</ord:customer>
</ord:order>
```

Namespace context:

```java
public final class MapNamespaceContext implements NamespaceContext {
    private final Map<String, String> namespaces;

    public MapNamespaceContext(Map<String, String> namespaces) {
        this.namespaces = Map.copyOf(namespaces);
    }

    @Override
    public String getNamespaceURI(String prefix) {
        return namespaces.getOrDefault(prefix, XMLConstants.NULL_NS_URI);
    }

    @Override
    public String getPrefix(String namespaceURI) {
        return namespaces.entrySet().stream()
            .filter(entry -> entry.getValue().equals(namespaceURI))
            .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
            .findFirst()
            .orElse(null);
    }

    @Override
    public Iterator<String> getPrefixes(String namespaceURI) {
        return namespaces.entrySet().stream()
            .filter(entry -> entry.getValue().equals(namespaceURI))
            .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
            .iterator();
    }
}
```

Use:

```java
XPath xpath = XPathFactory.newInstance().newXPath();

xpath.setNamespaceContext(
    new MapNamespaceContext(Map.of("ord", "urn:order"))
);

String customer = xpath.evaluate(
    "/ord:order/ord:customer/text()",
    document
);
```

Rule:

```text
XPath prefix is your code's prefix.
It does not have to match XML document prefix.
It must map to the same namespace URI.
```

---

# 20. XPath Injection

Bad:

```java
String expression = "/users/user[name='" + username + "']";
```

If `username` contains quote or XPath syntax, it can change query meaning.

Safer:

```java
NodeList users = (NodeList) xpath.evaluate(
    "/users/user",
    document,
    XPathConstants.NODESET
);

for (int i = 0; i < users.getLength(); i++) {
    Element user = (Element) users.item(i);

    String name = directChild(user, "name")
        .map(Element::getTextContent)
        .orElse("");

    if (username.equals(name)) {
        // matched safely in Java
    }
}
```

Rule:

```text
Do not build XPath expression by concatenating untrusted input.
Filter untrusted values in Java or use a library supporting variables.
```

---

# 21. XSLT Transformation

```java
TransformerFactory factory = TransformerFactory.newInstance();

factory.setFeature(XMLConstants.FEATURE_SECURE_PROCESSING, true);
factory.setAttribute(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_DTD, "");
factory.setAttribute(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_STYLESHEET, "");

Transformer transformer = factory.newTransformer(
    new StreamSource(xsltInputStream)
);

transformer.transform(
    new StreamSource(xmlInputStream),
    new StreamResult(outputStream)
);
```

Use case:

```text
XML to XML
XML to HTML
XML to text
legacy integration mapping
report generation
```

---

# 22. Compile XSLT Templates

`Transformer` is not ideal to share across threads. Compile reusable stylesheet into `Templates`.

```java
TransformerFactory factory = TransformerFactory.newInstance();

factory.setFeature(XMLConstants.FEATURE_SECURE_PROCESSING, true);
factory.setAttribute(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_DTD, "");
factory.setAttribute(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_STYLESHEET, "");

Templates templates = factory.newTemplates(
    new StreamSource(xsltInputStream)
);
```

Use per request:

```java
Transformer transformer = templates.newTransformer();

transformer.transform(
    new StreamSource(xmlInputStream),
    new StreamResult(outputStream)
);
```

Rule:

```text
Cache Templates.
Create new Transformer per transformation.
```

---

# 23. XSLT Security

Disable external access:

```java
factory.setAttribute(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_DTD, "");
factory.setAttribute(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_STYLESHEET, "");
```

Avoid:

```text
untrusted XSLT
XSLT extension functions
external document() access
remote stylesheet import/include
```

If users can upload XSLT, treat it as code-like capability, not as harmless text.

---

# 24. XSD Validation

## 24.1 Compile Schema

```java
public static Schema compileSchema(Source xsd)
        throws SAXException {
    SchemaFactory factory = SchemaFactory.newInstance(
        XMLConstants.W3C_XML_SCHEMA_NS_URI
    );

    factory.setFeature(XMLConstants.FEATURE_SECURE_PROCESSING, true);
    factory.setProperty(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_DTD, "");
    factory.setProperty(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_SCHEMA, "");

    return factory.newSchema(xsd);
}
```

## 24.2 Validate XML

```java
public static void validateXml(
    InputStream xml,
    Schema schema
) throws IOException, SAXException {
    Validator validator = schema.newValidator();

    validator.setProperty(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_DTD, "");
    validator.setProperty(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_SCHEMA, "");

    validator.setErrorHandler(new StrictXmlErrorHandler());

    validator.validate(new StreamSource(xml));
}
```

Use:

```java
Schema schema = compileSchema(
    new StreamSource(Files.newInputStream(Path.of("order.xsd")))
);

try (InputStream xml = Files.newInputStream(Path.of("order.xml"))) {
    validateXml(xml, schema);
}
```

---

# 25. Error Handler

```java
public final class StrictXmlErrorHandler implements ErrorHandler {
    @Override
    public void warning(SAXParseException exception)
            throws SAXException {
        throw exception;
    }

    @Override
    public void error(SAXParseException exception)
            throws SAXException {
        throw exception;
    }

    @Override
    public void fatalError(SAXParseException exception)
            throws SAXException {
        throw exception;
    }
}
```

Alternative: collect all validation errors.

```java
public final class CollectingErrorHandler implements ErrorHandler {
    private final List<SAXParseException> errors = new ArrayList<>();

    @Override
    public void warning(SAXParseException exception) {
        errors.add(exception);
    }

    @Override
    public void error(SAXParseException exception) {
        errors.add(exception);
    }

    @Override
    public void fatalError(SAXParseException exception) {
        errors.add(exception);
    }

    public List<SAXParseException> errors() {
        return List.copyOf(errors);
    }
}
```

---

# 26. EntityResolver

For trusted local DTD/schema resolution, use resolver instead of arbitrary network access.

```java
DocumentBuilder builder = factory.newDocumentBuilder();

builder.setEntityResolver((publicId, systemId) -> {
    if ("https://example.com/schema/order.dtd".equals(systemId)) {
        return new InputSource(
            Files.newInputStream(Path.of("schemas/order.dtd"))
        );
    }

    throw new SAXException("External entity not allowed: " + systemId);
});
```

Rule:

```text
Prefer local allowlisted resolver.
Do not allow arbitrary systemId resolution.
```

---

# 27. LSResourceResolver for XSD Imports

```java
public final class LocalSchemaResolver implements LSResourceResolver {
    private final Map<String, Path> allowedSchemas;

    public LocalSchemaResolver(Map<String, Path> allowedSchemas) {
        this.allowedSchemas = Map.copyOf(allowedSchemas);
    }

    @Override
    public LSInput resolveResource(
        String type,
        String namespaceURI,
        String publicId,
        String systemId,
        String baseURI
    ) {
        Path path = allowedSchemas.get(systemId);

        if (path == null) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException(
                "Schema import not allowed: " + systemId
            );
        }

        return new PathLsInput(publicId, systemId, path);
    }
}
```

Simplified `LSInput`:

```java
public final class PathLsInput implements LSInput {
    private final String publicId;
    private final String systemId;
    private final Path path;

    public PathLsInput(String publicId, String systemId, Path path) {
        this.publicId = publicId;
        this.systemId = systemId;
        this.path = path;
    }

    @Override
    public Reader getCharacterStream() {
        try {
            return Files.newBufferedReader(path, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
        } catch (IOException e) {
            throw new UncheckedIOException(e);
        }
    }

    @Override public void setCharacterStream(Reader characterStream) {}
    @Override public InputStream getByteStream() { return null; }
    @Override public void setByteStream(InputStream byteStream) {}
    @Override public String getStringData() { return null; }
    @Override public void setStringData(String stringData) {}
    @Override public String getSystemId() { return systemId; }
    @Override public void setSystemId(String systemId) {}
    @Override public String getPublicId() { return publicId; }
    @Override public void setPublicId(String publicId) {}
    @Override public String getBaseURI() { return null; }
    @Override public void setBaseURI(String baseURI) {}
    @Override public String getEncoding() { return "UTF-8"; }
    @Override public void setEncoding(String encoding) {}
    @Override public boolean getCertifiedText() { return false; }
    @Override public void setCertifiedText(boolean certifiedText) {}
}
```

Use:

```java
SchemaFactory factory = SchemaFactory.newInstance(
    XMLConstants.W3C_XML_SCHEMA_NS_URI
);

factory.setResourceResolver(
    new LocalSchemaResolver(Map.of("common.xsd", Path.of("schemas/common.xsd")))
);
```

---

# 28. Namespaces

XML:

```xml
<order xmlns="urn:order">
  <customer>Fajar</customer>
</order>
```

DOM namespace-aware:

```java
factory.setNamespaceAware(true);
```

Get root namespace:

```java
String namespace = document.getDocumentElement().getNamespaceURI();
```

Get elements by namespace:

```java
NodeList nodes = document.getElementsByTagNameNS(
    "urn:order",
    "customer"
);
```

Rule:

```text
For serious XML integration, turn namespace awareness on.
Do not rely only on tag names when namespaces exist.
```

---

# 29. Creating Namespaced XML

```java
Document document = builder.newDocument();

String ns = "urn:order";

Element order = document.createElementNS(ns, "ord:order");
document.appendChild(order);

Element customer = document.createElementNS(ns, "ord:customer");
customer.setTextContent("Fajar");
order.appendChild(customer);
```

---

# 30. XML Escaping

Do not manually concatenate unescaped XML.

Bad:

```java
String xml = "<name>" + userInput + "</name>";
```

If `userInput` contains `<`, `&`, etc., XML breaks.

Better with DOM:

```java
Element name = document.createElement("name");
name.setTextContent(userInput);
```

Better with StAX:

```java
writer.writeCharacters(userInput);
```

Rule:

```text
Use XML APIs to write text and attributes.
Do not manually build XML with string concatenation for untrusted data.
```

---

# 31. XML Declaration and Encoding

```java
transformer.setOutputProperty(OutputKeys.ENCODING, "UTF-8");
transformer.setOutputProperty(OutputKeys.OMIT_XML_DECLARATION, "no");
```

Output:

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
```

Rule:

```text
Keep bytes and declared encoding consistent.
Prefer UTF-8.
```

---

# 32. Pretty Print XML

```java
Transformer transformer = transformerFactory.newTransformer();

transformer.setOutputProperty(OutputKeys.INDENT, "yes");
transformer.setOutputProperty("{http://xml.apache.org/xslt}indent-amount", "2");
```

Note:

```text
Indent amount property is implementation-specific.
Good for diagnostics, not a data contract.
```

---

# 33. XML to String

```java
public static String documentToString(Document document)
        throws TransformerException {
    TransformerFactory factory = TransformerFactory.newInstance();

    factory.setFeature(XMLConstants.FEATURE_SECURE_PROCESSING, true);
    factory.setAttribute(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_DTD, "");
    factory.setAttribute(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_STYLESHEET, "");

    Transformer transformer = factory.newTransformer();

    transformer.setOutputProperty(OutputKeys.ENCODING, "UTF-8");
    transformer.setOutputProperty(OutputKeys.OMIT_XML_DECLARATION, "no");

    StringWriter writer = new StringWriter();

    transformer.transform(
        new DOMSource(document),
        new StreamResult(writer)
    );

    return writer.toString();
}
```

---

# 34. XML from String

```java
public static Document stringToDocument(String xml)
        throws Exception {
    byte[] bytes = xml.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);

    try (InputStream input = new ByteArrayInputStream(bytes)) {
        return parseSecureDom(input);
    }
}
```

---

# 35. Processing Large XML

Bad:

```java
Document document = builder.parse(hugeXmlInput);
```

Better:

```java
XMLStreamReader reader =
    secureXmlInputFactory().createXMLStreamReader(input, "UTF-8");

while (reader.hasNext()) {
    int event = reader.next();

    if (event == XMLStreamConstants.START_ELEMENT
            && "order".equals(reader.getLocalName())) {
        // process only what you need
    }
}
```

Rule:

```text
DOM loads everything.
StAX/SAX streams.
Use StAX/SAX for large XML.
```

---

# 36. XML Import Pipeline Pattern

```text
1. Receive XML stream.
2. Enforce max size at transport/file layer.
3. Parse securely.
4. Validate against XSD if contract requires.
5. Stream process records if large.
6. Map XML to domain command.
7. Validate business rules.
8. Persist in transaction/batch.
9. Audit import result.
```

Example:

```java
public ImportResult importOrders(InputStream xml) throws Exception {
    Schema schema = schemaCache.orderSchema();

    validateXml(xml, schema);

    // Important: validation consumed stream.
    // Reopen file or buffer small XML.
    // For large XML, combine parse+validate using streaming validator pipeline.
}
```

Practical note:

```text
InputStream can be consumed once.
If you validate then parse, use reopenable source, file, byte array for small XML,
or design a streaming validation+processing pipeline.
```

---

# 37. XXE Attack Example

Dangerous XML:

```xml
<?xml version="1.0"?>
<!DOCTYPE data [
  <!ENTITY xxe SYSTEM "file:///etc/passwd">
]>
<data>&xxe;</data>
```

If parser resolves entity, attacker may read local file or trigger SSRF.

Prevention:

```text
Disable DOCTYPE.
Disable external entities.
Restrict external DTD/schema/stylesheet access.
Do not resolve arbitrary external resources.
```

OWASP’s XXE cheat sheet gives Java-specific guidance for disabling XXE in common parser factories. ([OWASP Cheat Sheet Series](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/XML_External_Entity_Prevention_Cheat_Sheet.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 38. Billion Laughs Attack

Dangerous idea:

```xml
<!DOCTYPE lolz [
 <!ENTITY lol "lol">
 <!ENTITY lol1 "&lol;&lol;&lol;&lol;&lol;&lol;&lol;&lol;&lol;&lol;">
 ...
]>
```

Goal:

```text
entity expansion DoS
CPU/memory exhaustion
```

Prevention:

```text
disallow DOCTYPE
enable secure processing
set processing limits
avoid entity expansion
```
