The JAXP secure-processing feature places resource limits on XML processors to counter some denial-of-service attacks, but external access restrictions are also needed. ([OpenJDK](https://openjdk.org/jeps/185?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 39. Processing Limits

JAXP supports security-related processing limits such as entity expansion and other constraints through properties/system properties. Oracle’s JAXP Security Guide documents processing limits and external access controls. ([Oracle Docs](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/24/security/java-api-xml-processing-jaxp-security-guide.html?embed=1&utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Typical JVM-level examples:

```bash
-Djdk.xml.entityExpansionLimit=1000
-Djdk.xml.totalEntitySizeLimit=5000000
-Djdk.xml.maxGeneralEntitySizeLimit=1000000
-Djdk.xml.elementAttributeLimit=10000
-Djdk.xml.maxElementDepth=100
```

Rule:

```text
Use application-level parser hardening first.
Use JVM/system processing limits as defense in depth.
Tune based on real XML contracts.
```

---

# 40. Secure TransformerFactory

```java
public static TransformerFactory secureTransformerFactory()
        throws TransformerConfigurationException {
    TransformerFactory factory = TransformerFactory.newInstance();

    factory.setFeature(XMLConstants.FEATURE_SECURE_PROCESSING, true);
    factory.setAttribute(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_DTD, "");
    factory.setAttribute(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_STYLESHEET, "");

    return factory;
}
```

Use for:

```text
DOM serialization
XSLT transformation
XML-to-text output
```

---

# 41. Secure SchemaFactory

```java
public static SchemaFactory secureSchemaFactory()
        throws SAXNotRecognizedException, SAXNotSupportedException,
               SAXNotSupportedException {
    SchemaFactory factory = SchemaFactory.newInstance(
        XMLConstants.W3C_XML_SCHEMA_NS_URI
    );

    factory.setFeature(XMLConstants.FEATURE_SECURE_PROCESSING, true);
    factory.setProperty(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_DTD, "");
    factory.setProperty(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_SCHEMA, "");

    return factory;
}
```

Note: method signature can simply throw `SAXException` in your application helper.

```java
public static SchemaFactory secureSchemaFactory() throws SAXException {
    SchemaFactory factory = SchemaFactory.newInstance(
        XMLConstants.W3C_XML_SCHEMA_NS_URI
    );

    factory.setFeature(XMLConstants.FEATURE_SECURE_PROCESSING, true);
    factory.setProperty(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_DTD, "");
    factory.setProperty(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_SCHEMA, "");

    return factory;
}
```

---

# 42. Secure XPathFactory

XPath itself usually operates on an existing DOM, so parser security happens before XPath. Still, avoid extension functions and untrusted expression construction.

```java
XPathFactory factory = XPathFactory.newInstance();

XPath xpath = factory.newXPath();
```

Rule:

```text
Secure the parser.
Avoid XPath injection.
Avoid custom extension functions unless trusted.
```

---

# 43. XML Validation Error Mapping

```java
try {
    validateXml(input, schema);
} catch (SAXParseException e) {
    throw new InvalidXmlException(
        "XML validation failed at line %d column %d"
            .formatted(e.getLineNumber(), e.getColumnNumber()),
        e
    );
}
```

External API response:

```json
{
  "code": "INVALID_XML",
  "message": "XML validation failed"
}
```

Internal log may include:

```text
line
column
schema rule
requestId
partnerId
fileName
```

Avoid logging full XML if it may contain PII/secrets.

---

# 44. XML and JAXB Note

JAXB is not part of the same core JAXP flow in modern Java SE the way many old Java EE examples assume. In Java 11+, JAXB APIs are no longer bundled as part of the JDK in the same way as Java 8-era examples.

Practical rule:

```text
For Java 17+, if you want JAXB-style object binding,
use explicit dependencies.
For pure standard library XML processing,
use DOM/SAX/StAX/XPath/XSLT/XSD APIs from java.xml.
```

---

# 45. XML Digital Signature Note

XML Signature and XML Encryption exist, but they are complex and easy to misuse.

Java includes XML Digital Signature APIs in `javax.xml.crypto`, but for enterprise integrations, follow partner/security architecture exactly.

Rule:

```text
Do not invent your own XML signature canonicalization scheme.
Use standard XML signature libraries/APIs and test with real partner samples.
Canonicalization matters.
Whitespace and namespace handling matter.
```

---

# 46. XML Canonicalization Gotcha

In XML, these can be semantically similar but byte-different:

```xml
<a b="1" c="2">text</a>
```

```xml
<a c="2" b="1">text</a>
```

For signatures, raw string comparison is not enough. XML canonicalization defines a normalized byte representation before signing/verifying.

Use official XML Signature stack if required.

---

# 47. XML Configuration Pattern

For internal trusted config XML:

```text
1. Load from controlled classpath/file.
2. Parse with secure parser anyway.
3. Validate schema if config is complex.
4. Map to immutable config object.
5. Fail fast on invalid config.
```

Example:

```java
public record XmlImportConfig(
    int batchSize,
    boolean validationEnabled
) {}
```

---

# 48. XML in REST APIs

If your Jakarta REST endpoint accepts XML:

```text
[ ] enforce Content-Type: application/xml or text/xml
[ ] enforce max body size
[ ] parse securely
[ ] validate schema if external contract
[ ] do not log raw XML
[ ] reject DOCTYPE
[ ] return safe validation errors
[ ] test XXE payloads
```

Example boundary:

```java
if (!List.of("application/xml", "text/xml").contains(contentType)) {
    throw new UnsupportedMediaTypeException();
}
```

---

# 49. XML Testing Checklist

Test these inputs:

```text
valid XML
invalid XML syntax
missing required element
wrong namespace
wrong schema version
huge XML
deeply nested XML
XXE payload
DOCTYPE payload
external DTD payload
entity expansion payload
unexpected extra elements
empty document
wrong encoding
malformed UTF-8
```

XXE test:

```xml
<!DOCTYPE foo [
  <!ENTITY xxe SYSTEM "file:///etc/passwd">
]>
<foo>&xxe;</foo>
```

Expected:

```text
parser rejects before resolving entity
no file/network access
safe error
```

---

# 50. Common Exceptions

| Exception | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `SAXParseException` | XML syntax/validation error with line/column |
| `ParserConfigurationException` | parser setup invalid/unsupported |
| `SAXException` | generic SAX parse/validation error |
| `XMLStreamException` | StAX parse/write error |
| `XPathExpressionException` | invalid XPath |
| `TransformerException` | XSLT/serialization error |
| `TransformerConfigurationException` | transformer setup/stylesheet error |
| `IOException` | stream/file/network issue |

Pattern:

```java
try {
    Document document = parseSecureDom(input);
} catch (SAXParseException e) {
    throw new InvalidXmlException("Malformed XML", e);
} catch (SAXException e) {
    throw new InvalidXmlException("Invalid XML", e);
} catch (IOException e) {
    throw new UncheckedIOException("Could not read XML", e);
}
```

---

# 51. Performance Rules

```text
DOM:
- simple
- memory heavy
- good for random access

SAX:
- low memory
- fast
- callback complexity

StAX:
- low memory
- easier control than SAX
- good for large import/export

XPath:
- convenient
- can be expensive
- avoid repeated evaluation in hot loops

XSLT:
- compile Templates once
- new Transformer per use

XSD:
- compile Schema once
- new Validator per validation
```

---

# 52. Thread Safety Rules

General safe practice:

```text
DocumentBuilderFactory: configure then create builders; do not mutate globally
DocumentBuilder: do not share across threads
SAXParser: do not share across threads
XMLStreamReader: one stream/thread
XPath: avoid sharing mutable configured instance across threads
TransformerFactory: configure carefully
Templates: reusable/thread-safe enough for compiled stylesheet usage
Transformer: create per transformation
Schema: reusable
Validator: create per validation
```

Practical rule:

```text
Cache Schema and Templates.
Create parser/validator/transformer instances per operation.
```

---

# 53. Integration Pattern: Partner XML Message

```text
Partner XML inbound:
1. HTTP receives body.
2. Check Content-Type.
3. Enforce max size.
4. Parse securely.
5. Validate against partner XSD.
6. Extract messageId, partnerId, timestamp.
7. Check duplicate messageId.
8. Map XML to command.
9. Process idempotently.
10. Return XML or agreed error format.
11. Audit request metadata, not raw secrets.
```

Idempotency:

```java
if (messageRepository.exists(partnerId, messageId)) {
    return alreadyProcessedResponse();
}
```

---

# 54. XML Versioning Pattern

Namespace versioning:

```xml
<order xmlns="urn:example:order:v1">
```

New version:

```xml
<order xmlns="urn:example:order:v2">
```

Handler:

```java
String namespace = document.getDocumentElement().getNamespaceURI();

switch (namespace) {
    case "urn:example:order:v1" -> handleV1(document);
    case "urn:example:order:v2" -> handleV2(document);
    default -> throw new UnsupportedXmlVersionException(namespace);
}
```

---

# 55. Anti-Patterns

| Anti-pattern | Problem |
|---|---|
| Parse untrusted XML with default factory | XXE risk |
| Enable external DTD/schema fetch | SSRF/file disclosure risk |
| DOM for huge XML | memory exhaustion |
| String concat to create XML | broken escaping/injection |
| XPath concat with user input | XPath injection |
| Ignore namespaces | wrong element matching |
| Share `DocumentBuilder` across threads | concurrency bugs |
| Not closing input streams | resource leak |
| Validate then parse same consumed stream | empty parse/failure |
| Log full XML | PII/secret leak |
| Trust XSLT from user | code-like behavior/risk |
| Skip schema validation for partner contract | bad data enters domain |
| Treat pretty print as canonical XML | signature/integrity bugs |
| Use regex to parse XML | incorrect parser choice |

---

# 56. Production Checklist

```text
Security
[ ] DOCTYPE disabled for untrusted XML?
[ ] External entities disabled?
[ ] External DTD/schema/stylesheet access restricted?
[ ] Secure processing enabled where supported?
[ ] Processing limits configured?
[ ] Input size bounded?
[ ] XXE tests included?
[ ] XPath injection avoided?
[ ] Raw XML not logged?

Correctness
[ ] Namespace-aware parsing enabled?
[ ] XSD validation used for external contract?
[ ] Encoding handled explicitly?
[ ] Direct child vs descendant selection intentional?
[ ] Validation errors mapped safely?
[ ] Schema version handled?

Performance
[ ] Large XML uses StAX/SAX?
[ ] DOM only for bounded XML?
[ ] Schema cached?
[ ] Templates cached?
[ ] Parser/Validator/Transformer created per operation?

Operations
[ ] Partner samples tested?
[ ] Malformed XML handled?
[ ] Duplicate message handling exists?
[ ] Audit captures metadata?
[ ] XML payload retention policy defined?
```

---

# 57. Decision Guide

```text
Need simple parse small XML?
-> Secure DOM

Need extract a few fields from huge XML?
-> Secure StAX

Need event pipeline and low memory?
-> Secure SAX

Need query with path-like expression?
-> DOM + XPath

Need transform XML to another format?
-> Secure TransformerFactory + XSLT

Need validate partner message?
-> Secure SchemaFactory + XSD

Need write large XML?
-> StAX XMLStreamWriter

Need sign XML?
-> XML Digital Signature API/library; do not custom-build

Need object binding?
-> Add JAXB/Jackson XML/etc. dependency explicitly
```

---

# 58. Compact Summary

```text
JAXP
- Java XML processing abstraction
- provider-based factories

DOM
- tree model
- easy but memory-heavy
- good for small XML

SAX
- push event parser
- low memory
- callback style

StAX
- pull event parser/writer
- low memory
- practical for large XML

XPath
- query DOM
- beware namespace and injection

XSLT
- transform XML
- secure TransformerFactory
- cache Templates

XSD
- validate XML contract
- cache Schema
- new Validator per validation

Security
- disable DOCTYPE
- disable external entities
- restrict external access
- enable secure processing
- set limits
- avoid untrusted XSLT
- do not log raw XML

Production Rule
- XML from outside your trust boundary is hostile until securely parsed and validated.
```

Kesimpulan praktis: **JAXP sangat powerful, tapi default XML processing bisa berbahaya jika tidak di-hardening.** Untuk Java enterprise, standar aman adalah: **secure factory, namespace-aware parsing, bounded input, XSD validation untuk kontrak eksternal, StAX/SAX untuk large XML, XPath tanpa concatenation input, XSLT tanpa external access, dan test khusus untuk XXE/entity expansion.**

---

**Sources:**

- [java.xml (Java SE 21 & JDK 21 [build 1])](https://download.java.net/java/early_access/genzgc/docs/api/java.xml/module-summary.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- [12 Java API for XML Processing (JAXP) Security Guide](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/17/security/java-api-xml-processing-jaxp-security-guide.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- [XML External Entity Prevention Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/XML_External_Entity_Prevention_Cheat_Sheet.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- [JEP 185: Restrict Fetching of External XML Resources](https://openjdk.org/jeps/185?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

## Response:
7/12/2026, 9:56:50 PM

Berikut **cheat sheet Java SQL Package / JDBC Core API** untuk **Java 17+**.

Fokusnya: `java.sql`, JDBC mental model, `Connection`, `PreparedStatement`, `ResultSet`, transaction, batch, generated keys, SQL types, temporal mapping, LOB, metadata, exception handling, dan production patterns.

# Java SQL Package Cheat Sheet

---

# 1. Mental Model

```text
Java Application
  ↓
JDBC API: java.sql / javax.sql
  ↓
JDBC Driver: PostgreSQL / Oracle / MySQL / SQL Server / etc.
  ↓
Database
```

`java.sql` adalah JDBC core API untuk mengakses dan memproses data dari data source, biasanya relational database. Package ini mencakup connection, statement execution, result processing, SQL type mapping, metadata, dan JDBC exceptions. ([Oracle Docs](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/api/java.sql/java/sql/package-summary.html))

---

# 2. Package Map

```text
java.sql
├── DriverManager
├── Driver
├── Connection
├── Statement
├── PreparedStatement
├── CallableStatement
├── ResultSet
├── Savepoint
├── DatabaseMetaData
├── ResultSetMetaData
├── ParameterMetaData
├── SQLException
├── SQLWarning
├── Types / JDBCType / SQLType
├── Date / Time / Timestamp
├── Blob / Clob / NClob
├── Array / Struct / Ref / RowId
└── SQLXML
```

Core JDBC API ada di `java.sql`, sedangkan `javax.sql` menambah API seperti `DataSource`, connection pooling, distributed transaction, dan RowSet. ([Oracle Docs](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/api/java.sql/java/sql/package-summary.html))

---

# 3. Decision Matrix

| Need | Use |
|---|---|
| Connect simple CLI/test | `DriverManager` |
| Connect production app | `DataSource` / connection pool |
| Execute fixed SQL without parameter | `Statement` |
| Execute SQL with parameters | `PreparedStatement` |
| Call stored procedure/function | `CallableStatement` |
| Read query result | `ResultSet` |
| Manual transaction | `Connection#setAutoCommit(false)` |
| Partial rollback | `Savepoint` |
| Insert many rows | batch with `PreparedStatement#addBatch()` |
| Retrieve generated ID | `RETURN_GENERATED_KEYS` |
| Inspect DB metadata | `DatabaseMetaData` |
| Inspect result columns | `ResultSetMetaData` |
| Handle DB errors | `SQLException`, SQLState, vendor code |
| Large binary/text | `Blob`, `Clob`, stream APIs |
| SQL date/time | Java Time API via `setObject` / `getObject` |

---

# 4. Maven Dependency

`java.sql` adalah bagian dari JDK, tapi **JDBC driver database tidak otomatis tersedia**. Untuk PostgreSQL, tambahkan driver.

```xml
<dependency>
    <groupId>org.postgresql</groupId>
    <artifactId>postgresql</artifactId>
    <version>${postgresql.version}</version>
</dependency>
```

Tanpa driver, kode JDBC bisa compile, tetapi koneksi ke database tertentu akan gagal saat runtime.

---

# 5. JPMS Module

Jika memakai Java Module System:

```java
module com.example.app {
    requires java.sql;
}
```

Kalau memakai `DataSource` dari `javax.sql`, module `java.sql` juga mencakup API JDBC standard tersebut dalam Java SE.

---

# 6. Basic Connection with `DriverManager`

```java
String url = "jdbc:postgresql://localhost:5432/appdb";
String username = "appuser";
String password = "secret";

try (Connection connection = DriverManager.getConnection(url, username, password)) {
    System.out.println("Connected: " + !connection.isClosed());
}
```

`DriverManager` adalah service dasar untuk mengelola JDBC drivers dan membuat connection berdasarkan JDBC URL. ([Oracle Docs](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/api/java.sql/java/sql/DriverManager.html))

---

# 7. JDBC URL Pattern

```text
jdbc:<database>://<host>:<port>/<database>?<params>
```

Examples:

```text
jdbc:postgresql://localhost:5432/appdb
jdbc:mysql://localhost:3306/appdb
jdbc:oracle:thin:@localhost:1521/XEPDB1
jdbc:sqlserver://localhost:1433;databaseName=appdb
```

Rule:

```text
JDBC URL is driver-specific.
Always check your database driver documentation.
```

---

# 8. Always Use Try-With-Resources

Good:

```java
try (
    Connection connection = DriverManager.getConnection(url, username, password);
    PreparedStatement statement = connection.prepareStatement(
        "SELECT id, name FROM customer WHERE id = ?"
    )
) {
    statement.setLong(1, customerId);

    try (ResultSet rs = statement.executeQuery()) {
        if (rs.next()) {
            return mapCustomer(rs);
        }
    }
}
```

JDBC 4.1 added support for using `Connection`, `Statement`, and `ResultSet` with try-with-resources. ([Oracle Docs](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/api/java.sql/java/sql/package-summary.html))

---

# 9. `Connection`

`Connection` represents a session with a specific database. It creates statements, manages transactions, handles commit/rollback, isolation, schema, read-only mode, and connection properties. ([Oracle Docs](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/api/java.sql/java/sql/package-summary.html))

Common methods:

```java
connection.prepareStatement(sql);
connection.createStatement();
connection.prepareCall(sql);

connection.setAutoCommit(false);
connection.commit();
connection.rollback();

connection.setTransactionIsolation(Connection.TRANSACTION_READ_COMMITTED);
connection.setReadOnly(true);
connection.setSchema("public");

connection.isValid(2);
connection.close();
```

---

# 10. `Statement` vs `PreparedStatement`

| Type | Use |
|---|---|
| `Statement` | static SQL without parameters |
| `PreparedStatement` | parameterized SQL |
| `CallableStatement` | stored procedure/function call |

Prefer `PreparedStatement` for almost all application SQL because it supports bind parameters and can be precompiled/reused by the driver/database. Oracle’s Javadoc describes `PreparedStatement` as a precompiled SQL statement that can be executed multiple times efficiently. ([Oracle Docs](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/api/java.sql/java/sql/PreparedStatement.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 11. `Statement`

```java
try (
    Statement statement = connection.createStatement();
    ResultSet rs = statement.executeQuery("SELECT id, name FROM customer")
) {
    while (rs.next()) {
        System.out.println(rs.getLong("id"));
        System.out.println(rs.getString("name"));
    }
}
```

Use `Statement` only when SQL is truly static and has no user input.

Bad:

```java
String sql = "SELECT * FROM customer WHERE name = '" + name + "'";
```

Good:

```java
String sql = "SELECT * FROM customer WHERE name = ?";
PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(sql);
ps.setString(1, name);
```

---

# 12. `PreparedStatement` Basics

```java
String sql = """
    SELECT id, name, email
    FROM customer
    WHERE status = ?
      AND created_at >= ?
    ORDER BY created_at DESC
    """;

try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(sql)) {
    ps.setString(1, "ACTIVE");
    ps.setObject(2, OffsetDateTime.now().minusDays(30));

    try (ResultSet rs = ps.executeQuery()) {
        while (rs.next()) {
            Customer customer = mapCustomer(rs);
        }
    }
}
```

Important:

```text
Parameter index starts from 1, not 0.
Bind values, not SQL identifiers.
```

---

# 13. SQL Injection Rule

Bad:

```java
String sql = "SELECT * FROM users WHERE username = '" + username + "'";
```

Good:

```java
String sql = "SELECT * FROM users WHERE username = ?";

try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(sql)) {
    ps.setString(1, username);

    try (ResultSet rs = ps.executeQuery()) {
        // read
    }
}
```

But `PreparedStatement` cannot parameterize table names, column names, sort directions, or SQL keywords.

Bad:

```java
String sql = "SELECT * FROM orders ORDER BY ?";
```

Better with allowlist:

```java
private static String allowedSortColumn(String input) {
    return switch (input) {
        case "createdAt" -> "created_at";
        case "status" -> "status";
        case "amount" -> "amount";
        default -> throw new IllegalArgumentException("Invalid sort column");
    };
}

private static String allowedSortDirection(String input) {
    return switch (input.toUpperCase(Locale.ROOT)) {
        case "ASC" -> "ASC";
        case "DESC" -> "DESC";
        default -> throw new IllegalArgumentException("Invalid sort direction");
    };
}
```

---

# 14. SELECT Pattern

```java
public Optional<Customer> findById(Connection connection, long id)
        throws SQLException {
    String sql = """
        SELECT id, name, email, created_at
        FROM customer
        WHERE id = ?
        """;

    try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(sql)) {
        ps.setLong(1, id);

        try (ResultSet rs = ps.executeQuery()) {
            if (!rs.next()) {
                return Optional.empty();
            }

            return Optional.of(mapCustomer(rs));
        }
    }
}
```

Mapper:

```java
private Customer mapCustomer(ResultSet rs) throws SQLException {
    return new Customer(
        rs.getLong("id"),
        rs.getString("name"),
        rs.getString("email"),
        rs.getObject("created_at", OffsetDateTime.class)
    );
}
```

---

# 15. INSERT Pattern

```java
public int insertCustomer(
    Connection connection,
    String name,
    String email
) throws SQLException {
    String sql = """
        INSERT INTO customer (name, email)
        VALUES (?, ?)
        """;

    try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(sql)) {
        ps.setString(1, name);
        ps.setString(2, email);

        return ps.executeUpdate();
    }
}
```

`executeUpdate()` returns affected row count for insert/update/delete.

---

# 16. INSERT with Generated Key

```java
public long insertCustomerReturningId(
    Connection connection,
    String name,
    String email
) throws SQLException {
    String sql = """
        INSERT INTO customer (name, email)
        VALUES (?, ?)
        """;

    try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(
        sql,
        Statement.RETURN_GENERATED_KEYS
    )) {
        ps.setString(1, name);
        ps.setString(2, email);

        int affected = ps.executeUpdate();

        if (affected != 1) {
            throw new SQLException("Expected 1 row inserted, got " + affected);
        }

        try (ResultSet keys = ps.getGeneratedKeys()) {
            if (!keys.next()) {
                throw new SQLException("No generated key returned");
            }

            return keys.getLong(1);
        }
    }
}
```

For PostgreSQL, many teams prefer explicit `RETURNING id`:

```java
String sql = """
    INSERT INTO customer (name, email)
    VALUES (?, ?)
    RETURNING id
    """;
```

---

# 17. UPDATE Pattern

```java
public boolean updateEmail(
    Connection connection,
    long customerId,
    String email
) throws SQLException {
    String sql = """
        UPDATE customer
        SET email = ?
        WHERE id = ?
        """;

    try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(sql)) {
        ps.setString(1, email);
        ps.setLong(2, customerId);

        return ps.executeUpdate() == 1;
    }
}
```

Optimistic locking:

```java
String sql = """
    UPDATE orders
    SET status = ?, version = version + 1
    WHERE id = ?
      AND version = ?
    """;
```

If affected rows `0`, either row not found or version conflict.

---

# 18. DELETE Pattern

```java
public boolean deleteById(Connection connection, long id)
        throws SQLException {
    String sql = "DELETE FROM customer WHERE id = ?";

    try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(sql)) {
        ps.setLong(1, id);

        return ps.executeUpdate() == 1;
    }
}
```

Soft delete:

```sql
UPDATE customer
SET deleted_at = now()
WHERE id = ?
```

Production rule:

```text
Prefer soft delete when audit/recovery/legal retention matters.
Use hard delete when data lifecycle explicitly allows it.
```

---

# 19. `ResultSet`

`ResultSet` represents tabular data returned by a database query, usually from executing a SQL query. ([Oracle Docs](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/api/java.sql/java/sql/ResultSet.html))

Basic:

```java
while (rs.next()) {
    long id = rs.getLong("id");
    String name = rs.getString("name");
}
```

Cursor starts before first row:

```text
Before first row
  ↓ rs.next()
First row
  ↓ rs.next()
Second row
  ↓
...
```

---

# 20. ResultSet Getter Methods

| SQL Type | Java Getter |
|---|---|
| integer | `getInt`, `getLong` |
| text | `getString` |
| boolean | `getBoolean` |
| numeric/decimal | `getBigDecimal` |
| date | `getObject(col, LocalDate.class)` |
| time | `getObject(col, LocalTime.class)` |
| timestamp | `getObject(col, LocalDateTime.class)` |
| timestamp with timezone | `getObject(col, OffsetDateTime.class)` if driver supports |
| binary | `getBytes`, `getBinaryStream` |
| UUID | `getObject(col, UUID.class)` if driver supports |
| JSON/JSONB | driver-specific, often `getString` or `PGobject` |

---

# 21. Null Handling

Primitive getters can hide SQL `NULL`.

```java
long value = rs.getLong("parent_id");

if (rs.wasNull()) {
    // parent_id was SQL NULL
}
```

Better for nullable values:

```java
Long parentId = rs.getObject("parent_id", Long.class);
Integer count = rs.getObject("count", Integer.class);
Boolean active = rs.getObject("active", Boolean.class);
```

Rule:

```text
Use getObject(column, WrapperType.class) for nullable primitive-like columns.
```

---

# 22. Column Index vs Column Name

By index:

```java
long id = rs.getLong(1);
String name = rs.getString(2);
```

By name:

```java
long id = rs.getLong("id");
String name = rs.getString("name");
```

Rule:

```text
Use column names for readability.
Use explicit column aliases to avoid ambiguity.
Avoid SELECT * in production queries.
```

Good:

```sql
SELECT
    c.id AS customer_id,
    c.name AS customer_name
FROM customer c
WHERE c.id = ?
```

---

# 23. SQL Types

`java.sql.Types` provides constants for generic SQL types, and `JDBCType` is an enum introduced in JDBC 4.2 for generic SQL type identification. ([Oracle Docs](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/api/java.sql/java/sql/package-summary.html))

Examples:

```java
ps.setNull(1, Types.VARCHAR);
ps.setObject(2, value, JDBCType.TIMESTAMP_WITH_TIMEZONE);
```

Common mapping:

| SQL | Java |
|---|---|
| `VARCHAR`, `TEXT` | `String` |
| `INTEGER` | `Integer` |
| `BIGINT` | `Long` |
| `NUMERIC`, `DECIMAL` | `BigDecimal` |
| `BOOLEAN` | `Boolean` |
| `DATE` | `LocalDate` |
| `TIME` | `LocalTime` |
| `TIMESTAMP` | `LocalDateTime` |
| `TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE` | `OffsetDateTime` |
| `BLOB`, `BYTEA` | `byte[]`, `InputStream` |
| `CLOB`, `TEXT` | `String`, `Reader` |

---

# 24. Java Time API with JDBC

Prefer Java Time API over old `java.sql.Date`, `java.sql.Time`, and `java.sql.Timestamp`.

Set values:

```java
ps.setObject(1, LocalDate.now());
ps.setObject(2, LocalDateTime.now());
ps.setObject(3, OffsetDateTime.now());
```

Read values:

```java
LocalDate date = rs.getObject("business_date", LocalDate.class);
LocalDateTime createdAt = rs.getObject("created_at", LocalDateTime.class);
OffsetDateTime eventTime = rs.getObject("event_time", OffsetDateTime.class);
```

Old JDBC classes still exist:

```java
java.sql.Date
java.sql.Time
java.sql.Timestamp
```

But for modern Java, prefer:

```text
LocalDate
LocalTime
LocalDateTime
OffsetDateTime
Instant, depending driver support
```

---

# 25. BigDecimal for Money

Good:

```java
BigDecimal amount = rs.getBigDecimal("amount");
ps.setBigDecimal(1, amount);
```

Bad:

```java
double amount = rs.getDouble("amount");
```

Rule:

```text
Use BigDecimal for money, tax, pricing, billing, and exact decimal values.
```

---

# 26. Transactions

Default JDBC connection usually starts with auto-commit enabled. For multi-statement unit of work, disable auto-commit.

```java
public void transfer(
    Connection connection,
    long fromAccountId,
    long toAccountId,
    BigDecimal amount
) throws SQLException {
    boolean oldAutoCommit = connection.getAutoCommit();

    try {
        connection.setAutoCommit(false);

        debit(connection, fromAccountId, amount);
        credit(connection, toAccountId, amount);

        connection.commit();

    } catch (SQLException e) {
        connection.rollback();
        throw e;
    } finally {
        connection.setAutoCommit(oldAutoCommit);
    }
}
```

Rule:

```text
One business unit of work = one transaction boundary.
Commit only after all required changes succeed.
Rollback on failure.
```

---

# 27. Savepoint

`Savepoint` represents a point inside the current transaction that can be used by `Connection.rollback(savepoint)`. ([Oracle Docs](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/api/java.sql/java/sql/package-summary.html))

```java
connection.setAutoCommit(false);

Savepoint beforeOptionalStep = null;

try {
    insertMainRecord(connection);

    beforeOptionalStep = connection.setSavepoint("before_optional_step");

    try {
        insertOptionalRecord(connection);
    } catch (SQLException optionalFailure) {
        connection.rollback(beforeOptionalStep);
    }

    connection.commit();

} catch (SQLException e) {
    connection.rollback();
    throw e;
}
```

Use savepoint when part of a transaction is optional or recoverable.

---

# 28. Isolation Levels

```java
connection.setTransactionIsolation(Connection.TRANSACTION_READ_COMMITTED);
```

Constants:

| Constant | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `TRANSACTION_READ_UNCOMMITTED` | weakest |
| `TRANSACTION_READ_COMMITTED` | common default |
| `TRANSACTION_REPEATABLE_READ` | stronger consistency |
| `TRANSACTION_SERIALIZABLE` | strongest, more contention |
| `TRANSACTION_NONE` | transactions unsupported |

Rule:

```text
Choose isolation based on business invariant, not habit.
Higher isolation can reduce anomalies but increase locking/retry.
```

---

# 29. Read-Only Transaction Hint

```java
connection.setReadOnly(true);
```

Use for:

```text
report query
read-only API
replica routing hint
intent documentation
```

Caution:

```text
It is a hint to driver/database.
Do not rely on it as the only security control.
```

---

# 30. Query Timeout

```java
try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(sql)) {
    ps.setQueryTimeout(10); // seconds

    try (ResultSet rs = ps.executeQuery()) {
        // read
    }
}
```

`SQLTimeoutException` is thrown when timeout specified by `Statement.setQueryTimeout`, `DriverManager.setLoginTimeout`, or related APIs expires. ([Oracle Docs](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/api/java.sql/java/sql/package-summary.html))

Rule:

```text
Every production query should have timeout strategy:
- JDBC query timeout
- connection pool timeout
- transaction timeout
- application request timeout
```

---

# 31. Fetch Size

```java
try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(sql)) {
    ps.setFetchSize(500);

    try (ResultSet rs = ps.executeQuery()) {
        while (rs.next()) {
            process(rs);
        }
    }
}
```

Use for large result sets.

Caution:

```text
Fetch size behavior is driver-specific.
Test with your JDBC driver and database.
```

---

# 32. Batch Insert

```java
String sql = """
    INSERT INTO customer (name, email)
    VALUES (?, ?)
    """;

try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(sql)) {
    for (CustomerInput input : inputs) {
        ps.setString(1, input.name());
        ps.setString(2, input.email());
        ps.addBatch();
    }

    int[] counts = ps.executeBatch();
}
```

Batch with chunking:

```java
int batchSize = 1000;
int count = 0;

for (CustomerInput input : inputs) {
    ps.setString(1, input.name());
    ps.setString(2, input.email());
    ps.addBatch();

    if (++count % batchSize == 0) {
        ps.executeBatch();
    }
}

ps.executeBatch();
```

Rule:

```text
Batch large writes.
Chunk batches.
Do not build one gigantic batch in memory.
```

---

# 33. Batch Update Exception

```java
try {
    ps.executeBatch();
} catch (BatchUpdateException e) {
    int[] updateCounts = e.getUpdateCounts();

    throw e;
}
```

`BatchUpdateException` indicates that not all commands in a batch update executed successfully. ([Oracle Docs](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/api/java.sql/java/sql/package-summary.html))

---

# 34. CallableStatement

Use for stored procedures/functions.

```java
String sql = "{ call update_customer_status(?, ?) }";

try (CallableStatement cs = connection.prepareCall(sql)) {
    cs.setLong(1, customerId);
    cs.setString(2, "ACTIVE");

    cs.execute();
}
```

With OUT parameter:

```java
String sql = "{ ? = call calculate_customer_score(?) }";

try (CallableStatement cs = connection.prepareCall(sql)) {
    cs.registerOutParameter(1, Types.INTEGER);
    cs.setLong(2, customerId);

    cs.execute();

    int score = cs.getInt(1);
}
```

`CallableStatement` is used to execute SQL stored procedures and derives from `PreparedStatement`. ([Oracle Docs](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/api/java.sql/java/sql/package-summary.html))

---

# 35. Large Objects: BLOB / CLOB

## Binary Stream

```java
String sql = """
    INSERT INTO document (id, content)
    VALUES (?, ?)
    """;

try (
    InputStream input = Files.newInputStream(path);
    PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(sql)
) {
    ps.setLong(1, documentId);
    ps.setBinaryStream(2, input, Files.size(path));

    ps.executeUpdate();
}
```

Read binary:

```java
try (InputStream input = rs.getBinaryStream("content")) {
    input.transferTo(outputStream);
}
```

## Text Stream

```java
try (Reader reader = Files.newBufferedReader(path, StandardCharsets.UTF_8)) {
    ps.setCharacterStream(1, reader);
}
```

Rule:

```text
For large payloads, stream.
Do not load huge BLOB/CLOB fully into byte[] or String.
```

---

# 36. SQLXML

```java
SQLXML xml = connection.createSQLXML();

try {
    xml.setString("<order><id>123</id></order>");

    try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(
        "INSERT INTO order_xml (payload) VALUES (?)"
    )) {
        ps.setSQLXML(1, xml);
        ps.executeUpdate();
    }
} finally {
    xml.free();
}
```

Read:

```java
SQLXML xml = rs.getSQLXML("payload");

try {
    String content = xml.getString();
} finally {
    xml.free();
}
```

`SQLXML` is the Java mapping for SQL XML data type. ([Oracle Docs](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/api/java.sql/java/sql/package-summary.html))

---

# 37. Array

```java
Array sqlArray = connection.createArrayOf(
    "text",
    new String[] {"A", "B", "C"}
);

try {
    ps.setArray(1, sqlArray);
} finally {
    sqlArray.free();
}
```

Read:

```java
Array array = rs.getArray("tags");

try {
    String[] tags = (String[]) array.getArray();
} finally {
    array.free();
}
```

Caution:

```text
Array support and element type names are database/driver-specific.
```

---

# 38. Metadata

## DatabaseMetaData

```java
DatabaseMetaData meta = connection.getMetaData();

System.out.println(meta.getDatabaseProductName());
System.out.println(meta.getDatabaseProductVersion());
System.out.println(meta.getDriverName());
System.out.println(meta.getDriverVersion());
```

`DatabaseMetaData` provides comprehensive information about the database as a whole. ([Oracle Docs](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/api/java.sql/java/sql/package-summary.html))

## ResultSetMetaData

```java
try (ResultSet rs = ps.executeQuery()) {
    ResultSetMetaData meta = rs.getMetaData();

    int columnCount = meta.getColumnCount();

    for (int i = 1; i <= columnCount; i++) {
        System.out.println(meta.getColumnLabel(i));
        System.out.println(meta.getColumnTypeName(i));
        System.out.println(meta.getColumnClassName(i));
    }
}
```

`ResultSetMetaData` provides information about columns in a `ResultSet`. ([Oracle Docs](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/api/java.sql/java/sql/package-summary.html))

---

# 39. SQLException

```java
try {
    ps.executeUpdate();
} catch (SQLException e) {
    String sqlState = e.getSQLState();
    int vendorCode = e.getErrorCode();
    String message = e.getMessage();

    throw e;
}
```

`SQLException` provides information about database access errors or other database-related errors. ([Oracle Docs](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/api/java.sql/java/sql/package-summary.html))

Useful fields:

| Method | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `getSQLState()` | standard-ish SQLState |
| `getErrorCode()` | vendor-specific error code |
| `getMessage()` | error message |
| `getCause()` | cause |
| `getNextException()` | chained SQL exception |

---

# 40. SQLState Classes

Common SQLState class prefixes:

| Prefix | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `08` | connection exception |
| `22` | data exception |
| `23` | integrity constraint violation |
| `28` | invalid authorization |
| `40` | transaction rollback |
| `42` | syntax/access rule violation |
| `0A` | feature not supported |

JDBC includes specific subclasses such as `SQLIntegrityConstraintViolationException`, `SQLSyntaxErrorException`, `SQLTransactionRollbackException`, and `SQLTimeoutException`. ([Oracle Docs](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/api/java.sql/java/sql/package-summary.html))

---

# 41. Exception Translation Pattern

```java
public RuntimeException translate(SQLException e) {
    String state = e.getSQLState();

    if (state == null) {
        return new DatabaseException("Database error", e);
    }

    if (state.startsWith("23")) {
        return new ConstraintViolationException("Constraint violation", e);
    }

    if (state.startsWith("40")) {
        return new TransactionConflictException("Transaction conflict", e);
    }

    if (state.startsWith("08")) {
        return new DatabaseUnavailableException("Database connection error", e);
    }

    if (state.startsWith("42")) {
        return new DatabaseProgrammingException("SQL syntax/access error", e);
    }

    return new DatabaseException("Database error", e);
}
```

Rule:

```text
Do not leak raw SQL error messages to external users.
Log safely for operators.
Translate to domain/application error.
```

---

# 42. Unique Constraint Handling

PostgreSQL unique violation SQLState is commonly `23505`.

```java
try {
    insertCustomer(connection, name, email);
} catch (SQLException e) {
    if ("23505".equals(e.getSQLState())) {
        throw new DuplicateEmailException(email, e);
    }

    throw e;
}
```

More portable approach:

```java
if (e instanceof SQLIntegrityConstraintViolationException) {
    throw new ConstraintViolationException(e);
}
```

Caution:

```text
Vendor-specific SQLState/code handling is often necessary for precise behavior.
Keep it isolated in a translator.
```

---

# 43. Connection Pooling

`java.sql.Connection` is a physical/logical database session abstraction, but production apps should not create a new DB connection for every request using `DriverManager`.

Prefer:

```text
DataSource + connection pool
```

Typical production flow:

```text
HTTP request
  ↓
borrow connection from pool
  ↓
execute transaction/query
  ↓
commit/rollback
  ↓
close connection
  ↓
connection returns to pool
```

Important:

```text
connection.close() with pool usually returns connection to pool,
not necessarily closes physical socket.
```

---

# 44. DataSource Pattern

Even though `DataSource` is in `javax.sql`, it is essential for real apps.

```java
public final class CustomerRepository {
    private final DataSource dataSource;

    public CustomerRepository(DataSource dataSource) {
        this.dataSource = dataSource;
    }

    public Optional<Customer> findById(long id) {
        try (Connection connection = dataSource.getConnection()) {
            return findById(connection, id);
        } catch (SQLException e) {
            throw translate(e);
        }
    }
}
```

Rule:

```text
Use DriverManager for demos/tools.
Use DataSource for application code.
```

---

# 45. Repository Pattern

```java
public final class OrderRepository {
    private final DataSource dataSource;

    public OrderRepository(DataSource dataSource) {
        this.dataSource = dataSource;
    }

    public Optional<Order> findById(UUID tenantId, UUID orderId) {
        String sql = """
            SELECT id, tenant_id, status, amount, created_at
            FROM orders
            WHERE tenant_id = ?
              AND id = ?
            """;

        try (
            Connection connection = dataSource.getConnection();
            PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(sql)
        ) {
            ps.setObject(1, tenantId);
            ps.setObject(2, orderId);

            try (ResultSet rs = ps.executeQuery()) {
                if (!rs.next()) {
                    return Optional.empty();
                }

                return Optional.of(mapOrder(rs));
            }

        } catch (SQLException e) {
            throw translate(e);
        }
    }
}
```

Tenant rule:

```text
For multi-tenant apps, tenant_id should be part of every tenant-scoped query.
```

---

# 46. Transaction Boundary Pattern

Avoid each repository opening its own connection if multiple operations must be atomic.

Good:

```java
public void approveOrder(UUID tenantId, UUID orderId, UUID approverId) {
    try (Connection connection = dataSource.getConnection()) {
        boolean oldAutoCommit = connection.getAutoCommit();

        try {
            connection.setAutoCommit(false);

            Order order = orderRepository.findById(connection, tenantId, orderId)
                .orElseThrow();

            order.approve(approverId);

            orderRepository.update(connection, order);
            auditRepository.insert(connection, AuditEvent.orderApproved(order));

            connection.commit();

        } catch (Exception e) {
            connection.rollback();
            throw e;
        } finally {
            connection.setAutoCommit(oldAutoCommit);
        }

    } catch (SQLException e) {
        throw translate(e);
    }
}
```

Rule:

```text
Connection represents transaction scope.
Pass Connection explicitly inside one transaction boundary.
```

---

# 47. Pagination

Offset pagination:

```java
String sql = """
    SELECT id, created_at, status
    FROM orders
    WHERE tenant_id = ?
    ORDER BY created_at DESC, id DESC
    LIMIT ?
    OFFSET ?
    """;

ps.setObject(1, tenantId);
ps.setInt(2, limit);
ps.setInt(3, offset);
```

Keyset pagination:

```java
String sql = """
    SELECT id, created_at, status
    FROM orders
    WHERE tenant_id = ?
      AND (created_at, id) < (?, ?)
    ORDER BY created_at DESC, id DESC
    LIMIT ?
    """;
```

Rule:

```text
Offset pagination is simple but expensive for deep pages.
Keyset pagination is better for large/scrolling datasets.
```

---

# 48. Dynamic IN Clause

Bad:

```java
String sql = "SELECT * FROM orders WHERE id IN (?)";
ps.setString(1, "1,2,3");
```

Better generate placeholders:

```java
public static String placeholders(int count) {
    return IntStream.range(0, count)
        .mapToObj(i -> "?")
        .collect(Collectors.joining(", "));
}
```

Use:

```java
List<UUID> ids = List.of(id1, id2, id3);

String sql = """
    SELECT id, status
    FROM orders
    WHERE id IN (%s)
    """.formatted(placeholders(ids.size()));

try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(sql)) {
    int index = 1;

    for (UUID id : ids) {
        ps.setObject(index++, id);
    }

    try (ResultSet rs = ps.executeQuery()) {
        // read
    }
}
```

If list is huge, use temp table, array binding, or database-specific features.

---

# 49. Upsert

JDBC has no universal upsert abstraction. Use database-specific SQL.

PostgreSQL:

```sql
INSERT INTO customer (id, email, name)
VALUES (?, ?, ?)
ON CONFLICT (email)
DO UPDATE SET name = EXCLUDED.name
```

Java:

```java
try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(sql)) {
    ps.setObject(1, id);
    ps.setString(2, email);
    ps.setString(3, name);
    ps.executeUpdate();
}
```

Rule:

```text
Keep vendor-specific SQL isolated.
Do not pretend every database has identical SQL behavior.
```

---

# 50. Locking Query

PostgreSQL example:

```sql
SELECT id, status
FROM orders
WHERE id = ?
FOR UPDATE
```

Java:

```java
connection.setAutoCommit(false);

try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(sql)) {
    ps.setObject(1, orderId);

    try (ResultSet rs = ps.executeQuery()) {
        // locked row until commit/rollback
    }

    connection.commit();
}
```

Rule:

```text
Locks live inside transactions.
Keep transactions short.
Never call slow remote APIs while holding DB locks.
```

---

# 51. Optimistic Locking

Schema:

```sql
ALTER TABLE orders ADD COLUMN version BIGINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0;
```

Update:

```sql
UPDATE orders
SET status = ?, version = version + 1
WHERE id = ?
  AND version = ?
```

Java:

```java
int updated = ps.executeUpdate();

if (updated == 0) {
    throw new OptimisticLockException("Order was modified by another transaction");
}
```

Use when:

```text
concurrent updates possible
user edits data
avoid long locks
detect lost update
```

---

# 52. Generated SQL Logging

Do not log:

```text
password
token
PII
full card number
Authorization header
raw secret
```

Log safely:

```text
query name
duration
row count
SQLState
error category
correlationId
```

Example:

```java
long start = System.nanoTime();

try {
    int rows = ps.executeUpdate();

    log.info(
        "SQL completed: name={}, rows={}, durationMs={}",
        "insertCustomer",
        rows,
        Duration.ofNanos(System.nanoTime() - start).toMillis()
    );

} catch (SQLException e) {
    log.warn(
        "SQL failed: name={}, sqlState={}, vendorCode={}",
        "insertCustomer",
        e.getSQLState(),
        e.getErrorCode()
    );

    throw e;
}
```

---

# 53. SQL Warning

```java
SQLWarning warning = statement.getWarnings();

while (warning != null) {
    log.warn(
        "SQL warning: state={}, code={}, message={}",
        warning.getSQLState(),
        warning.getErrorCode(),
        warning.getMessage()
    );

    warning = warning.getNextWarning();
}

statement.clearWarnings();
```

Use warnings for diagnostics, not primary error handling.

---

# 54. Resource Closing Order

Good:

```java
try (
    Connection connection = dataSource.getConnection();
    PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(sql);
    ResultSet rs = ps.executeQuery()
) {
    while (rs.next()) {
        // read
    }
}
```

Closing order is reverse:

```text
ResultSet closes
PreparedStatement closes
Connection closes/returns to pool
```

Anti-pattern:

```java
Connection connection = dataSource.getConnection();
// missing close on exception
```

---

# 55. SQL and Java Type Safety

Problem:

```java
ps.setString(1, "123");      // DB column is BIGINT
rs.getString("amount");      // DB column is NUMERIC
```

Better:

```java
ps.setLong(1, customerId);
BigDecimal amount = rs.getBigDecimal("amount");
```

Rule:

```text
Use Java type matching the database column.
Avoid generic setObject/getObject without type discipline.
```

---

# 56. Database Time Handling

Recommended patterns:

```text
created_at / updated_at / event timestamp:
- store as timestamptz / timestamp with time zone when DB supports it
- use OffsetDateTime or Instant semantics

business_date:
- store as DATE
- use LocalDate

local scheduled time:
- store local date/time + zone if future timezone rules matter
```

Avoid:

```text
storing local time as global timestamp without zone meaning
using system default timezone implicitly
mixing LocalDateTime with absolute event time
```

---

# 57. SQLXML / JSON / Vendor Types

`java.sql` covers standard SQL types, but databases have vendor-specific types:

```text
PostgreSQL UUID
PostgreSQL JSONB
PostgreSQL ARRAY
PostgreSQL ENUM
Oracle STRUCT
SQL Server UNIQUEIDENTIFIER
```

Pattern:

```java
UUID id = rs.getObject("id", UUID.class);
ps.setObject(1, id);
```

For JSONB in PostgreSQL, many codebases use:

```java
ps.setObject(1, jsonString, Types.OTHER);
```

Rule:

```text
Keep vendor-specific mappings in repository/adapter layer.
Do not leak driver-specific classes into domain model.
```

---

# 58. Query Object Pattern

```java
public record PageRequest(
    int limit,
    int offset
) {
    public PageRequest {
        if (limit < 1 || limit > 500) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("Invalid limit");
        }

        if (offset < 0) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("Invalid offset");
        }
    }
}
```

```java
public record OrderSearch(
    UUID tenantId,
    Optional<String> status,
    PageRequest page
) {}
```

Build SQL carefully:

```java
StringBuilder sql = new StringBuilder("""
    SELECT id, status, created_at
    FROM orders
    WHERE tenant_id = ?
    """);

List<Object> params = new ArrayList<>();
params.add(search.tenantId());

if (search.status().isPresent()) {
    sql.append(" AND status = ?");
    params.add(search.status().get());
}

sql.append(" ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT ? OFFSET ?");
params.add(search.page().limit());
params.add(search.page().offset());
```

Bind:

```java
try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(sql.toString())) {
    for (int i = 0; i < params.size(); i++) {
        ps.setObject(i + 1, params.get(i));
    }
}
```

---

# 59. Stored Procedure vs Application Logic

Use stored procedures when:

```text
logic must be close to data
bulk data operation
legacy database contract
performance-critical set-based processing
DB-owned business workflow
```

Prefer Java service logic when:

```text
logic needs rich domain model
external API coordination
testability matters
multi-database portability matters
workflow orchestration is outside DB
```

Rule:

```text
Do not split one invariant randomly between Java and PL/pgSQL.
Define ownership clearly.
```

---

# 60. JDBC in Jakarta REST Service

Typical flow:

```text
HTTP request
  ↓
Resource/controller
  ↓
Service transaction boundary
  ↓
Repository with Connection
  ↓
PreparedStatement
  ↓
ResultSet mapper
  ↓
Domain object / DTO
```

Avoid doing JDBC directly in resource/controller for non-trivial apps.

Good layering:

```text
Resource -> Service -> Repository -> JDBC
```

---

# 61. Production Anti-Patterns

| Anti-pattern | Problem |
|---|---|
| SQL string concat with user input | SQL injection |
| `SELECT *` | fragile mapping/performance |
| no try-with-resources | connection leak |
| no query timeout | stuck requests |
| no transaction boundary | partial data changes |
| transaction around remote API call | long DB locks |
| new connection per request without pool | slow/resource exhaustion |
| loading huge result into list | memory spike |
| no tenant filter | cross-tenant data leak |
| using `double` for money | precision bug |
| ignoring affected row count | missed update/delete failure |
| swallowing `SQLException` | hidden data failure |
| exposing raw SQL error to user | info leak |
| no batch for bulk insert | poor performance |
| no optimistic locking | lost update |
| using DB local timezone accidentally | temporal bugs |

---

# 62. Security Checklist

```text
[ ] Use PreparedStatement for values.
[ ] Allowlist dynamic identifiers/order columns.
[ ] Never concatenate user input into SQL.
[ ] Enforce tenant_id in tenant-scoped queries.
[ ] Use least-privilege DB user.
[ ] Do not log secrets or sensitive bind values.
[ ] Use TLS to database if crossing network.
[ ] Store DB password in secret manager/env, not source.
[ ] Set query timeout.
[ ] Limit result size.
[ ] Validate pagination limit.
[ ] Translate SQL errors safely.
```

---

# 63. Reliability Checklist

```text
[ ] Connection acquired from DataSource/pool?
[ ] Connection closed/returned to pool?
[ ] Statements and ResultSets closed?
[ ] Transactions commit/rollback correctly?
[ ] AutoCommit restored if manually changed?
[ ] SQLException translated?
[ ] SQLState/vendor code logged?
[ ] Query timeout set?
[ ] Batch operations chunked?
[ ] Large objects streamed?
[ ] Retry policy exists only for safe transient failures?
```

---

# 64. Performance Checklist

```text
[ ] Avoid SELECT *.
[ ] Fetch only needed columns.
[ ] Use indexes matching WHERE/ORDER BY.
[ ] Batch inserts/updates.
[ ] Use fetch size for large reads.
[ ] Avoid N+1 queries.
[ ] Keep transactions short.
[ ] Avoid remote calls inside transaction.
[ ] Use keyset pagination for deep pages.
[ ] Use connection pool.
[ ] Measure query duration.
[ ] Analyze slow queries in DB.
```

---

# 65. Compact Summary

```text
DriverManager
- simple connection factory
- good for tools/tests
- production usually uses DataSource

Connection
- database session
- creates statements
- owns transaction boundary

Statement
- static SQL
- avoid for user input

PreparedStatement
- parameterized SQL
- default for application queries
- prevents value-based SQL injection

CallableStatement
- stored procedure/function call

ResultSet
- query result cursor
- starts before first row
- use rs.next()

SQLException
- database error
- inspect SQLState and vendor code
- translate safely

Types / JDBCType
- SQL type constants/mapping

Blob / Clob / SQLXML / Array
- special SQL type mappings
- free/close resources where applicable

Transaction
- setAutoCommit(false)
- commit on success
- rollback on failure
- use savepoint for partial rollback

Production Rule
- DataSource + PreparedStatement + try-with-resources + explicit transaction + timeout + safe exception translation.
```

Kesimpulan praktis: **`java.sql` adalah fondasi JDBC.** Untuk Java enterprise, pola paling aman adalah **DataSource/connection pool, PreparedStatement, explicit transaction boundary, try-with-resources, SQLState-aware error translation, tenant-safe queries, timeout, batching, dan type mapping yang disiplin**.

---

**Sources:**

- [java.sql (Java SE 21 & JDK 21)](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/api/java.sql/java/sql/package-summary.html)

## Response:
7/12/2026, 10:01:58 PM

Berikut **cheat sheet Java Tricks, Common Patterns, dan Design Patterns** untuk **Java 17+**, dengan fokus enterprise backend.

# Java Tricks, Common Patterns, Design Patterns Cheat Sheet

---

# 1. Mental Model

```text
Java Skill Layers
├── Tricks / Idioms
│   ├── syntax modern
│   ├── null-safety
│   ├── collection usage
│   ├── stream usage
│   ├── exception discipline
│   └── immutability
│
├── Common Implementation Patterns
│   ├── DTO
│   ├── mapper
│   ├── result type
│   ├── repository
│   ├── service
│   ├── validation
│   ├── retry
│   ├── idempotency
│   └── transaction boundary
│
└── Design Patterns
    ├── creational
    ├── structural
    ├── behavioral
    ├── architectural
    └── enterprise integration
```

Rule utama:

```text
Trick makes code shorter.
Pattern makes code repeatably understandable.
Design pattern makes responsibility and change boundary clear.
```

---

# 2. Java Tricks / Idioms

## 2.1 Prefer Guard Clause

Bad:

```java
public void approve(Order order) {
    if (order != null) {
        if (order.status() == OrderStatus.SUBMITTED) {
            order.approve();
        }
    }
}
```

Better:

```java
public void approve(Order order) {
    Objects.requireNonNull(order, "order");

    if (order.status() != OrderStatus.SUBMITTED) {
        throw new IllegalStateException("Order is not submitted");
    }

    order.approve();
}
```

Why:

```text
less nesting
clear failure path
happy path obvious
```

---

## 2.2 `Objects.requireNonNull`

```java
public CustomerService(CustomerRepository repository) {
    this.repository = Objects.requireNonNull(repository, "repository");
}
```

Use for constructor dependency.

```text
Fail fast when required dependency is missing.
```

---

## 2.3 `record` for Immutable Data Carrier

```java
public record CustomerDto(
    String id,
    String name,
    String email
) {}
```

With validation:

```java
public record Money(BigDecimal amount, Currency currency) {
    public Money {
        Objects.requireNonNull(amount, "amount");
        Objects.requireNonNull(currency, "currency");

        if (amount.signum() < 0) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("amount must not be negative");
        }
    }
}
```

Use record for:

```text
DTO
value object
query result
command
event
response
configuration snapshot
```

Avoid record for:

```text
mutable entity
lazy-loaded ORM entity
object with complex lifecycle
```

---

## 2.4 `var` Rule

Good:

```java
var customers = customerRepository.findActiveCustomers();
var formatter = DateTimeFormatter.ISO_OFFSET_DATE_TIME;
```

Bad:

```java
var x = get();
var data = process(input);
```

Rule:

```text
Use var when type is obvious from right side.
Avoid var when it hides important meaning.
```

---

## 2.5 Switch Expression

```java
String label = switch (status) {
    case DRAFT -> "Draft";
    case SUBMITTED -> "Submitted";
    case APPROVED -> "Approved";
    case REJECTED -> "Rejected";
};
```

With logic:

```java
int priority = switch (severity) {
    case LOW -> 1;
    case MEDIUM -> 5;
    case HIGH -> 10;
    case CRITICAL -> {
        log.warn("Critical severity detected");
        yield 100;
    }
};
```

---

## 2.6 Pattern Matching `instanceof`

Old:

```java
if (value instanceof String) {
    String text = (String) value;
    System.out.println(text.length());
}
```

Modern:

```java
if (value instanceof String text) {
    System.out.println(text.length());
}
```

---

## 2.7 Sealed Type for Controlled Variants

```java
public sealed interface PaymentResult
        permits PaymentResult.Success, PaymentResult.Failed {

    record Success(String transactionId) implements PaymentResult {}

    record Failed(String reasonCode, String message) implements PaymentResult {}
}
```

Use when:

```text
all possible variants are known
you want exhaustive switch
domain result needs clear shape
```

---

## 2.8 Prefer `List.copyOf`

Bad:

```java
public Order(List<OrderLine> lines) {
    this.lines = lines;
}
```

Better:

```java
public Order(List<OrderLine> lines) {
    this.lines = List.copyOf(lines);
}
```

Getter:

```java
public List<OrderLine> lines() {
    return lines;
}
```

Rule:

```text
Protect internal collection from outside mutation.
```

---

## 2.9 Use `EnumSet` and `EnumMap`

Bad:

```java
Set<Permission> permissions = new HashSet<>();
```

Better:

```java
EnumSet<Permission> permissions = EnumSet.of(
    Permission.ORDER_READ,
    Permission.ORDER_APPROVE
);
```

EnumMap:

```java
EnumMap<OrderStatus, String> labels = new EnumMap<>(OrderStatus.class);

labels.put(OrderStatus.SUBMITTED, "Submitted");
labels.put(OrderStatus.APPROVED, "Approved");
```

Use for enum keys/values.

---

## 2.10 `Map.computeIfAbsent`

```java
Map<String, List<Order>> ordersByCustomer = new HashMap<>();

ordersByCustomer
    .computeIfAbsent(customerId, ignored -> new ArrayList<>())
    .add(order);
```

Caution:

```text
Keep mapping function simple.
Do not perform slow remote calls inside computeIfAbsent.
Be careful with ConcurrentHashMap recursive updates.
```

---

## 2.11 `Map.merge` for Counting

```java
Map<String, Integer> countByStatus = new HashMap<>();

for (Order order : orders) {
    countByStatus.merge(order.status(), 1, Integer::sum);
}
```

For concurrent high-write counters:

```java
ConcurrentHashMap<String, LongAdder> counters = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();

counters.computeIfAbsent("order.approved", ignored -> new LongAdder())
    .increment();
```

---

## 2.12 Optional Best Use

Good return type:

```java
public Optional<Customer> findById(CustomerId id) {
    // ...
}
```

Good usage:

```java
Customer customer = customerRepository.findById(id)
    .orElseThrow(() -> new NotFoundException("Customer not found"));
```

Avoid:

```java
private Optional<String> name;      // usually avoid Optional fields
public void setName(Optional<String> name) // avoid Optional parameter
```

Rule:

```text
Optional is good for return value that may be absent.
Avoid Optional as field, parameter, or collection element.
```

---

# 3. Common Implementation Patterns

---

# 3.1 DTO Pattern

DTO separates API shape from domain model.

```java
public record CreateOrderRequest(
    String customerId,
    List<CreateOrderLineRequest> lines
) {}

public record CreateOrderLineRequest(
    String productId,
    int quantity
) {}
```

Response:

```java
public record OrderResponse(
    String id,
    String status,
    BigDecimal totalAmount
) {}
```

Rule:

```text
Do not expose domain entity directly as REST response.
DTO is API contract.
Domain object is business model.
```

---

# 3.2 Mapper Pattern

```java
public final class OrderMapper {
    public OrderResponse toResponse(Order order) {
        return new OrderResponse(
            order.id().value(),
            order.status().name(),
            order.totalAmount()
        );
    }
}
```

Rule:

```text
Keep mapping boring.
Avoid business logic inside mapper.
```

---

# 3.3 Command Pattern as Request to Use Case

```java
public record ApproveOrderCommand(
    OrderId orderId,
    UserId approverId,
    String reason
) {}
```

Service:

```java
public final class ApproveOrderUseCase {
    public void handle(ApproveOrderCommand command) {
        Order order = orderRepository.findById(command.orderId())
            .orElseThrow(OrderNotFoundException::new);

        order.approve(command.approverId(), command.reason());

        orderRepository.save(order);
    }
}
```

Use when:

```text
operation has explicit input
input needs validation
operation may be queued/audited/retried
```

---

# 3.4 Result Type Pattern

Instead of throwing exception for expected business outcome:

```java
public sealed interface Result<T> {
    record Success<T>(T value) implements Result<T> {}
    record Failure<T>(String code, String message) implements Result<T> {}
}
```

Use:

```java
public Result<Order> submit(Order order) {
    if (order.lines().isEmpty()) {
        return new Result.Failure<>("ORDER_EMPTY", "Order has no lines");
    }

    order.submit();

    return new Result.Success<>(order);
}
```

Rule:

```text
Use exception for exceptional failure.
Use Result for expected domain failure.
```

---

# 3.5 Validation Pattern

```java
public final class ValidationErrors {
    private final List<String> errors = new ArrayList<>();

    public void add(String error) {
        errors.add(error);
    }

    public boolean hasErrors() {
        return !errors.isEmpty();
    }

    public List<String> all() {
        return List.copyOf(errors);
    }
}
```

Use:

```java
ValidationErrors errors = new ValidationErrors();

if (request.customerId() == null) {
    errors.add("customerId is required");
}

if (request.lines().isEmpty()) {
    errors.add("at least one line is required");
}

if (errors.hasErrors()) {
    throw new ValidationException(errors.all());
}
```

---

# 3.6 Repository Pattern

Repository hides persistence detail.

```java
public interface OrderRepository {
    Optional<Order> findById(OrderId id);

    void save(Order order);
}
```

JDBC implementation:

```java
public final class JdbcOrderRepository implements OrderRepository {
    private final DataSource dataSource;

    public JdbcOrderRepository(DataSource dataSource) {
        this.dataSource = dataSource;
    }

    @Override
    public Optional<Order> findById(OrderId id) {
        // JDBC query here
    }

    @Override
    public void save(Order order) {
        // JDBC insert/update here
    }
}
```

Rule:

```text
Domain depends on repository interface.
Infrastructure implements repository.
```

---

# 3.7 Service / Use Case Pattern

```java
public final class OrderApplicationService {
    private final OrderRepository orderRepository;
    private final AuthorizationService authorizationService;
    private final Clock clock;

    public void approveOrder(SecurityContext context, OrderId orderId) {
        Order order = orderRepository.findById(orderId)
            .orElseThrow(OrderNotFoundException::new);

        authorizationService.requirePermission(context, "order:approve");

        order.approve(context.userId(), clock.instant());

        orderRepository.save(order);
    }
}
```

Application service coordinates:

```text
transaction
authorization
repository
domain method
external gateway
audit
event publishing
```

Domain object owns:

```text
business invariant
state transition
calculation
```

---

# 3.8 Transaction Boundary Pattern

```java
public void approveOrder(OrderId orderId, UserId approverId) {
    transactionTemplate.execute(() -> {
        Order order = orderRepository.findByIdForUpdate(orderId)
            .orElseThrow(OrderNotFoundException::new);

        order.approve(approverId);

        orderRepository.save(order);
        auditRepository.insert(AuditEvent.orderApproved(order));

        return null;
    });
}
```

Rule:

```text
One use case = one transaction boundary.
Do not hold DB transaction while calling slow remote API.
```

---

# 3.9 Domain Event Pattern

```java
public sealed interface DomainEvent {
    Instant occurredAt();
}
```

```java
public record OrderApprovedEvent(
    OrderId orderId,
    UserId approvedBy,
    Instant occurredAt
) implements DomainEvent {}
```

Aggregate:

```java
public final class Order {
    private final List<DomainEvent> events = new ArrayList<>();

    public void approve(UserId userId, Instant now) {
        if (status != OrderStatus.SUBMITTED) {
            throw new IllegalStateException("Order is not submitted");
        }

        status = OrderStatus.APPROVED;
        events.add(new OrderApprovedEvent(id, userId, now));
    }

    public List<DomainEvent> pullEvents() {
        List<DomainEvent> copy = List.copyOf(events);
        events.clear();
        return copy;
    }
}
```

Use when:

```text
something happened in domain
other parts need to react
audit/integration/outbox needed
```

---

# 3.10 Transactional Outbox Pattern

Problem:

```text
DB commit succeeds.
Message publish fails.
System becomes inconsistent.
```

Pattern:

```text
Same transaction:
1. Save domain data.
2. Save outbox event.

Separate worker:
3. Read unpublished outbox events.
4. Publish message.
5. Mark as published.
```

Outbox table:

```sql
CREATE TABLE outbox_event (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    aggregate_type TEXT NOT NULL,
    aggregate_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    event_type TEXT NOT NULL,
    payload JSONB NOT NULL,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,
    published_at TIMESTAMPTZ
);
```

Rule:

```text
Use outbox when DB change and message publish must be reliable.
```

---

# 3.11 Idempotency Pattern

For POST/payment/order submission:

```java
public record IdempotencyKey(String value) {
    public IdempotencyKey {
        if (value == null || value.isBlank()) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("idempotency key is required");
        }
    }
}
```

Flow:

```text
1. Client sends Idempotency-Key.
2. Server checks if key already processed.
3. If yes, return previous result.
4. If no, process and store result under key.
```

Use for:

```text
payment
order creation
external callback
retryable POST
workflow command
```

---

# 3.12 Retry Pattern

```java
public <T> T retry(int maxAttempts, Supplier<T> operation) {
    RuntimeException last = null;

    for (int attempt = 1; attempt <= maxAttempts; attempt++) {
        try {
            return operation.get();
        } catch (RuntimeException e) {
            last = e;
            sleep(backoff(attempt));
        }
    }

    throw last;
}
```

Rule:

```text
Retry only transient failures.
Retry only safe/idempotent operations.
Use timeout + backoff + max attempt.
```

---

# 3.13 Bulkhead Pattern

```java
public final class Bulkhead {
    private final Semaphore semaphore;

    public Bulkhead(int maxConcurrent) {
        this.semaphore = new Semaphore(maxConcurrent);
    }

    public <T> T execute(Supplier<T> operation) {
        boolean acquired = false;

        try {
            acquired = semaphore.tryAcquire(500, TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS);

            if (!acquired) {
                throw new ServiceUnavailableException("Bulkhead full");
            }

            return operation.get();

        } catch (InterruptedException e) {
            Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
            throw new RuntimeException(e);
        } finally {
            if (acquired) {
                semaphore.release();
            }
        }
    }
}
```

Use for:

```text
downstream API
database-heavy operation
legacy service
limited resource
```

---

# 4. Creational Design Patterns

---

# 4.1 Factory Method

```java
public interface PaymentProcessor {
    PaymentResult process(Payment payment);
}
```

```java
public final class PaymentProcessorFactory {
    public PaymentProcessor create(PaymentMethod method) {
        return switch (method) {
            case CREDIT_CARD -> new CreditCardPaymentProcessor();
            case BANK_TRANSFER -> new BankTransferPaymentProcessor();
            case WALLET -> new WalletPaymentProcessor();
        };
    }
}
```

Use when:

```text
creation depends on type/config
caller should not know concrete class
```

---

# 4.2 Static Factory Method

```java
public final class Money {
    private final BigDecimal amount;
    private final Currency currency;

    private Money(BigDecimal amount, Currency currency) {
        this.amount = amount;
        this.currency = currency;
    }

    public static Money of(BigDecimal amount, Currency currency) {
        return new Money(amount, currency);
    }

    public static Money zero(Currency currency) {
        return new Money(BigDecimal.ZERO, currency);
    }
}
```

Benefits:

```text
clear name
can validate
can cache
can return subtype
```

---

# 4.3 Builder Pattern

Good for object with many optional fields.

```java
public final class HttpClientConfig {
    private final URI baseUri;
    private final Duration connectTimeout;
    private final Duration requestTimeout;
    private final boolean retryEnabled;

    private HttpClientConfig(Builder builder) {
        this.baseUri = builder.baseUri;
        this.connectTimeout = builder.connectTimeout;
        this.requestTimeout = builder.requestTimeout;
        this.retryEnabled = builder.retryEnabled;
    }

    public static Builder builder(URI baseUri) {
        return new Builder(baseUri);
    }

    public static final class Builder {
        private final URI baseUri;
        private Duration connectTimeout = Duration.ofSeconds(5);
        private Duration requestTimeout = Duration.ofSeconds(10);
        private boolean retryEnabled = false;

        private Builder(URI baseUri) {
            this.baseUri = Objects.requireNonNull(baseUri);
        }

        public Builder connectTimeout(Duration value) {
            this.connectTimeout = value;
            return this;
        }

        public Builder requestTimeout(Duration value) {
            this.requestTimeout = value;
            return this;
        }

        public Builder retryEnabled(boolean value) {
            this.retryEnabled = value;
            return this;
        }

        public HttpClientConfig build() {
            return new HttpClientConfig(this);
        }
    }
}
```

Use:

```java
HttpClientConfig config = HttpClientConfig.builder(URI.create("https://api.example.com"))
    .connectTimeout(Duration.ofSeconds(3))
    .retryEnabled(true)
    .build();
```

---

# 4.4 Singleton

Prefer dependency injection. If truly needed:

```java
public enum AppClock {
    INSTANCE;

    public Instant now() {
        return Instant.now();
    }
}
```

But in enterprise code, better:

```java
Clock clock = Clock.systemUTC();
```

Rule:

```text
Singleton is often hidden global state.
Prefer constructor injection.
```

---

# 5. Structural Design Patterns

---

# 5.1 Adapter Pattern

Convert one interface into another.

External API:

```java
public interface PartnerOrderClient {
    PartnerOrderResponse fetchOrder(String partnerOrderId);
}
```

Domain port:

```java
public interface OrderLookup {
    Optional<OrderSnapshot> findExternalOrder(String externalId);
}
```

Adapter:

```java
public final class PartnerOrderLookupAdapter implements OrderLookup {
    private final PartnerOrderClient client;

    public PartnerOrderLookupAdapter(PartnerOrderClient client) {
        this.client = client;
    }

    @Override
    public Optional<OrderSnapshot> findExternalOrder(String externalId) {
        PartnerOrderResponse response = client.fetchOrder(externalId);

        return Optional.of(new OrderSnapshot(
            response.id(),
            response.status(),
            response.amount()
        ));
    }
}
```

Use when:

```text
external model differs from internal model
you want to isolate vendor/partner API
```

---

# 5.2 Facade Pattern

Simplify complex subsystem.

```java
public final class OrderApprovalFacade {
    private final OrderService orderService;
    private final NotificationService notificationService;
    private final AuditService auditService;

    public void approve(OrderId orderId, UserId approverId) {
        Order order = orderService.approve(orderId, approverId);
        auditService.recordOrderApproved(order, approverId);
        notificationService.notifyOrderApproved(order);
    }
}
```

Use when:

```text
caller should not coordinate many services directly
you need simpler API over complex workflow
```

---

# 5.3 Decorator Pattern

Add behavior without changing core implementation.

```java
public interface OrderClient {
    OrderResponse getOrder(OrderId id);
}
```

Core:

```java
public final class HttpOrderClient implements OrderClient {
    public OrderResponse getOrder(OrderId id) {
        // HTTP call
    }
}
```

Logging decorator:

```java
public final class LoggingOrderClient implements OrderClient {
    private final OrderClient delegate;

    public LoggingOrderClient(OrderClient delegate) {
        this.delegate = delegate;
    }

    @Override
    public OrderResponse getOrder(OrderId id) {
        long start = System.nanoTime();

        try {
            return delegate.getOrder(id);
        } finally {
            long durationMs = Duration.ofNanos(System.nanoTime() - start).toMillis();
            log.info("getOrder completed: id={}, durationMs={}", id, durationMs);
        }
    }
}
```

Other decorators:

```text
retry
timeout
metrics
logging
caching
authorization
```

---

# 5.4 Proxy Pattern

Control access to real object.

```java
public final class AuthorizedOrderService implements OrderService {
    private final OrderService delegate;
    private final AuthorizationService authorizationService;

    @Override
    public Order approve(SecurityContext context, OrderId id) {
        authorizationService.requirePermission(context, "order:approve");
        return delegate.approve(context, id);
    }
}
```

Use when:

```text
access control
lazy loading
remote call
transaction boundary
caching
```

---

# 5.5 Composite Pattern

Tree-like structure.

```java
public sealed interface MenuItem permits MenuAction, MenuGroup {
    String label();
}
```

```java
public record MenuAction(
    String label,
    String action
) implements MenuItem {}
```

```java
public record MenuGroup(
    String label,
    List<MenuItem> children
) implements MenuItem {
    public MenuGroup {
        children = List.copyOf(children);
    }
}
```

Use for:

```text
tree
menu
organization hierarchy
workflow node
rule group
```

---

# 6. Behavioral Design Patterns

---

# 6.1 Strategy Pattern

Choose algorithm at runtime.

```java
public interface PricingStrategy {
    Money calculate(Order order);
}
```

```java
public final class RegularPricingStrategy implements PricingStrategy {
    public Money calculate(Order order) {
        return order.subtotal();
    }
}
```

```java
public final class DiscountPricingStrategy implements PricingStrategy {
    public Money calculate(Order order) {
        return order.subtotal().multiply(new BigDecimal("0.90"));
    }
}
```

Selector:

```java
public final class PricingService {
    private final Map<CustomerType, PricingStrategy> strategies;

    public Money price(Order order, CustomerType customerType) {
        PricingStrategy strategy = strategies.get(customerType);

        if (strategy == null) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("Unsupported customer type");
        }

        return strategy.calculate(order);
    }
}
```

Use when:

```text
many if-else based on type
algorithm varies by config/type
```

---

# 6.2 Template Method Pattern

Common flow, customizable steps.

```java
public abstract class ImportJob<T> {
    public final ImportResult run(Path file) {
        validateFile(file);

        List<T> records = parse(file);

        validateRecords(records);

        return persist(records);
    }

    protected abstract List<T> parse(Path file);

    protected abstract void validateRecords(List<T> records);

    protected abstract ImportResult persist(List<T> records);

    private void validateFile(Path file) {
        if (!Files.isRegularFile(file)) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("Invalid file");
        }
    }
}
```

Use when:

```text
same workflow
different steps
must enforce order
```

---

# 6.3 Command Pattern

Encapsulate action as object.

```java
public interface Command {
    void execute();
}
```

```java
public record SendEmailCommand(
    String to,
    String subject,
    String body
) implements Command {
    @Override
    public void execute() {
        // send email
    }
}
```

Use for:

```text
job queue
workflow action
audit action
undo/redo
deferred execution
```

---

# 6.4 Chain of Responsibility

```java
public interface OrderValidator {
    void validate(Order order, ValidationErrors errors);
}
```

```java
public final class OrderHasLinesValidator implements OrderValidator {
    public void validate(Order order, ValidationErrors errors) {
        if (order.lines().isEmpty()) {
            errors.add("Order must have lines");
        }
    }
}
```

```java
public final class OrderValidationChain {
    private final List<OrderValidator> validators;

    public void validate(Order order) {
        ValidationErrors errors = new ValidationErrors();

        for (OrderValidator validator : validators) {
            validator.validate(order, errors);
        }

        if (errors.hasErrors()) {
            throw new ValidationException(errors.all());
        }
    }
}
```

Use for:

```text
validation
filter pipeline
security checks
message processing
```

---

# 6.5 State Pattern

Bad:

```java
if (status == DRAFT) { ... }
```
